{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Deeply study the TOOL + SKILL + REPORTING capability surface of Strix (an open-source agentic AI pentest tool) at /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/strix. I'm assessing folding its core ideas into a fused security+personal-AI system.\n\nRead and explain in DEPTH:\n1. **The tool framework** — strix/tools/__init__.py and each tool package: agents_graph/, finish/, notes/, proxy/, reporting/, thinking/, todo/, load_skill/, web_search/. For EACH tool: what does it do, what's its interface to the agent (how is it registered/exposed to the LLM as a callable tool — decorators? schemas?), and what capability does it grant? Which tools execute against a TARGET (offensive) vs are agent-internal (notes/todo/thinking/finish)?\n2. **How is a \"tool\" defined + registered** — is there a common tool base/decorator? How do tool schemas get generated for the LLM (openai-agents SDK function tools)? How does a tool run INSIDE the Docker sandbox vs on the host? (cross-reference strix/runtime).\n3. **The proxy** — strix/tools/proxy/{tools.py,caido_api.py} + strix/runtime/caido_bootstrap.py. How does Strix intercept/replay HTTP (Caido, a Burp alternative)? What can the agent do with it (intercept, modify, replay, scope)?\n4. **The skills system** — strix/tools/load_skill/tool.py + strix/skills/. What is a \"skill\"? How are skills defined, discovered, and loaded into the agent's context? Are there built-in skills (pentest playbooks)? This is important — it's how domain expertise is injected.\n5. **Reporting + findings** — strix/report/{writer.py,sarif.py,dedupe.py,state.py,usage.py} + strix/tools/reporting/tool.py. How are vulnerabilities recorded, deduplicated, scored (CVSS via the `cvss` dep), and exported (SARIF)? What's the finding data model?\n6. **The prompt/agent instructions** — strix/agents/prompt.py + strix/tools/*/prompt files (find any .jinja or prompt text). What is the agent told to do? What's its methodology?\n\nBe concrete with file:line references and quote key code (tool signatures, the finding model, a skill example, the system prompt structure). Do NOT modify anything."},"uuid":"009904d1-602c-4ac7-91c4-02d753ab6d2e","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:21:07.812Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"bf06c7fb-5b87-47dd-8792-be85df761995","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- crucible: How to operate CRUCIBLE — the authorized, prove-don't-guess offensive-security engine in framework/v2. Read this BEFORE running an engagement/scan, re-verifying a finding, inspecting the event spine, reasoning about the veracity/anti-hallucination layer, the learning/metacognition core, or the safety stack. Use it when a task names CRUCIBLE, OBSIDIAN, `python3 -m framework.v2 <subcommand>`, findings/oracles/evidence certificates, the blackboard event spine, critics, reflection, or the reward/calibration loop.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. BEFORE invoking, check if the question is specific enough to research directly — if underspecified (e.g., \"what car to buy\" without budget/use-case/region), ask 2-3 clarifying questions to narrow scope. Then pass the refined question as args, weaving the answers in.\n- adversarial-sweep: Independent multi-lens adversarial re-attack of a defensive-oracle domain — the FORGE stage-9 companion to RED-PEN. Attack from disjoint lenses, refute each objection with a real repro, report only what survives. - Before merging any AEGIS/FORGE domain stream, alongside (not instead of) the red-pen agent. Pass the domain spec via args. On the email-auth stream it caught 3 CRITICAL/LOW defects PAST a RED-PEN PASS — near-zero-FP cannot be self-certified by one reviewer.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. report_state.update_scan_final_fields(\n60\t            executive_summary=executive_summary.strip(),\n61\t            methodology=methodology.strip(),\n62\t            technical_analysis=technical_analysis.strip(),\n63\t            recommendations=recommendations.strip(),\n64\t        )\n65\t        vuln_count = len(report_state.vulnerability_reports)\n66\t    except (ImportError, AttributeError) as e:\n67\t        logger.exception(\"finish_scan persistence failed\")\n68\t        return {\"success\": False, \"error\": f\"Failed to complete scan: {e!s}\"}\n69\t    else:\n70\t        logger.info(\n71\t            \"finish_scan: completed scan with %d vulnerability report(s)\",\n72\t            vuln_count,\n73\t        )\n74\t        return {\n75\t            \"success\": True,\n76\t            \"scan_completed\": True,\n77\t            \"message\": \"Scan completed successfully\",\n78\t            \"vulnerabilities_found\": vuln_count,\n79\t        }\n80\t\n81\t\n82\t@function_tool(timeout=60)\n83\tasync def finish_scan(\n84\t    ctx: RunContextWrapper,\n85\t    executive_summary: str,\n86\t    methodology: str,\n87\t    technical_analysis: str,\n88\t    recommendations: str,\n89\t) -> str:\n90\t    \"\"\"Finalize the scan — persist the customer-facing report.\n91\t\n92\t    **Root-agent only.** Subagents must call ``agent_finish`` from the\n93\t    multi-agent graph tools instead. Calling this finalizes everything:\n94\t\n95\t    1. Verifies you are the root agent.\n96\t    2. Writes the four narrative sections to the scan record.\n97\t    3. Marks the scan completed and stops execution.\n98\t\n99\t    **This is a terminal action, not a status probe.** Whatever you pass\n100\t    is persisted VERBATIM as the final, customer-facing report and then\n101\t    execution stops. There is no draft mode and no second chance: never\n102\t    submit placeholder, provisional, or \"checking if done\" text in any\n103\t    field, and never call ``finish_scan`` to poll whether subagents are\n104\t    done (use ``view_agent_graph`` / ``wait_for_message`` for that).\n105\t    Call it exactly ONCE, only when every field holds genuine, finished\n106\t    assessment prose.\n107\t\n108\t    **Pre-flight checklist (mandatory — do not skip):**\n109\t\n110\t    1. **Call ``view_agent_graph`` first.** Inspect every entry in the\n111\t       summary. If ANY agent is in ``running`` / ``waiting`` state,\n112\t       you MUST NOT call ``finish_scan`` yet —\n113\t       wrap them up first via ``send_message_to_agent`` (ask them to\n114\t       finish), ``wait_for_message`` (block until their report\n115\t       arrives), or ``stop_agent`` (graceful cancel). Only ``completed``\n116\t       / ``crashed`` / ``stopped`` agents are safe to leave behind.\n117\t       Calling ``finish_scan`` while children are alive orphans their\n118\t       work and produces an incomplete report.\n119\t    2. All vulnerabilities you found are filed via\n120\t       ``create_vulnerability_report`` — or, for known-CVE dependency\n121\t       findings, ``create_dependency_report`` (un-reported findings are\n122\t       not tracked and not credited). A dependency CVE already filed via\n123\t       ``create_dependency_report`` counts as reported; it does NOT need\n124\t       re-filing here and does NOT block finishing.\n125\t    3. Don't double-report — one report per distinct vulnerability.\n126\t    4. **Attack-chaining gate.** Do NOT finish until you have genuinely\n127\t       considered chaining the confirmed findings into higher-impact,\n128\t       end-to-end attack paths and tested every plausibly-related\n129\t       combination. You may rule out combinations you can confidently\n130\t       call unrelated — note why instead of padding chains. Any\n131\t       validated chain must already be filed via\n132\t       ``create_vulnerability_report`` — a demonstrated end-to-end chain\n133\t       is a PoC-backed vulnerability, so it uses that tool even when one\n134\t       link is a dependency CVE (the standalone CVE stays in its own\n135\t       ``create_dependency_report``) — and surfaced prominently in\n136\t       ``executive_summary`` / ``technical_analysis``. Finding no real\n137\t       chain after a serious attempt is acceptable; skipping the\n138\t       chaining reasoning, or ignoring a plausibly-related combination,\n139\t       is not.\n140\t\n141\t    **Calling this multiple times overwrites the previous report.**\n142\t    Make the single call comprehensive.\n143\t\n144\t    **Report output rules** (this content may be rendered into generated\n145\t    reports):\n146\t\n147\t    - Never mention internal infrastructure: no local/absolute paths\n148\t      (``/workspace/...``), no agent names, no sandbox/orchestrator/\n149\t      tooling references, no system prompts, no model-internal errors.\n150\t      Never leak internal identifiers (proxy request IDs, internal\n151\t      vulnerability report IDs, or any system-generated IDs) into any\n152\t      field.\n153\t    - Tone: formal, third-person, objective, concise. This is a\n154\t      consultant deliverable, not an engineering log.\n155\t    - Each section has a specific role:\n156\t\n157\t        - ``executive_summary`` — for non-technical leadership. Risk\n158\t          posture, business impact (data exposure / compliance /\n159\t          reputation), notable criticals, overarching remediation\n160\t          theme.\n161\t        - ``methodology`` — frameworks followed (OWASP WSTG, PTES,\n162\t          OSSTMM, NIST), engagement type (black/gray/white box), scope\n163\t          and constraints, categories of testing performed. **No**\n164\t          internal execution detail.\n165\t        - ``technical_analysis`` — consolidated findings overview with\n166\t          severity model and systemic root causes. Reference individual\n167\t          vuln reports for repro steps; don't duplicate raw evidence.\n168\t        - ``recommendations`` — prioritized actions grouped by urgency\n169\t          (Immediate / Short-term / Medium-term), each with concrete\n170\t          remediation steps. End with retest/validation guidance.\n171\t\n172\t    - **Formatting — use markdown in every field.** These fields may be\n173\t      rendered into generated reports, so structure them clearly: lead\n174\t      each section with a short ``# Heading``, use ``**bold**`` for labels/emphasis,\n175\t      ``inline code`` for identifiers/paths/parameters, bullet or\n176\t      numbered lists for enumerations, and fenced code blocks\n177\t      (```` ```language ````) for any code/payload excerpts. Never emit\n178\t      one flat wall of prose or leave code unformatted.\n179\t    - If **zero** vulnerabilities were found, say so plainly and\n180\t      characterize the posture positively; ``technical_analysis`` should\n181\t      summarize the areas tested and confirm no issues, and\n182\t      ``recommendations`` should focus on general hardening.\n183\t\n184\t    Example (abbreviated — mirror this structure, not the wording)::\n185\t\n186\t        executive_summary:\n187\t            # Executive Summary\n188\t\n189\t            An external assessment of the **Acme Customer Portal**\n190\t            identified multiple weaknesses that could lead to\n191\t            unauthorized access to customer data.\n192\t\n193\t            **Overall risk posture:** Elevated.\n194\t\n195\t            **Key findings**\n196\t            - Confirmed SSRF in a URL-preview feature reaching internal\n197\t              network ranges.\n198\t            - Broken tenant isolation enabling cross-tenant data access.\n199\t\n200\t            **Business impact**\n201\t            - Potential exposure of customer records across tenants.\n202\t\n203\t        methodology:\n204\t            # Methodology\n205\t\n206\t            Conducted per the **OWASP WSTG**.\n207\t\n208\t            **Engagement type:** Gray-box external test.\n209\t            **Scope:** `https://app.acme.example`, `.../api/v1/`.\n210\t\n211\t            **Activities:** recon, authn/session review, authorization\n212\t            and tenant-isolation testing, input/SSRF testing.\n213\t\n214\t        technical_analysis:\n215\t            # Technical Analysis\n216\t\n217\t            **Severity model** reflects exploitability x impact.\n218\t\n219\t            1. **SSRF in URL preview** (Critical) — insufficient\n220\t               destination validation; reaches link-local addresses.\n221\t            2. **Broken tenant isolation** (High) — object identifiers\n222\t               accepted without ownership checks.\n223\t\n224\t            **Systemic themes:** authorization enforced inconsistently;\n225\t            no deny-by-default egress policy.\n226\t\n227\t        recommendations:\n228\t            # Recommendations\n229\t\n230\t            **Immediate**\n231\t            1. Remediate SSRF: enforce a destination allowlist,\n232\t               deny-by-default, re-validate on every redirect hop.\n233\t\n234\t            **Short-term**\n235\t            2. Centralize authorization with deny-by-default middleware.\n236\t\n237\t            **Retest & validation:** re-test immediate items to confirm\n238\t            SSRF and tenant-isolation controls hold.\n239\t\n240\t    Args:\n241\t        executive_summary: Business-level summary for leadership.\n242\t        methodology: Frameworks, scope, and approach.\n243\t        technical_analysis: {"parentUuid":"429ae585-7f1c-41a6-8285-99bda268ae43","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016Kx2y4HdxvaxnzihqnFs78","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"``web_search`` — Perplexity-backed security-focused web search.\"\"\"\n2\t\n3\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n4\t\n5\timport asyncio\n6\timport json\n7\timport logging\n8\tfrom typing import Any\n9\t\n10\timport requests\n11\tfrom agents import RunContextWrapper, function_tool\n12\t\n13\tfrom strix.config import load_settings\n14\t\n15\t\n16\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n17\t\n18\t\n19\t_SYSTEM_PROMPT = \"\"\"You are assisting a cybersecurity agent specialized in vulnerability scanning\n20\tand security assessment running on Kali Linux. When responding to search queries:\n21\t\n22\t1. Prioritize cybersecurity-relevant information including:\n23\t   - Vulnerability details (CVEs, CVSS scores, impact)\n24\t   - Security tools, techniques, and methodologies\n25\t   - Exploit information and proof-of-concepts\n26\t   - Security best practices and mitigations\n27\t   - Penetration testing approaches\n28\t   - Web application security findings\n29\t\n30\t2. Provide technical depth appropriate for security professionals\n31\t3. Include specific versions, configurations, and technical details when available\n32\t4. Focus on actionable intelligence for security assessment\n33\t5. Cite reliable security sources (NIST, OWASP, CVE databases, security vendors)\n34\t6. When providing commands or installation instructions, prioritize Kali Linux compatibility\n35\t   and use apt package manager or tools pre-installed in Kali\n36\t7. Be detailed and specific - avoid general answers. Always include concrete code examples,\n37\t   command-line instructions, configuration snippets, or practical implementation steps\n38\t   when applicable\n39\t\n40\tStructure your response to be comprehensive yet concise, emphasizing the most critical\n41\tsecurity implications and details.\"\"\"\n42\t\n43\t\n44\tdef _do_search(query: str) -> dict[str, Any]:  # noqa: PLR0911 - each error class needs its own sanitized return\n45\t    if not query or not query.strip():\n46\t        return {\"success\": False, \"error\": \"Query cannot be empty\"}\n47\t\n48\t    api_key = load_settings().integrations.perplexity_api_key\n49\t    if not api_key:\n50\t        logger.warning(\"web_search invoked without PERPLEXITY_API_KEY configured\")\n51\t        return {\n52\t            \"success\": False,\n53\t            \"error\": (\n54\t                \"Web search is not configured for this scan \"\n55\t                \"(operator needs to set PERPLEXITY_API_KEY). Proceed without it\"\n56\t            ),\n57\t        }\n58\t    logger.info(\"web_search query (len=%d): %s\", len(query), query[:120])\n59\t\n60\t    url = \"https://api.perplexity.ai/chat/completions\"\n61\t    headers = {\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {api_key}\", \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\"}\n62\t    payload = {\n63\t        \"model\": \"sonar-reasoning-pro\",\n64\t        \"messages\": [\n65\t            {\"role\": \"system\", \"content\": _SYSTEM_PROMPT},\n66\t            {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": query},\n67\t        ],\n68\t    }\n69\t\n70\t    try:\n71\t        response = requests.post(url, headers=headers, json=payload, timeout=300)\n72\t        response.raise_for_status()\n73\t        content = response.json()[\"choices\"][0][\"message\"][\"content\"]\n74\t    except requests.exceptions.Timeout:\n75\t        logger.warning(\"web_search timed out\")\n76\t        return {\n77\t            \"success\": False,\n78\t            \"error\": \"Web search timed out. Try again or shorten the query\",\n79\t        }\n80\t    except requests.exceptions.HTTPError as exc:\n81\t        status = exc.response.status_code if exc.response is not None else None\n82\t        logger.exception(\"web_search HTTP error status=%s\", status)\n83\t        if status is not None and 400 <= status < 500:\n84\t            return {\n85\t                \"success\": False,\n86\t                \"error\": (\n87\t                    \"Web search rejected the query. Refine it \"\n88\t                    \"(more specific, shorter, no unusual characters) and retry\"\n89\t                ),\n90\t            }\n91\t        return {\n92\t            \"success\": False,\n93\t            \"error\": \"Web search service is unavailable. Try again later\",\n94\t        }\n95\t    except requests.exceptions.RequestException:\n96\t        logger.exception(\"web_search network error\")\n97\t        return {\n98\t            \"success\": False,\n99\t            \"error\": \"Web search network error. Try again later\",\n100\t        }\n101\t    except (KeyError, IndexError, ValueError):\n102\t        logger.exception(\"web_search response shape unexpected\")\n103\t        return {\n104\t            \"success\": False,\n105\t            \"error\": \"Web search returned an unexpected response. Try again\",\n106\t        }\n107\t    except Exception:\n108\t        logger.exception(\"web_search failed\")\n109\t        return {\n110\t            \"success\": False,\n111\t            \"error\": \"Web search failed unexpectedly\",\n112\t        }\n113\t    else:\n114\t        return {\n115\t            \"success\": True,\n116\t            \"query\": query,\n117\t            \"content\": content,\n118\t        }\n119\t\n120\t\n121\t@function_tool(timeout=330)\n122\tasync def web_search(ctx: RunContextWrapper, query: str) -> str:\n123\t    \"\"\"Real-time web search via Perplexity — your primary research tool.\n124\t\n125\t    Use it liberally for anything that's not in your training data:\n126\t\n127\t    - Current CVEs, advisories, and 0-days for a specific\n128\t      service/version (``OpenSSH 9.6 RCE``, ``Jenkins 2.401.3 auth\n129\t      bypass``).\n130\t    - Latest WAF / EDR bypass techniques (``Cloudflare WAF SQLi\n131\t      bypass 2025``, ``CrowdStrike Falcon evasion``).\n132\t    - Tool documentation, flag references, payload galleries.\n133\t    - Target reconnaissance / OSINT (company tech stack, leaked\n134\t      credentials, exposed assets).\n135\t    - Cloud-provider misconfiguration patterns\n136\t      (Azure/AWS/GCP-specific attack paths).\n137\t    - Bug-bounty writeups and security research papers.\n138\t    - Compliance frameworks and CWE/CVSS guidance.\n139\t    - Picking the right Python lib / Kali tool for a job (``best 2025\n140\t      lib for JWT alg-confusion``).\n141\t    - When stuck — looking up the exact error message, ``Access\n142\t      denied`` quirks, kernel-specific local-privesc exploits.\n143\t\n144\t    Be specific: include version numbers, error messages, target\n145\t    technology, and the exact problem you're stuck on. The more context\n146\t    in the query, the more actionable the answer. Vague queries get\n147\t    generic answers.\n148\t\n149\t    A security-focused system prompt biases responses toward CVEs,\n150\t    exploits, Kali-compatible tooling, and concrete code/command\n151\t    examples.\n152\t\n153\t    **Good example queries** (each is a full sentence, names a\n154\t    version/product, and asks one concrete thing):\n155\t\n156\t    - ``\"Found OpenSSH 7.4 on port 22 — any known RCE or privesc for\n157\t      this exact version?\"``\n158\t    - ``\"Cloudflare WAF is blocking my sqlmap on a login form — what\n159\t      bypass techniques work in 2025?\"``\n160\t    - ``\"Target runs WordPress 5.8.3 + WooCommerce 6.1.1 — current\n161\t      RCE chains for this combo?\"``\n162\t    - ``\"Low-priv shell on Ubuntu 20.04 kernel 5.4.0-74-generic — what\n163\t      local privesc exploits hit this kernel?\"``\n164\t    - ``\"Compromised domain user on Windows Server 2019 AD — quietest\n165\t      paths to Domain Admin without tripping EDR?\"``\n166\t    - ``\"'Access denied' uploading a webshell to IIS 10.0 — alternate\n167\t      Windows IIS upload bypass techniques?\"``\n168\t    - ``\"Discovered Jenkins 2.401.3 on staging — current authn-bypass\n169\t      and RCE exploits for this version?\"``\n170\t    - ``\"Best 2025 Python lib for JWT algorithm-confusion + weak-secret\n171\t      cracking?\"``\n172\t\n173\t    Args:\n174\t        query: The search query — a full sentence with version numbers,\n175\t            target tech, and the specific question. Treat it like a\n176\t            ticket title for a senior security engineer.\n177\t    \"\"\"\n178\t    result = await asyncio.to_thread(_do_search, query)\n179\t    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)\n180\t"}]},"uuid":"54151bc9-d291-480b-8b43-2974aeb83a79","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:21:33.680Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"429ae585-7f1c-41a6-8285-99bda268ae43","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"bc4eccf5-27a7-4699-a55d-758a171e1f1a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EnXt5GBf5f4Dm8uABZFBY7","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Per-run notes storage — mirrored to {state_dir}/notes.json.\"\"\"\n2\t\n3\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n4\t\n5\timport asyncio\n6\timport json\n7\timport logging\n8\timport tempfile\n9\timport threading\n10\timport uuid\n11\tfrom datetime import UTC, datetime\n12\tfrom pathlib import Path\n13\tfrom typing import Any\n14\t\n15\tfrom agents import RunContextWrapper, function_tool\n16\t\n17\t\n18\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n19\t\n20\t\n21\t_notes_storage: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}\n22\t_VALID_NOTE_CATEGORIES = [\"general\", \"findings\", \"methodology\", \"questions\", \"plan\", \"wiki\"]\n23\t_notes_lock = threading.RLock()\n24\t_DEFAULT_CONTENT_PREVIEW_CHARS = 280\n25\t_NOTE_ID_GENERATION_ATTEMPTS = 1024\n26\t\n27\t_notes_path: Path | None = None\n28\t\n29\t\n30\tdef _generate_note_id() -> str | None:\n31\t    for _ in range(_NOTE_ID_GENERATION_ATTEMPTS):\n32\t        note_id = uuid.uuid4().hex[:6]\n33\t        if note_id not in _notes_storage:\n34\t            return note_id\n35\t    return None\n36\t\n37\t\n38\tdef hydrate_notes_from_disk(state_dir: Path) -> None:\n39\t    global _notes_path  # noqa: PLW0603\n40\t    _notes_path = state_dir / \"notes.json\"\n41\t    with _notes_lock:\n42\t        _notes_storage.clear()\n43\t        if not _notes_path.exists():\n44\t            return\n45\t        try:\n46\t            data = json.loads(_notes_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n47\t        except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n48\t            logger.exception(\n49\t                \"notes.json at %s is unreadable; starting with empty notes\",\n50\t                _notes_path,\n51\t            )\n52\t            return\n53\t        if not isinstance(data, dict):\n54\t            return\n55\t        _notes_storage.update(\n56\t            {\n57\t                nid: note\n58\t                for nid, note in data.items()\n59\t                if isinstance(nid, str) and isinstance(note, dict)\n60\t            }\n61\t        )\n62\t        logger.info(\n63\t            \"notes hydrated from %s (%d note(s))\",\n64\t            _notes_path,\n65\t            len(_notes_storage),\n66\t        )\n67\t\n68\t\n69\tdef _persist() -> None:\n70\t    path = _notes_path\n71\t    if path is None:\n72\t        return\n73\t    try:\n74\t        payload = json.dumps(_notes_storage, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)\n75\t        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n76\t        with (\n77\t            _notes_lock,\n78\t            tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(\n79\t                mode=\"w\",\n80\t                encoding=\"utf-8\",\n81\t                dir=str(path.parent),\n82\t                prefix=f\".{path.name}.\",\n83\t                suffix=\".tmp\",\n84\t                delete=False,\n85\t            ) as tmp,\n86\t        ):\n87\t            tmp.write(payload)\n88\t            tmp_path = Path(tmp.name)\n89\t        tmp_path.replace(path)\n90\t    except Exception:\n91\t        logger.exception(\"notes persist to %s failed\", path)\n92\t\n93\t\n94\tdef _filter_notes(\n95\t    category: str | None = None,\n96\t    tags: list[str] | None = None,\n97\t    search_query: str | None = None,\n98\t) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n99\t    filtered: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n100\t    for note_id, note in _notes_storage.items():\n101\t        if category and note.get(\"category\") != category:\n102\t            continue\n103\t        if tags:\n104\t            note_tags = note.get(\"tags\", [])\n105\t            if not any(tag in note_tags for tag in tags):\n106\t                continue\n107\t        if search_query:\n108\t            search_lower = search_query.lower()\n109\t            title_match = search_lower in note.get(\"title\", \"\").lower()\n110\t            content_match = search_lower in note.get(\"content\", \"\").lower()\n111\t            if not (title_match or content_match):\n112\t                continue\n113\t        entry = note.copy()\n114\t        entry[\"note_id\"] = note_id\n115\t        filtered.append(entry)\n116\t    filtered.sort(key=lambda x: x.get(\"created_at\", \"\"), reverse=True)\n117\t    return filtered\n118\t\n119\t\n120\tdef _to_note_listing_entry(\n121\t    note: dict[str, Any],\n122\t    *,\n123\t    include_content: bool = False,\n124\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n125\t    entry = {\n126\t        \"note_id\": note.get(\"note_id\"),\n127\t        \"title\": note.get(\"title\", \"\"),\n128\t        \"category\": note.get(\"category\", \"general\"),\n129\t        \"tags\": note.get(\"tags\", []),\n130\t        \"created_at\": note.get(\"created_at\", \"\"),\n131\t        \"updated_at\": note.get(\"updated_at\", \"\"),\n132\t    }\n133\t    content = str(note.get(\"content\", \"\"))\n134\t    if include_content:\n135\t        entry[\"content\"] = content\n136\t    elif content:\n137\t        if len(content) > _DEFAULT_CONTENT_PREVIEW_CHARS:\n138\t            entry[\"content_preview\"] = f\"{content[:_DEFAULT_CONTENT_PREVIEW_CHARS].rstrip()}...\"\n139\t        else:\n140\t            entry[\"content_preview\"] = content\n141\t    return entry\n142\t\n143\t\n144\tdef _create_note_impl(\n145\t    title: str,\n146\t    content: str,\n147\t    category: str = \"general\",\n148\t    tags: list[str] | None = None,\n149\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n150\t    with _notes_lock:\n151\t        try:\n152\t            if not title or not title.strip():\n153\t                return {\"success\": False, \"error\": \"Title cannot be empty\", \"note_id\": None}\n154\t            if not content or not content.strip():\n155\t                return {\"success\": False, \"error\": \"Content cannot be empty\", \"note_id\": None}\n156\t            if category not in _VALID_NOTE_CATEGORIES:\n157\t                return {\n158\t                    \"success\": False,\n159\t                    \"error\": (\n160\t                        f\"Invalid category. Must be one of: {', '.join(_VALID_NOTE_CATEGORIES)}\"\n161\t                    ),\n162\t                    \"note_id\": None,\n163\t                }\n164\t\n165\t            note_id = _generate_note_id()\n166\t            if note_id is None:\n167\t                return {\n168\t                    \"success\": False,\n169\t                    \"error\": \"Failed to generate a unique note ID\",\n170\t                    \"note_id\": None,\n171\t                }\n172\t\n173\t            timestamp = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat()\n174\t            note = {\n175\t                \"title\": title.strip(),\n176\t                \"content\": content.strip(),\n177\t                \"category\": category,\n178\t                \"tags\": tags or [],\n179\t                \"created_at\": timestamp,\n180\t                \"updated_at\": timestamp,\n181\t            }\n182\t            _notes_storage[note_id] = note\n183\t        except (ValueError, TypeError) as e:\n184\t            return {\"success\": False, \"error\": f\"Failed to create note: {e}\", \"note_id\": None}\n185\t        else:\n186\t            _persist()\n187\t            return {\n188\t                \"success\": True,\n189\t                \"note_id\": note_id,\n190\t                \"message\": f\"Note '{title}' created successfully\",\n191\t                \"total_count\": observation, finding, methodology step, or research note.\n304\t\n305\t    Notes are visible to every agent in the same scan for the lifetime\n306\t    of the run; they live in-memory only and are cleared when the\n307\t    process exits.\n308\t\n309\t    For actionable tasks, use ``todo`` instead — notes are for capturing\n310\t    information, todos are for tracking work.\n311\t\n312\t    Categories:\n313\t\n314\t    - ``general`` — default, anything that doesn't fit elsewhere.\n315\t    - ``findings`` — confirmed vulnerabilities or weaknesses (write\n316\t      these up promptly; you'll cite them when filing reports).\n317\t    - ``methodology`` — what you tried, what worked, what didn't —\n318\t      useful for the final scan report.\n319\t    - ``questions`` — open questions / things to come back to.\n320\t    - ``plan`` — multi-step plans you want to track.\n321\t    - ``wiki`` — long-form repository or target maps.\n322\t\n323\t    Tags are free-form (e.g. ``[\"sqli\", \"auth\", \"critical\"]``) — useful\n324\t    for later ``list_notes(tags=...)`` filtering.\n325\t\n326\t    Args:\n327\t        title: Short headline.\n328\t        content: Full note body. Markdown is preserved.\n329\t        category: One of the categories above. Default ``\"general\"``.\n330\t        tags: Optional free-form tags.\n331\t    \"\"\"\n332\t    return json.dumps(\n333\t        await asyncio.to_thread(_create_note_impl, title, content, category, tags),\n334\t        ensure_ascii=False,\n335\t        default=str,\n336\t    )\n337\t\n338\t\n339\t@function_tool(timeout=30)\n340\tasync def list_notes(\n341\t    ctx: RunContextWrapper,\n342\t    category: str | None = None,\n343\t    tags: list[str] | None = None,\n344\t    search: str | None = None,\n345\t    include_content: bool = False,\n346\t) -> str:\n347\t    \"\"\"List existing notes — metadata-first by default.\n348\t\n349\t    Filters compose: passing ``category=\"findings\"`` and\n350\t    ``tags=[\"sqli\"]`` returns notes that are *both* in the findings\n351\t    category AND have at least one of those tags.\n352\t\n353\t    By default each entry includes a ``content_preview`` (first 280\n354\t    chars). Set ``include_content=True`` to get full bodies — useful\n355\t    when you need to scan many notes; expensive in tokens for large\n356\t    notes.\n357\t\n358\t    Args:\n359\t        category: Filter by category.\n360\t        tags: Filter to notes that have any of these tags.\n361\t        search: Substring match against title and content.\n362\t        include_content: When False (default) entries have a preview;\n363\t            when True the full ``content`` is included.\n364\t    \"\"\"\n365\t    return json.dumps(\n366\t        await asyncio.to_thread(\n367\t            _list_notes_impl,\n368\t            category=category,\n369\t            tags=tags,\n370\t            search=search,\n371\t            include_content=include_content,\n372\t        ),\n373\t        ensure_ascii=False,\n374\t        default=str,\n375\t    )\n376\t\n377\t\n378\t@function_tool(timeout=30)\n379\tasync def get_note(ctx: RunContextWrapper, note_id: str) -> str:\n380\t    \"\"\"Fetch one note by its 6-char ID. Returns the full content.\n381\t\n382\t    Args:\n383\t        note_id: Note id from ``create_note`` or a ``list_notes`` entry.\n384\t    \"\"\"\n385\t    return json.dumps(\n386\t        await asyncio.to_thread(_get_note_impl, note_id), ensure_ascii=False, default=str\n387\t    )\n388\t\n389\t\n390\t@function_tool(timeout=30)\n391\tasync def update_note(\n392\t    ctx: RunContextWrapper,\n393\t    note_id: str,\n394\t    title: str | None = None,\n395\t    content: str | None = None,\n396\t    tags: list[str] | None = None,\n397\t) -> str:\n398\t    \"\"\"Update a note's title, content, or tags.\n399\t\n400\t    Pass ``None`` for any field you want left unchanged. logging.getLogger(__name__)\n18\t\n19\t\n20\tVALID_PRIORITIES = [\"low\", \"normal\", \"high\", \"critical\"]\n21\tVALID_STATUSES = [\"pending\", \"in_progress\", \"done\"]\n22\t\n23\t_PRIORITY_RANK = {\"critical\": 0, \"high\": 1, \"normal\": 2, \"low\": 3}\n24\t_STATUS_RANK = {\"done\": 0, \"in_progress\": 1, \"pending\": 2}\n25\t\n26\t\n27\tdef _todo_sort_key(todo: dict[str, Any]) -> tuple[int, int, str]:\n28\t    return (\n29\t        _STATUS_RANK.get(todo.get(\"status\", \"pending\"), 99),\n30\t        _PRIORITY_RANK.get(todo.get(\"priority\", \"normal\"), 99),\n31\t        todo.get(\"created_at\", \"\"),\n32\t    )\n33\t\n34\t\n35\t_todos_storage: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, Any]]] = {}\n36\t\n37\t_todos_path: Path | None = None\n38\t_todos_io_lock = threading.RLock()\n39\t\n40\t\n41\tdef hydrate_todos_from_disk(state_dir: Path) -> None:\n42\t    global _todos_path  # noqa: PLW0603\n43\t    _todos_path = state_dir / \"todos.json\"\n44\t    with _todos_io_lock:\n45\t        _todos_storage.clear()\n46\t        if not _todos_path.exists():\n47\t            return\n48\t        try:\n49\t            data = json.loads(_todos_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n50\t        except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n51\t            logger.exception(\n52\t                \"todos.json at %s is unreadable; starting with empty todos\",\n53\t                _todos_path,\n54\t            )\n55\t            return\n56\t        if not isinstance(data, dict):\n57\t            return\n58\t        loaded = 0\n59\t        for aid, by_id in data.items():\n60\t            if not isinstance(aid, str) or not isinstance(by_id, dict):\n61\t                continue\n62\t            cleaned = {\n63\t                str(tid): t\n64\t                for tid, t in by_id.items()\n65\t                if isinstance(tid, str) and isinstance(t, dict)\n66\t            }\n67\t            if cleaned:\n68\t                _todos_storage[aid] = cleaned\n69\t                loaded += len(cleaned)\n70\t        logger.info(\n71\t            \"todos hydrated from %s (%d agent(s), %d todo(s))\",\n72\t            _todos_path,\n73\t            len(_todos_storage),\n74\t            loaded,\n75\t        )\n76\t\n77\t\n78\tdef _persist() -> None:\n79\t    path = _todos_path\n80\t    if path is None:\n81\t        return\n82\t    try:\n83\t        payload = json.dumps(_todos_storage, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)\n84\t        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n85\t        with (\n86\t            _todos_io_lock,\n87\t            tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(\n88\t                mode=\"w\",\n89\t                encoding=\"utf-8\",\n90\t                dir=str(path.parent),\n91\t                prefix=f\".{path.name}.\",\n92\t                suffix=\".tmp\",\n93\t                delete=False,\n94\t            ) as tmp,\n95\t        ):\n96\t            tmp.write(payload)\n97\t            tmp_path = Path(tmp.name)\n98\t        tmp_path.replace(path)\n99\t    except Exception:\n100\t        logger.exception(\"todos persist to %s failed\", path)\n101\t\n102\t\n103\tdef _agent_id_from(ctx: RunContextWrapper) -> str:\n104\t    inner = ctx.context if isinstance(ctx.context, dict) else {}\n105\t    return str(inner.get(\"agent_id\") or \"default\")\n106\t\n107\t\n108\tdef _get_agent_todos(agent_id: str) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:\n109\t    return _todos_storage.setdefault(agent_id, {})\n110\t\n111\t\n112\tdef _normalize_priority(priority: str | None, default: str = \"normal\") -> str:\n113\t    candidate = (priority or default or \"normal\").lower()\n114\t    if candidate not in VALID_PRIORITIES:\n115\t        raise ValueError(f\"Invalid priority. Must be one of: {', '.join(VALID_PRIORITIES)}\")\n116\t    return candidate\n117\t\n118\t\n119\tdef _sorted_todos(agent_id: str) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n120\t    todos_list = [\n121\t        {**todo, \"todo_id\": todo_id} for todo_id, todo in _get_agent_todos(agent_id).items()\n122\t    ]\n123\t    todos_list.sort(key=_todo_sort_key)\n124\t    return todos_list\n125\t\n126\t\n127\tdef _normalize_todo_ids(raw_ids: Any) -> list[str]:\n128\t    if raw_ids is None:\n129\t        return []\n130\t    if isinstance(raw_ids, str):\n131\t        stripped = raw_ids.strip()\n132\t        if not stripped:\n133\t            return []\n134\t        try:\n135\t            data = json.loads(stripped)\n136\t        except json.JSONDecodeError:\n137\t            data = stripped.split(\",\") if \",\" in stripped else [stripped]\n138\t        if isinstance(data, list):\n139\t            return [str(item).strip() for item in data if str(item).strip()]\n140\t        return [str(data).strip()]\n141\t    if isinstance(raw_ids, list):\n142\t        return [str(item).strip() for item in raw_ids if str(item).strip()]\n143\t    return [str(raw_ids).strip()]\n144\t\n145\t\n146\tdef _normalize_bulk_updates(raw_updates: Any) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n147\t    if raw_updates is None:\n148\t        return []\n149\t    data: Any = raw_updates\n150\t    if isinstance(raw_updates, str):\n151\t        stripped = raw_updates.strip()\n152\t        if not stripped:\n153\t            return []\n154\t        try:\n155\t            data = json.loads(stripped)\n156\t        except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n157\t            raise ValueError(\"Updates must be valid JSON\") from e\n158\t\n159\t    if isinstance(data, dict):\n160\t for item in data:\n166\t        if not isinstance(item, dict):\n167\t            raise TypeError(\"Each update must be an object with todo_id\")\n168\t        todo_id = item.get(\"todo_id\") or \"todo_id\": str(todo_id).strip(),\n174\t                \"title\": item.get(\"title\"),\n175\t                \"description\": item.get(\"description\"),\n176\t                \"priority\": item.get(\"priority\"),\n177\t                \"status\": item.get(\"status\"),\n178\t            },\n179\t        )\n180\t    return normalized\n181\t\n182\t\n183\tdef _normalize_bulk_todos(raw_todos: Any) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n184\t    if raw_todos is None:\n185\t        return []\n186\t    data: Any = raw_todos\n187\t    if isinstance(raw_todos, str):\n188\t        stripped = raw_todos.strip()\n189\t        if not stripped:\n190\t            return []\n191\t        try:\n192\t            data = json.loads(stripped)\n193\t        except json.JSONDecodeError:\n194\t            entries = [line.strip(\" -*\\t\") for line in stripped.splitlines() if line.strip(\" -*\\t\")]\n195\t            return [{\"title\": entry} for entry in entries]\n196\t\n197\t    if isinstance(data, dict):\n198\t        data = [data]\n199\t    if not isinstance(data, list):\n200\t        raise TypeError(\"Todos must be provided as a list, dict, or JSON string\")\n201\t\n202\t    normalized: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n203\t    for item in data:\n204\t        if isinstance(item, str):\n205\t            title = item.strip()\n206\t            if title:\n207\t                normalized.append({\"title\": title})\n208\t            continue\n209\t        if not isinstance(item, dict):\n210\t            raise TypeError(\"Each todo entry must be a string or object with a title\")\n211\t        title = item.get(\"title\", \"\")\n212\t        if not isinstance(title, str) or not title.strip():\n213\t            raise ValueError(\"Each todo entry must include a non-empty 'title'\")\n214\t        normalized.append(\n215\t            {\n216\t                \"title\": title.strip(),\n217\t                \"description\": (item.get(\"description\") or \"\").strip() or None,\n218\t                \"priority\": item.get(\"priority\"),\n219\t            },\n220\t        )\n221\t    return normalized\n222\t\n223\t\n224\tdef _apply_single_update(\n225\t    agent_todos: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],\n226\t    todo_id: str,\n227\t    title: str | None = None,\n228\t    description: str | None = None,\n229\t    priority: str | None = None,\n230\t    status: str | None = None,\n231\t) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n232\t    if todo_id not in agent_todos:\n233\t        return {\"todo_id\": todo_id, \"error\": f\"Todo with ID '{todo_id}' not found\"}\n234\t    todo = agent_todos[todo_id]\n235\t    if title is not None:\n236\t        if not title.strip():\n237\t            return {\"todo_id\": todo_id, \"error\": \"Title cannot be empty\"}\n238\t        todo[\"title\"] = title.strip()\n239\t    if description is not None:\n240\t        todo[\"description\"] = description.strip() if description else None\n241\t    if priority is not None:\n242\t        try:\n243\t            todo[\"priority\"] = _normalize_priority(priority, str(todo.get(\"priority\", \"normal\")))\n244\t        except ValueError as exc:\n245\t            return {\"todo_id\": todo_id, \"error\": str(exc)}\n246\t    if status is not None:\n247\t        status_candidate = status.lower()\n248\t        if status_candidate not in VALID_STATUSES:\n249\t            return {\n250\t                \"todo_id\": todo_id,\n251\t                \"error\": f\"Invalid status. _EXTRA_SKILL_DIRS.append(resolved)\n35\t        logger.info(\"Registered extra skill dir: %s\", resolved)\n36\t\n37\t\n38\tdef registered_skill_dirs() -> tuple[Path, ...]:\n39\t    \"\"\"Return  if builtin.is_dir():\n48\t        roots.append(builtin)\n49\t    return tuple(roots)\n50\t\n51\t\n52\tdef _iter_user_skill_files() -> Iterator[tuple[str, str]]:\n53\t    \"\"\"Yield ``(category_name, return candidate if candidate.exists() else None\n93\t\n94\t\n95\tdef get_all_skill_names() -> set[str]:\n96\t    \"\"\"Return every user-selectable skill name (bare, no category prefix).\"\"\"\n97\t Available skills: {sorted(available)}\"\n173\t    ambiguous = sorted({s for s in skill_list if \"/\" not in s} & _get_ambiguous_skill_names())\n174\t    if ambiguous:\n175\t        return (\n176\t            f\"Ambiguous skill name(s): {ambiguous}. Use category-qualified names from: \"\n177\t            f\"{sorted(available_keys)}\"\n178\t        )\n179\t    return None\n180\t\n181\t\n182\tdef _track_skill_loaded(skill_name: str, file_path: Path) -> None:\n183\t    builtin = get_strix_resource_path(\"skills\")\n184\t    if not file_path.is_relative_to(builtin):\n185\t        skill_name = \"custom\"\n186\t\n187\t    def _send() -> None:\n188\t        posthog.skill_loaded(skill_name)\n189\t        scarf.skill_loaded(skill_name)\n190\t\n191\t    threading.Thread(target=_send, daemon=True).start()\n192\t\n193\t\n194\tdef _candidate_skill_files(skill_name: str) -> list[Path]:\n195\t    \"\"\"Resolve *skill_name* to effective matching files.\"\"\"\n196\t    if \"/\" in skill_name:\n197\t        return _qualified_skill_files(skill_name)\n198\t    return _bare_skill_files(skill_name)\n199\t\n200\t\n201\tdef load_skills(skill_names: list[str]) -> dict[str, str]:\n202\t    \"\"\"Load skill markdown bodies (frontmatter stripped) by name.\n203\t\n204\t    Skill files live at ``strix/skills/<category>/<name>.md`` (or any\n205\t    directory added via :func:`register_skill_dir`, searched first).\n206\t    Names can be ``\"name\"`` (any category), ``\"category/name\"``, or a\n207\t    bare file at the skills root. Missing skills are logged and skipped.\n208\t    \"\"\"\n209\t    search_dirs = skill_search_dirs()\n210\t    if not search_dirs:\n211\t        return {}\n212\t\n213\t    skill_content: dict[str, str] = {}\n214\t    for skill_name in skill_names:\n215\t        candidates = _candidate_skill_files(skill_name)\n216\t        if not candidates:\n217\t            logger.warning(\"Skill not found: %s\", skill_name)\n218\t            continue\n219\t        if len(candidates) > 1:\n220\t            logger.warning(\"Ambiguous skill name %s; use a category-qualified name\", skill_name)\n221\t            continue\n222\t        file_path = candidates[0]\n223\t\n224\t        try:\n225\t            content = file_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n226\t        except (OSError, ValueError) as e:\n227\t            logger.warning(\"Failed to load skill %s: %s\", skill_name, e)\n228\t            continue\n229\t\n230\t        var_name = skill_name.split(\"/\")[-1]\n231\t        skill_content[var_name] = _FRONTMATTER_PATTERN.sub(\"\", content).lstrip()\n232\t        logger.debug(\"Loaded skill: %s -> %s\", skill_name, var_name)\n233\t        _track_skill_loaded(var_name, file_path)\n234\t\n235\t    logger.debug(\"load_skills: %d skill(s) resolved\", len(skill_content))\n236\t    return skill_content\n237\t"}]},"uuid":"106134b8-cb33-4404-a65d-443ef30ddbd4","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:21:49.842Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d8f8dd88-24ee-491c-99f0-02cb5a434404","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

| None,\n174\t    cve: str | None,\n175\t    cwe: str | None,\n176\t    code_locations: list[dict[str, Any]] | None,\n177\t    fix_pr_body: str | None = None,\n178\t    agent_id: str | None = None,\n179\t    agent_name: str | None = None,\n180\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n181\t    errors: list[str] = []\n182\t    fields = {\n183\t        \"title\": title,\n184\t        \"description\": description,\n185\t        \"impact\": impact,\n186\t        \"target\": target,\n187\t        \"technical_analysis\": technical_analysis,\n188\t        \"poc_description\": poc_description,\n189\t        \"poc_script_code\": poc_script_code,\n190\t        \"remediation_steps\": remediation_steps,\n191\t        \"evidence\": evidence,\n192\t        \"assumptions\": assumptions,\n193\t    }\n194\t    for name, msg in _REQUIRED_FIELDS.items():\n195\t        if not str(fields.get(name) or \"\").strip():\n196\t            errors.append(msg)\n197\t\n198\t    fix_effort = (fix_effort or \"\").strip().lower()\n199\t    if fix_effort not in _VALID_FIX_EFFORT:\n200\t        errors.append(\n201\t            f\"Invalid fix_effort: {fix_effort!r}. Must be one of: {sorted(_VALID_FIX_EFFORT)}\"\n202\t        )\n203\t\n204\t    if not isinstance(cvss_breakdown, dict) or not cvss_breakdown:\n205\t        errors.append(\"cvss_breakdown: must be an object with the 8 CVSS metrics\")\n206\t        cvss_breakdown = {}\n207\t    else:\n208\t        for name, valid in _CVSS_VALID.items():\n209\t            value = cvss_breakdown.get(name)\n210\t            if value not in valid:\n211\t                errors.append(f\"Invalid {name}: {value}. Must be one of: {valid}\")\n212\t\n213\t    parsed_locations = _normalize_code_locations(code_locations)\n214\t    if parsed_locations:\n215\t        errors.extend(_validate_code_locations(parsed_locations))\n216\t    if cve:\n217\t        cve = _extract_cve(cve)\n218\t        cve_err = _validate_cve(cve)\n219\t        if cve_err:\n220\t            errors.append(cve_err)\n221\t    if cwe:\n222\t        cwe = _extract_cwe(cwe)\n223\t        cwe_err = _validate_cwe(cwe)\n224\t        if cwe_err:\n225\t            errors.append(cwe_err)\n226\t\n227\t    if errors:\n228\t        return {\"success\": False, \"error\": \"Validation failed\", \"errors\": errors}\n229\t\n230\t    cvss_score, severity, _vector = _calculate_cvss(cvss_breakdown)\n231\t\n232\t    try:\n233\t        from strix.report.state import get_global_report_state\n234\t\n235\t        report_state = get_global_report_state()\n236\t        if report_state is None:\n237\t            logger.warning(\"No global report state; vulnerability report not persisted\")\n238\t            return {\n239\t                \"success\": True,\n240\t                \"message\": f\"Vulnerability report '{title}' created (not persisted)\",\n241\t                \"warning\": \"Report could not be persisted - report state unavailable\",\n242\t            }\n243\t\n244\t        from strix.report.dedupe import check_duplicate\n245\t\n246\t        existing = report_state.get_existing_vulnerabilities()\n247\t        candidate = {\n248\t            \"title\": title,\n249\t            \"description\": description,\n250\t            \"impact\": impact,\n251\t            \"target\": target,\n252\t            \"technical_analysis\": technical_analysis,\n253\t            \"poc_description\": poc_description,\n254\t            \"poc_script_code\": poc_script_code,\n255\t            \"endpoint\": endpoint,\n256\t            \"method\": method,\n257\t        }\n258\t        dedupe = await check_duplicate(candidate, existing)\n259\t        if dedupe.get(\"is_duplicate\"):\n260\t            duplicate_id = dedupe.get(\"duplicate_id\", \"\")\n261\t            duplicate_title = next(\n262\t                (r.get(\"title\", \"Unknown\") for r in existing if r.get(\"id\") == duplicate_id),\n263\t                \"\",\n264\t            )\n265\t            return {\n266\t                \"success\": False,\n267\t                \"error\": (\n268\t                    f\"Potential duplicate of '{duplicate_title}' \"\n269\t                    f\"(id={duplicate_id[:8]}...) — do not re-report the same vulnerability\"\n270\t                ),\n271\t                \"duplicate_of\": duplicate_id,\n272\t                \"duplicate_title\": duplicate_title,\n273\t                \"confidence\": dedupe.get(\"confidence\", 0.0),\n274\t                \"reason\": dedupe.get(\"reason\", \"\"),\n275\t            }\n276\t\n277\t        report_id = report_state.add_vulnerability_report(\n278\t            title=title,\n279\t            description=description,\n280\t            severity=severity,\n281\t            impact=impact,\n282\t            target=target,\n283\t            technical_analysis=technical_analysis,\n284\t            poc_description=poc_description,\n285\t            poc_script_code=poc_script_code,\n286\t            remediation_steps=remediation_steps,\n287\t            evidence=evidence,\n288\t            assumptions=assumptions,\n289\t            fix_effort=fix_effort,\n290\t            cvss=cvss_score,\n291\t            cvss_breakdown=cvss_breakdown,\n292\t            endpoint=endpoint,\n293\t            method=method,\n294\t            cve=cve,\n295\t            cwe=cwe,\n296\t            code_locations=parsed_locations,\n297\t            fix_pr_body=fix_pr_body,\n298\t            agent_id=agent_id if isinstance(agent_id, str) else None,\n299\t            agent_name=agent_name if isinstance(agent_name, str) else None,\n300\t        )\n301\t    except (ImportError, AttributeError) as e:\n302\t        logger.exception(\"create_vulnerability_report persistence failed\")\n303\t        return {\"success\": False, \"error\": f\"Failed to create vulnerability report: {e!s}\"}\n304\t    else:\n305\t        logger.info(\n306\t            \"Vulnerability report created: id=%s severity=%s cvss=%.1f title=%s\",\n307\t            report_id,\n308\t            severity,\n309\t            cvss_score,\n310\t            title,\n311\t        )\n312\t        return {\n313\t            \"success\": True,\n314\t            \"message\": f\"Vulnerability report '{title}' created successfully\",\n315\t            \"report_id\": report_id,\n316\t            \"severity\": severity,\n317\t            \"cvss_score\": cvss_score,\n318\t        }\n319\t\n320\t\n321\t@function_tool(timeout=180, strict_mode=False)\n322\tasync def create_vulnerability_report(\n323\t    ctx: RunContextWrapper,\n324\t    title: str,\n325\t    description: str,\n326\t    impact: str,\n327\t    target: str,\n328\t    technical_analysis: str,\n329\t    poc_description: str,\n330\t    poc_script_code: str,\n331\t    remediation_steps: str,\n332\t    evidence: str,\n333\t    assumptions: str,\n334\t    fix_effort: str,\n335\t    cvss_breakdown: dict[str, str],\n336\t    endpoint: str | None = None,\n337\t    method: str | None = None,\n338\t    cve: str | None = None,\n339\t    cwe: str | None = None,\n340\t    code_locations: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,\n341\t    fix_pr_body: str | None = None,\n342\t) -> str:\n343\t    \"\"\"File a vulnerability report — one report per fully-verified finding.\n344\t\n345\t    **When to file**: you have a concrete vulnerability with a working\n346\t    proof-of-concept and you're 100% sure it's a real issue.\n347\t\n348\t    **When NOT to file**:\n349\t\n350\t    - General security observations without a specific vulnerability.\n351\t    - Suspicions you haven't confirmed with a PoC.\n352\t    - Tracking multiple vulnerabilities at once — one report per vuln.\n353\t    - Re-reporting something you (or another agent) already filed.\n354\t    - Known-CVE dependency / supply-chain findings that can't be\n355\t      dynamically PoC'd — a vulnerable dependency version pinned in a\n356\t      lockfile/manifest that matches a published advisory. File those\n357\t      with ``create_dependency_report`` instead, never with this tool.\n358\t\n359\t    Automatic LLM-based **deduplication** rejects reports that describe\n360\t    the same root cause on the same asset as an existing report. If you\n361\t    get a ``duplicate_of`` response, do NOT retry — move on to other\n362\t    areas.\n363\t\n364\t    **Report output rules** (this content may be rendered into generated\n365\t    reports):\n366\t\n367\t    - No internal/system details: never mention paths like\n368\t      ``/workspace``, internal tools, agents, sandboxes, models, system\n369\t      prompts, internal errors / stack traces, or tester environment.\n370\t      Never leak internal identifiers (proxy request IDs, internal\n371\t      report IDs) into any field.\n372\t    - Tone: formal, objective, third-person, vendor-neutral, concise.\n373\t      Avoid internal-guidance headings like \"QUICK\", \"Approach\", or\n374\t      \"Techniques\" that read like an engineering runbook rather than a\n375\t      client deliverable.\n376\t    - **Use markdown in every text field**: ``**bold**`` for emphasis,\n377\t      ``inline code`` for identifiers/values/parameters, and fenced\n378\t      code blocks (```` ```language ````) for any code/payload/HTTP\n379\t      excerpt. Never leave code bare/unformatted. When referencing a\n380\t      file, annotate the fence, e.g.\n381\t      ```` ```python title=app.py startLineNumber=42 endLineNumber=50 ````.\n382\t    - Field discipline: ``poc_description`` is steps only — NO code (all\n383\t      code goes in ``poc_script_code``); ``remediation_steps`` is prose\n384\t      only — NO code/diffs (code fixes go in ``code_locations``).\n385\t    - Numbered steps allowed only in PoC and Remediation sections.\n386\t    - Avoid hedging language; be precise and non-vague.\n387\t    - Follow a standard pentest report structure across the fields:\n388\t      (1) overview (``description``), (2) severity & CVSS vector\n389\t      (``cvss_breakdown``), (3) affected asset(s) (``target`` /\n390\t      ``endpoint``), (4) technical details (``technical_analysis``),\n391\t      (5) proof of concept (``poc_description`` + ``poc_script_code``),\n392\t      (6) impact (``impact``), (7) evidence (``evidence``), and\n393\t      (8) remediation (``remediation_steps``).\n394\t\n395\t    **White-box requirement**: when source is available, you MUST\n396\t    populate ``code_locations``. See the ``code_locations`` arg below\n397\t    for the full rules around ``fix_before`` / ``fix_after``,\n398\t    multi-part fixes, and informational-vs-actionable entries.\n399\t\n400\t    **CVSS breakdown** is an object with all 8 metrics (each a single\n401\t    uppercase letter):\n402\t\n403\t    - ``attack_vector``: ``N`` (Network), ``A`` (Adjacent), ``L``\n404\t      (Local), ``P`` (Physical)\n405\t    - ``attack_complexity``: ``L`` / ``H``\n406\t    - ``privileges_required``: ``N`` / ``L`` / ``H``\n407\t    - ``user_interaction``: ``N`` / ``R``\n408\t    - ``scope``: ``U`` (Unchanged) / ``C`` (Changed)\n409\t    - ``confidentiality`` / ``integrity`` / ``availability``: ``N`` /\n410\t      ``L`` / ``H``\n411\t\n412\t    Example::\n413\t\n414\t        {\n415\t            \"attack_vector\": \"N\",\n416\t            \"attack_complexity\": \"L\",\n417\t            \"privileges_required\": \"N\",\n418\t            \"user_interaction\": \"N\",\n419\t            \"scope\": \"U\",\n420\t            \"confidentiality\": \"H\",\n421\t            \"integrity\": \"H\",\n422\t            \"availability\": \"H\"\n423\t        }\n424\t\n425\t    **CVE / CWE rules**: pass the bare ID only (``CVE-2024-1234``,\n426\t    ``CWE-89``) — no name, no parenthetical. Be 100% certain; if\n427\t    unsure, use ``web_search`` to verify the ID before passing, or omit\n428\t    the field entirely. Always prefer the most specific child CWE over\n429\t    a broad parent (CWE-89 not CWE-74; CWE-78 not CWE-77). Do NOT use\n430\t    broad/parent CWEs like CWE-74, CWE-20, CWE-200, CWE-284, or\n431\t    CWE-693.\n432\t\n433\t    Common CWE references (use the ID only — names are listed here\n434\t    just for your lookup):\n435\t\n436\t    - **Injection**: CWE-79 XSS, CWE-89 SQLi, CWE-78 OS Command\n437\t      Injection, CWE-94 Code Injection, CWE-77 Command Injection.\n438\t    - **Auth / Access**: CWE-287 Improper Authentication, CWE-862\n439\t      Missing Authorization, CWE-863 Incorrect Authorization, CWE-306\n440\t      Missing Auth for Critical Function, CWE-639 Authz Bypass via\n441\t      User-Controlled Key.\n442\t    - **Web**: CWE-352 CSRF, CWE-918 SSRF, CWE-601 Open Redirect,\n443\t      CWE-434 Unrestricted File Upload.\n444\t    - **Memory**: CWE-787 OOB Write, CWE-125 OOB Read, CWE-416 UAF,\n445\t      CWE-120 Classic Buffer Overflow.\n446\t    - **Data**: CWE-502 Deserialization of Untrusted Data, CWE-22\n447\t      Path Traversal, CWE-611 XXE.\n448\t    - **Crypto / Config**: CWE-798 Hard-coded Credentials, CWE-327\n449\t      Broken / Risky Crypto, CWE-311 Missing Encryption, CWE-916 Weak\n450\t      Password Hashing.\n451\t\n452\t    Args:\n453\t        title: Specific finding title (e.g.\n454\t            ``\"SQL Injection in /api/users login parameter\"``). Don't\n455\t            include the CVE number in the title.\n456\t        description: Concise, non-technical TL;DR of the vulnerability\n457\t            (1-3 sentences) — it appears first in the report. Deep\n458\t            technical detail and root-cause analysis belong in\n459\t            ``technical_analysis``, not here.\n460\t        impact: What an attacker achieves; business risk; data at risk.\n461\t        target: Affected URL / domain / repository.\n462\t        technical_analysis: The mechanism and root cause.\n463\t        poc_description: Step-by-step reproduction (steps only, no code).\n464\t        poc_script_code: Working PoC (Python preferred).\n465\t        remediation_steps: Specific, actionable fix (prose, no code).\n466\t        evidence: Concrete proof the issue is real and exploitable —\n467\t            request/response excerpts, observed behavior, tool output.\n468\t            Use fenced code blocks; no internal identifiers/paths.\n469\t        assumptions: Short note on the assumptions/prerequisites that\n470\t            make this finding impactful or exploitable (e.g. \"assumes an\n471\t            authenticated low-privilege user\").\n472\t        fix_effort: One of ``trivial`` / ``low`` / ``medium`` / ``high``.\n473\t        cvss_breakdown: 8-metric object per the format above.\n474\t        endpoint: API path / Git path (e.g. ``/api/login``).\n475\t        method: HTTP method when relevant.\n476\t        cve: ``CVE-YYYY-NNNNN`` if certain, else omit.\n477\t        cwe: ``CWE-NNN`` (most specific child) if certain, else omit.\n478\t        code_locations: White-box findings — list of location objects.\n479\t\n480\t            **How ``fix_before`` / ``fix_after`` work**: they're used as\n481\t            literal GitHub/GitLab PR suggestion blocks. When a reviewer\n482\t            accepts the suggestion, the platform replaces the **exact\n483\t            lines from ``start_line`` to ``end_line``** with\n484\t            ``fix_after``. Therefore:\n485\t\n486\t            1. ``fix_before`` must be a **VERBATIM** copy of the source\n487\t               at those lines — same whitespace, indentation, line\n488\t               breaks. If it doesn't match character-for-character, the\n489\t               suggestion will corrupt the code when accepted.\n490\t            2. ``fix_after`` is the COMPLETE replacement for that\n491\t               entire block (may be more or fewer lines).\n492\t            3. ``start_line`` / ``end_line`` must precisely cover the\n493\t               lines in ``fix_before`` — no more, no less.\n494\t\n495\t            **Multi-part fixes**: many fixes touch multiple\n496\t            non-contiguous parts of a file (e.g. add an import at the\n497\t            top AND change code lower down). Since each\n498\t            ``fix_before`` / ``fix_after`` pair covers ONE contiguous\n499\t            block, create **separate location entries** for each\n500\t            non-contiguous part. Use ``label`` to describe each part's\n501\t            role (``\"Add escape helper import\"``, ``\"Sanitize input\n502\t            before SQL\"``). Order primary fix first, supporting\n503\t            changes (imports, config) after.\n504\t\n505\t            **Informational vs actionable**:\n506\t            - With ``fix_before`` / ``fix_after``: actionable fix\n507\t              (renders as a PR suggestion block).\n508\t            - Without them: informational context (e.g. showing the\n509\t              source of tainted data, or a sink that doesn't need\n510\t              direct editing).\n511\t\n512\t            **Per-location fields**:\n513\t            - ``file`` (REQUIRED): path **relative** to repo root. No\n514\t              leading slash, no ``..``, no ``/workspace/`` prefix.\n515\t              Right: ``\"src/db/queries.ts\"``. Wrong:\n516\t              ``\"/workspace/repo/src/db/queries.ts\"``, ``\"./src/x.py\"``,\n517\t              ``\"../../etc/passwd\"``.\n518\t            - ``start_line`` (REQUIRED): 1-based; positive integer.\n519\t              Verify against the actual file — do NOT guess.\n520\t            - ``end_line`` (REQUIRED): 1-based; ``>= start_line``.\n521\t              Only equal to ``start_line`` when the block truly is one\n522\t              line.\n523\t            - ``snippet`` (optional): verbatim source at this range.\n524\t            - ``label`` (optional): short role description; especially\n525\t              important for multi-part fixes.\n526\t            - ``fix_before`` (optional): verbatim copy of the\n527\t              vulnerable code, lines ``start_line``-``end_line``.\n528\t            - ``fix_after`` (optional): complete replacement for that\n529\t              block; syntactically valid.\n530\t\n531\t            **Common mistakes to avoid**:\n532\t            - Guessing line numbers instead of reading the file.\n533\t            - Paraphrasing / reformatting code in ``fix_before``.\n534\t            - Setting ``start_line == end_line`` when the vulnerable\n535\t              code spans multiple lines.\n536\t            - Bundling an import addition and a far-away code change\n537\t              into one location — split them.\n538\t            - Padding ``fix_before`` with surrounding context lines\n539\t              that aren't part of the fix.\n540\t            - Duplicating the same change across multiple locations.\n541\t        fix_pr_body: Optional. When source is available and you have a\n542\t            concrete fix, a markdown PR-description body proposing the\n543\t            fix (summary + rationale). Prose/markdown only — the code\n544\t            change itself belongs in ``code_locations``. Omit for\n545\t            black-box findings.\n546\t\n547\t    Example (abbreviated — mirror this structure)::\n548\t\n549\t        title: \"Reflected XSS in /search q parameter\"\n550\t        description:\n551\t            The **`q`** parameter of `/search` reflects user input into\n552\t            the HTML response without encoding, allowing script\n553\t            injection.\n554\t        technical_analysis:\n555\t            The handler interpolates `q` directly into the page body:\n556\t\n557\t            ```python title=views.py startLineNumber=42 endLineNumber=44\n558\t            html = f\"<h2>Results for {q}</h2>\"\n559\t            return HttpResponse(html)\n560\t            ```\n561\t\n562\t            No output encoding is applied, so `<script>` executes.\n563\t        poc_description:\n564\t            1. Navigate to `/search?q=<payload>`.\n565\t            2. Observe the payload executes in the victim's browser.\n566\t        poc_script_code:\n567\t            ```\n568\t            GET /search?q=<script>alert(document.domain)</script>\n569\t            ```\n570\t        evidence:\n571\t            Response echoes the payload verbatim:\n572\t\n573\t            ```html\n574\t            <h2>Results for <script>alert(document.domain)</script></h2>\n575\t            ```\n576\t        assumptions:\n577\t            Assumes a victim can be induced to open a crafted link.\n578\t        remediation_steps:\n579\t            Context-encode all user input rendered into HTML; prefer the\n580\t            template engine's auto-escaping over string interpolation.\n581\t        fix_effort: \"low\"\n582\t    \"\"\"\n583\t    inner = ctx.context if isinstance(ctx.context, dict) else {}\n584\t    raw_agent_id = inner.get(\"agent_id\")\n585\t    agent_id = raw_agent_id if isinstance(raw_agent_id, str) else None\n586\t    agent_name = None\n587\t    coordinator = inner.get(\"coordinator\")\n588\t    if agent_id is not None and coordinator is not None:\n589\t        names = getattr(coordinator, \"names\", {})\n590\t        if isinstance(names, dict):\n591\t            raw_agent_name = names.get(agent_id)\n592\t            agent_name = raw_agent_name if isinstance(raw_agent_name, str) else None\n593\t\n594\t    result = await _do_create(\n595\t        title=title,\n596\t        description=description,\n597\t        impact=impact,\n598\t        target=target,\n599\t        technical_analysis=technical_analysis,\n600\t        poc_description=poc_description,\n601\t        poc_script_code=poc_script_code,\n602\t        remediation_steps=remediation_steps,\n603\t        evidence=evidence,\n604\t        assumptions=assumptions,\n605\t        fix_effort=fix_effort,\n606\t        cvss_breakdown=cvss_breakdown,\n607\t        endpoint=endpoint,\n608\t        method=method,\n609\t        cve=cve,\n610\t        cwe=cwe,\n611\t        code_locations=code_locations,\n612\t        fix_pr_body=fix_pr_body,\n613\t        agent_id=agent_id,\n614\t        agent_name=agent_name,\n615\t    )\n616\t    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)\n617\t\n618\t\n619\t_DEP_SEVERITY_FROM_CVSS = {\n620\t    (9.0, 10.0): \"critical\",\n621\t    (7.0, 9.0): \"high\",\n622\t    (4.0, 7.0): \"medium\",\n623\t    (0.0, 4.0): \"low\",\n624\t}\n625\t\n626\t\n627\tdef _dependency_severity(advisory_cvss: float | None) -> tuple[float, str]:\n628\t    if advisory_cvss is None:\n629\t        return 0.0, \"info\"\n630\t    score = max(0.0, min(10.0, advisory_cvss))\n631\t    for (lo, hi), label in _DEP_SEVERITY_FROM_CVSS.items():\n632\t        if lo <= score < hi or (hi == 10.0 and score == 10.0):\n633\t            return score, label\n634\t    return score, \"none\"\n635\t\n636\t\n637\tdef _build_dependency_metadata(\n638\t    *,\n639\t    package_name: str,\n640\t    installed_version: str,\n641\t    package_ecosystem: str | None,\n642\t    fixed_version: str | None,\n643\t) -> dict[str, str]:\n644\t    metadata = {\n645\t        \"package_name\": package_name.strip(),\n646\t        \"installed_version\": installed_version.strip(),\n647\t    }\n648\t    if package_ecosystem and package_ecosystem.strip():\n649\t        metadata[\"package_ecosystem\"] = package_ecosystem.strip()\n650\t    if fixed_version and fixed_version.strip():\n651\t        metadata[\"fixed_version\"] = fixed_version.strip()\n652\t    return metadata\n653\t\n654\t\n655\tdef _build_dependency_evidence(\n656\t    *,\n657\t    cve: str,\n658\t    package_name: str,\n659\t    installed_version: str,\n660\t    fixed_version: str | None,\n661\t) -> str:\n662\t    evidence = (\n663\t        f\"**Advisory evidence:** `{cve}` applies to `{package_name}` \"\n664\t        f\"at installed version `{installed_version}`.\"\n665\t    )\n666\t    if fixed_version and fixed_version.strip():\n667\t        evidence += f\" The advisory is fixed in `{fixed_version.strip()}`.\"\n668\t    return evidence\n669\t\n670\t\n671\tasync def _do_create_dependency(  # noqa: PLR0912\n672\t    *,\n673\t    title: str,\n674\t    description: str,\n675\t    target: str,\n676\t    cve: str,\n677\t    package_name: str,\n678\t    installed_version: str,\n679\t    impact: str,\n680\t    remediation_steps: str,\n681\t    assumptions: str,\n682\t    package_ecosystem: str | None,\n683\t    fixed_version: str | None,\n684\t    cwe: str | None,\n685\t    advisory_cvss: float | None,\n686\t    technical_analysis: str | None,\n687\t    fix_effort: str,\n688\t    agent_id: str | None = None,\n689\t    agent_name: str | None = None,\n690\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n691\t    errors: list[str] = []\n692\t    required = {\n693\t        \"title\": title,\n694\t        \"description\": description,\n695\t        \"target\": target,\n696\t        \"package_name\": package_name,\n697\t        \"installed_version\": installed_version,\n698\t        \"package_ecosystem\": package_ecosystem,\n699\t        \"impact\": impact,\n700\t        \"remediation_steps\": remediation_steps,\n701\t        \"assumptions\": assumptions,\n702\t    }\n703\t    for name, value in
