{"parentUuid":"ea6db3d6-3ed2-4134-ae66-3a6ac0106fcc","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019yvusTWDQjuXEKyC2DV7R5","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Jinja-based system-prompt renderer.\"\"\"\n2\t\n3\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n4\t\n5\timport logging\n6\tfrom typing import Any\n7\t\n8\tfrom jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader, select_autoescape\n9\t\n10\tfrom strix.skills import get_available_skills, load_skills, skill_search_dirs\n11\tfrom strix.utils.resource_paths import get_strix_resource_path\n12\t\n13\t\n14\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n15\t\n16\t\n17\t_PROMPT_DIRNAME = \"prompts\"\n18\t\n19\t\n20\tdef _resolve_skills(\n21\t    *,\n22\t    requested: list[str] | None,\n23\t    scan_mode: str = \"deep\",\n24\t    is_whitebox: bool = False,\n25\t    is_root: bool = False,\n26\t) -> list[str]:\n27\t    \"\"\"Build the deduped, ordered skills list for the prompt render.\n28\t\n29\t    Order:\n30\t\n31\t    1. Whatever the caller asked for, in order.\n32\t    2. ``scan_modes/<mode>`` (always).\n33\t    3. ``tooling/agent_browser`` (always — every agent has shell + the\n34\t       agent-browser CLI).\n35\t    4. ``tooling/python`` (always — Python runs through ``exec_command``;\n36\t       sandbox scripts can import ``caido_api`` for Caido automation).\n37\t    5. ``coordination/root_agent`` for the root agent only — orchestration\n38\t       guidance for delegating to specialist subagents.\n39\t    6. Whitebox-specific skills if applicable.\n40\t    \"\"\"\n41\t    ordered: list[str] = list(requested or [])\n42\t    ordered.append(f\"scan_modes/{scan_mode}\")\n43\t    ordered.append(\"tooling/agent_browser\")\n44\t    ordered.append(\"tooling/python\")\n45\t    if is_root:\n46\t        ordered.append(\"coordination/root_agent\")\n47\t    if is_whitebox:\n48\t        ordered.append(\"coordination/source_aware_whitebox\")\n49\t        ordered.append(\"custom/source_aware_sast\")\n50\t\n51\t    deduped: list[str] = []\n52\t    seen: set[str] = set()\n53\t    for skill in ordered:\n54\t        if skill and skill not in seen:\n55\t            deduped.append(skill)\n56\t            seen.add(skill)\n57\t    return deduped\n58\t\n59\t\n60\tdef render_system_prompt(\n61\t    *,\n62\t    skills: list[str] | None = None,\n63\t    scan_mode: str = \"deep\",\n64\t    is_whitebox: bool = False,\n65\t    is_root: bool = False,\n66\t    interactive: bool = False,\n67\t    system_prompt_context: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n68\t) -> str:\n69\t    \"\"\"Render the system prompt. Returns empty string on template failure.\"\"\"\n70\t    try:\n71\t        prompt_dir = get_strix_resource_path(\"agents\", _PROMPT_DIRNAME)\n72\t        loader_dirs = [prompt_dir, *skill_search_dirs()]\n73\t        env = Environment(\n74\t            loader=FileSystemLoader(loader_dirs),\n75\t            autoescape=select_autoescape(\n76\t                enabled_extensions=(),\n77\t                default_for_string=False,\n78\t            ),\n79\t        )\n80\t\n81\t        skills_to_load = _resolve_skills(\n82\t            requested=skills,\n83\t            scan_mode=scan_mode,\n84\t            is_whitebox=is_whitebox,\n85\t            is_root=is_root,\n86\t        )\n87\t        skill_content = load_skills(skills_to_load)\n88\t        env.globals[\"get_skill\"] = lambda name: skill_content.get(name, \"\")\n89\t\n90\t        rendered = env.get_template(\"system_prompt.jinja\").render(\n91\t            loaded_skill_names=list(skill_content.keys()),\n92\t            available_skills=get_available_skills(),\n93\t            interactive=interactive,\n94\t            system_prompt_context=system_prompt_context or {},\n95\t            **skill_content,\n96\t        )\n97\t    except Exception:\n98\t        logger.exception(\"render_system_prompt failed; returning empty prompt\")\n99\t        return \"\"\n100\t    else:\n101\t        logger.debug(\n102\t            \"render_system_prompt: scan_mode=%s root=%s whitebox=%s skills=%d prompt_len=%d\",\n103\t            scan_mode,\n104\t            is_root,\n105\t            is_whitebox,\n106\t            len(skill_content),\n107\t            len(rendered),\n108\t        )\n109\t        return str(rendered)\n110\t"}]},"uuid":"d5083c25-85f6-4615-bb3c-e193973b0a60","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:22:27.430Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ea6db3d6-3ed2-4134-ae66-3a6ac0106fcc","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"d40ee2d1-a24e-43c3-80ae-a0f7e15f335a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01147jysKFjTnQBQqLDgz1xb","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"SARIF 2.1.0 output for Strix vulnerability reports.\n2\t\n3\tBuilds a GitHub code-scanning compatible SARIF document from Strix findings\n4\tso CI pipelines can upload findings via ``github/codeql-action/upload-sarif``,\n5\tingest into ASPM platforms, or normalise across scanners.\n6\t\n7\tSchema: SARIF 2.1.0 (OASIS). The output is validated against the official\n8\tschema at https://json.schemastore.org/sarif-2.1.0.json in tests.\n9\t\n10\tIntegration:\n11\t  - ``ReportState._save_artifacts`` calls :func:`write_sarif` to emit a\n12\t    ``findings.sarif`` sidecar alongside the existing CSV + markdown + JSON\n13\t    artefacts on every save. The call is wrapped in try/except there so a\n14\t    SARIF failure never blocks the CSV + markdown + run-record path.\n15\t\n16\tDesign notes:\n17\t  * Rules are keyed on CWE (``id = CWE-NNN``), falling back to CVE, then\n18\t    to finding-id, then to a title slug. CWE values are normalised from\n19\t    Strix output variants (``CWE-306``, ``cwe: 306``, ``306``) to the\n20\t    canonical ``CWE-NNN`` form so dedup works across runs.\n21\t  * SARIF only has three levels (error / warning / note). Strix's five\n22\t    severities collapse into them. The raw severity label and CVSS score\n23\t    survive in ``result.properties.strix`` for downstream tools that can\n24\t    distinguish CRITICAL vs HIGH.\n25\t  * GitHub code-scanning uses ``rule.properties['security-severity']``\n26\t    (a 0.0-10.0 string) to rank alerts. We populate it from CVSS when\n27\t    available, otherwise from a conservative label -> score map.\n28\t  * File locations must be repo-relative POSIX paths. Paths that look\n29\t    like URIs, absolute paths, or traversal patterns are rejected rather\n30\t    than emitted as invalid code-scanning alerts.\n31\t  * Findings with a fix suggestion (``code_locations[].fix_before`` +\n32\t    ``fix_after``) are emitted as SARIF ``fixes`` so code-scanning can\n33\t    render a one-click suggested change.\n34\t  * Endpoint / target-only findings (typical of DAST) carry a SARIF\n35\t    ``logicalLocations`` entry so the finding keeps a meaningful anchor\n36\t    even without a source file + line.\n37\t  * When a repository context is supplied (repo URL / commit / branch),\n38\t    the run carries ``versionControlProvenance`` + ``automationDetails``\n39\t    so code-scanning can bind alerts to the scanned commit and branch.\n40\t  * Findings without safe locations still appear in the SARIF output,\n41\t    anchored to SECURITY.md and flagged via\n42\t    ``properties.synthetic_location`` rather than being dropped silently.\n43\t\"\"\"\n44\t\n45\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n46\t\n47\timport hashlib\n48\timport json\n49\timport logging\n50\timport os\n51\timport re\n52\tfrom pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath\n53\tfrom typing import Any, cast\n54\t\n55\t\n56\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n57\t\n58\t\n59\tSARIF_SCHEMA = \"https://json.schemastore.org/sarif-2.1.0.json\"\n60\tSARIF_VERSION = \"2.1.0\"\n61\tTOOL_NAME = \"Strix\"\n62\tTOOL_INFORMATION_URI = \"https://strix.ai\"\n63\t\n64\t# Synthetic anchor for findings that have no safe code location. SARIF\n65\t# requires every result to carry at least one location, and GitHub\n66\t# code-scanning's UI handles locationless results unreliably. Anchoring\n67\t# to SECURITY.md keeps the result valid + visible while a\n68\t# ``properties.synthetic_location: true`` flag lets downstream tooling\n69\t# distinguish synthetic anchors from real source locations. Anchoring\n70\t# also lets the partialFingerprints + class-hash code path cover these\n71\t# findings instead of re-orphaning them on every run.\n72\t_SYNTHETIC_LOCATION_URI = \"SECURITY.md\"\n73\t\n74\t\n75\t# SARIF only has three result levels; Strix's five severities collapse here.\n76\t# Original label survives in ``result.properties.strix.severity``.\n77\t_SEVERITY_TO_LEVEL = {\n78\t    \"critical\": \"error\",\n79\t    \"high\": \"error\",\n80\t    \"medium\": \"warning\",\n81\t    \"low\": \"note\",\n82\t    \"info\": \"note\",\n83\t    \"informational\": \"note\",\n84\t}\n85\t\n86\t# GitHub code-scanning reads ``rule.properties['security-severity']`` (a\n87\t# 0.0-10.0 string) to rank alerts. We prefer CVSS from the finding; absent\n88\t# that we fall back to a conservative label -> score map.\n89\t_SEVERITY_TO_SCORE = {\n90\t    \"critical\": \"9.5\",\n91\t    \"high\": \"8.0\",\n92\t    \"medium\": \"5.5\",\n93\t    \"low\": \"3.0\",\n94\t    \"info\": \"1.0\",\n95\t    \"informational\": \"1.0\",\n96\t}\n97\t\n98\t\n99\t# CWE → STRIDE-leg mapping for SARIF rule tagging. Each finding's rule gets one\n100\t# or more ``stride:<leg>`` tags (Spoofing / Tampering / Repudiation /\n101\t# Information disclosure / Denial of service / Elevation of privilege) so\n102\t# consumers — the GitHub code-scanning Security tab, ASPM dashboards, coverage\n103\t# reports — can group and filter findings by threat-model leg. SARIF results\n104\t# inherit their rule's tags via ``ruleId``, so tagging the rule is sufficient.\n105\t#\n106\t# Where a CWE plausibly spans multiple legs the dominant one is listed first.\n107\t# Unmapped / no-CWE findings fall back to ``_DEFAULT_STRIDE_LEGS`` so every\n108\t# finding carries at least one leg (no coverage gaps in downstream reports).\n109\t_CWE_TO_STRIDE: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {\n110\t    # Spoofing — authentication / identity\n111\t    \"287\": (\"S\",),  # Improper Authentication\n112\t    \"290\": (\"S\",),  # Authentication Bypass by Spoofing\n113\t    \"294\": (\"S\",),  # Authentication Bypass by Capture-replay\n114\t    \"306\": (\"S\", \"E\"),  # Missing Authentication for Critical Function\n115\t    \"345\": (\"S\", \"T\"),  # Insufficient Verification of Data Authenticity\n116\t    \"346\": (\"S\",),  # Origin Validation Error\n117\t    \"352\": (\"T\", \"S\"),  # Cross-Site Request Forgery\n118\t    \"384\": (\"S\",),  # Session Fixation\n119\t    \"521\": (\"S\",),  # Weak Password Requirements\n120\t    \"613\": (\"S\",),  # Insufficient Session Expiration\n121\t    \"640\": (\"S\",),  # Weak Password Recovery Mechanism\n122\t    \"259\": (\"S\", \"I\"),  # Use of Hard-coded Password\n123\t    \"798\": (\"S\", \"I\"),  # Use of Hard-coded Credentials\n124\t    \"1391\": (\"S\",),  # Use of Weak Credentials\n125\t    # Tampering — integrity\n126\t    \"20\": (\"T\",),  # Improper Input Validation\n127\t    \"73\": (\"T\", \"I\"),  # External Control of File Name or Path\n128\t    \"78\": (\"T\", \"E\"),  # OS Command Injection\n129\t    \"79\": (\"T\", \"I\"),  # Cross-Site Scripting\n130\t    \"89\": (\"T\",),  # SQL Injection\n131\t    \"91\": (\"T\",),  # XML Injection\n132\t    \"94\": (\"T\", \"E\"),  # Code Injection\n133\t    \"434\": (\"T\",),  # Unrestricted File Upload\n134\t    \"502\": (\"T\", \"E\"),  # Deserialization of Untrusted Data\n135\t    \"915\": (\"E\", \"T\"),  # Improperly Controlled Modification (Mass Assignment)\n136\t    \"918\": (\"T\", \"I\"),  # Server-Side Request Forgery\n137\t    \"1336\": (\"T\", \"E\"),  # Server-Side Template Injection\n138\t    # Repudiation — audit / logging\n139\t    \"117\": (\"R\",),  # Improper Output Neutralization for Logs\n140\t    \"223\": (\"R\",),  # Omission of Security-relevant Information\n141\t    \"778\": (\"R\",),  # Insufficient Logging\n142\t    # Information disclosure — confidentiality\n143\t    \"200\": (\"I\",),  # Exposure of Sensitive Information\n144\t    \"201\": (\"I\",),  # Insertion of Sensitive Info into Sent Data\n145\t    \"209\": (\"I\",),  # Generation of Error Message Containing Sensitive Info\n146\t    \"256\": (\"I\",),  # Plaintext Storage of a Password\n147\t    \"311\": (\"I\",),  # Missing Encryption of Sensitive Data\n148\t    \"319\": (\"I\",),  # Cleartext Transmission of Sensitive Information\n149\t    \"327\": (\"I\",),  # Use of a Broken or Risky Cryptographic Algorithm\n150\t    \"328\": (\"I\",),  # Use of Weak Hash\n151\t    \"522\": (\"I\",),  # Insufficiently Protected Credentials\n152\t    \"525\": (\"I\",),  # Use of Web Browser Cache Containing Sensitive Info\n153\t    \"532\": (\"I\",),  # Insertion of Sensitive Information into Log File\n154\t    \"538\": (\"I\",),  # Insertion of Sensitive Info into Externally-Accessible File\n155\t    \"598\": (\"I\",),  # Use of GET Request Method With Sensitive Query Strings\n156\t    # Denial of service — availability\n157\t    \"400\": (\"D\",),  # Uncontrolled Resource Consumption\n158\t    \"770\": (\"D\",),  # Allocation of Resources Without Limits or Throttling\n159\t    \"1333\": (\"D\",),  # Inefficient Regular Expression Complexity (ReDoS)\n160\t    # Elevation of privilege — authorization\n161\t    \"269\": (\"E\",),  # Improper Privilege Management\n162\t    \"284\": (\"E\",),  # Improper Access Control\n163\t    \"285\": (\"E\",),  # Improper Authorization\n164\t    \"639\": (\"E\",),  # Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR/BOLA)\n165\t    \"732\": (\"E\",),  # Incorrect Permission Assignment for Critical Resource\n166\t    \"862\": (\"E\",),  # Missing Authorization\n167\t    \"863\": (\"E\",),  # Incorrect Authorization\n168\t    \"1220\": (\"E\",),  # Insufficient Granularity of Access Control\n169\t    # Multi-leg\n170\t    \"22\": (\"T\", \"I\"),  # Path Traversal — write/read arbitrary paths (T) + file disclosure (I)\n171\t    \"611\": (\"I\", \"T\"),  # XML External Entity (XXE)\n172\t}\n173\t\n174\t# Default for unmapped / no-CWE findings: tampering + information-disclosure is\n175\t# the most-common shape for an unclassified bug (matches the fork's and\n176\t# strix-triage's DEFAULT_STRIDE_LEGS convention).\n177\t_DEFAULT_STRIDE_LEGS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"T\", \"I\")\n178\t\n179\t\n180\tdef _stride_legs_for_cwe(cwe: str | None) -> tuple[str, ...]:\n181\t    \"\"\"Map a CWE id (``CWE-306`` / ``306`` / ``cwe: 306``) to STRIDE legs.\n182\t    Returns ``_DEFAULT_STRIDE_LEGS`` for no-CWE / unrecognised input so every\n183\t    finding gets at least one leg tag.\"\"\"\n184\t    if not cwe:\n185\t        return _DEFAULT_STRIDE_LEGS\n186\t    digits = \"\".join(c for c in str(cwe) if c.isdigit())\n187\t    if not digits:\n188\t        return _DEFAULT_STRIDE_LEGS\n189\t    return _CWE_TO_STRIDE.get(digits, _DEFAULT_STRIDE_LEGS)\n190\t\n191\t\n192\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n193\t# Public API\n194\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n195\t\n196\t\n197\tdef build_sarif_report(\n198\t    vulnerability_reports: list[dict[str, Any]],\n199\t    *,\n200\t    tool_version: str | None = None,\n201\t    repository_context: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n202\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n203\t    \"\"\"Return a SARIF 2.1.0 document for findings.\n204\t\n205\t    ``repository_context`` (optional) supplies VCS provenance for repo\n206\t    scans: ``repositoryUri``, ``repositoryFullName``, ``commitSha``,\n207\t    ``branch``, ``ref``. When present, the run carries\n208\t    ``versionControlProvenance`` + ``automationDetails`` so code-scanning\n209\t    can bind alerts to the scanned commit; it is omitted for URL / IP\n210\t    (DAST) targets that have no repository.\n211\t\n212\t    Findings without safe source locations are anchored synthetically\n213\t    to SECURITY.md and flagged via ``properties.synthetic_location``.\n214\t    They're still emitted as proper SARIF results so they (a) flow\n215\t    through code-scanning normally rather than being shunted into a\n216\t    run-properties summary the UI can't render, and (b) carry the\n217\t    partialFingerprints + class hash so cross-run dismissal stickiness\n218\t    works for them.\n219\t    \"\"\"\n220\t    rules_by_id: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}\n221\t    rule_index_by_id: dict[str, int] = {}\n222\t    results: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n223\t    synthetic_location_count = 0\n224\t    dropped_unsafe_location_findings: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n225\t\n226\t    for report in vulnerability_reports:\n227\t        locations, is_synthetic, dropped_location_count = _build_locations(report)\n228\t        if is_synthetic:\n229\t            synthetic_location_count += 1\n230\t\n231\t        if dropped_location_count:\n232\t            dropped_unsafe_location_findings.append(\n233\t                _dropped_location_summary(report, dropped_location_count)\n234\t            )\n235\t\n236\t        rule_id = _rule_id(report)\n237\t        if rule_id not in rules_by_id:\n238\t            rule_index_by_id[rule_id] = len(rules_by_id)\n239\t            rules_by_id[rule_id] = _build_rule(rule_id, report)\n240\t        results.append(\n241\t            _build_result(\n242\t                rule_id,\n243\t                rule_index_by_id[rule_id],\n244\t                report,\n245\t                locations,\n246\t                is_synthetic=is_synthetic,\n247\t            )\n248\t        )\n249\t\n250\t    driver: dict[str, Any] = {\n251\t        \"name\": TOOL_NAME,\n252\t        \"informationUri\": TOOL_INFORMATION_URI,\n253\t        \"rules\": list(rules_by_id.values()),\n254\t    }\n255\t    if tool_version:\n256\t        driver[\"version\"] = tool_version\n257\t\n258\t    run: dict[str, Any] = {\n259\t        \"tool\": {\"driver\": driver},\n260\t        \"results\": results,\n261\t    }\n262\t\n263\t    run_properties: dict[str, Any] = {}\n264\t    if synthetic_location_count:\n265\t        # Surface the count for observability without duplicating the\n266\t        # findings themselves — they're already in `results[]` with\n267\t        # `properties.synthetic_location: true`. Having a top-level count\n268\t        # means CI logs / dashboards can bookkeep without parsing the\n269\t        # result list.\n270\t        run_properties[\"syntheticLocationCount\"] = synthetic_location_count\n271\t    if dropped_unsafe_location_findings:\n272\t        run_properties[\"droppedUnsafeLocationCount\"] = sum(\n273\t            finding[\"droppedLocationCount\"] for finding in dropped_unsafe_location_findings\n274\t        )\n275\t        run_properties[\"droppedUnsafeLocationFindings\"] = dropped_unsafe_location_findings\n276\t    if run_properties:\n277\t        run[\"properties\"] = run_properties\n278\t\n279\t    if repository_context:\n280\t        _apply_repository_context(run, repository_context)\n281\t\n282\t    return {\n283\t        \"version\": SARIF_VERSION,\n284\t        \"$schema\": SARIF_SCHEMA,\n285\t        \"runs\": [run],\n286\t    }\n287\t\n288\t\n289\tdef write_sarif_report(\n290\t    output_path: Path,\n291\t    vulnerability_reports: list[dict[str, Any]],\n292\t    *,\n293\t    tool_version: str | None = None,\n294\t    repository_context: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n295\t) -> None:\n296\t    \"\"\"Write a SARIF report to disk, creating parent directories first.\n297\t\n298\t    Writes to a sibling temp file and atomically replaces the target, so a\n299\t    crash or serialization error mid-write can never leave a truncated\n300\t    ``findings.sarif`` for CI to upload in place of the last complete snapshot.\n301\t    \"\"\"\n302\t    output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n303\t    sarif = build_sarif_report(\n304\t        vulnerability_reports,\n305\t        tool_version=tool_version,\n306\t        repository_context=repository_context,\n307\t    )\n308\t    tmp_path = output_path.with_name(f\"{output_path.name}.{os.getpid()}.tmp\")\n309\t    try:\n310\t        with tmp_path.open(\"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as sarif_file:\n311\t            json.dump(sarif, sarif_file, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)\n312\t            sarif_file.write(\"\\n\")\n313\t        tmp_path.replace(output_path)  # atomic on the same filesystem\n314\t    finally:\n315\t        tmp_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n316\t\n317\t\n318\tdef write_sarif(\n319\t    run_dir: Path,\n320\t    reports: list[dict[str, Any]],\n321\t    *,\n322\t    tool_version: str | None = None,\n323\t    repository_context: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n324\t    filename: str = \"findings.sarif\",\n325\t) -> Path:\n326\t    \"\"\"Write ``findings.sarif`` alongside existing outputs in ``run_dir``.\n327\t\n328\t    Returns the output path. This is the ``ReportState`` entry point: SARIF\n329\t    writing must never break the CSV + markdown path, so the caller wraps\n330\t    it in try/except.\n331\t    \"\"\"\n332\t    out = run_dir / filename\n333\t    write_sarif_report(\n334\t        out,\n335\t        reports,\n336\t        tool_version=tool_version,\n337\t        repository_context=repository_context,\n338\t    )\n339\t    logger.info(\n340\t        \"Wrote SARIF 2.1.0 report: %s (%d results)\",\n341\t        out,\n342\t        len(reports),\n343\t    )\n344\t    return out\n345\t\n346\t\n347\t# ``build_sarif_document`` is a convenience alias for callers that prefer a\n348\t# name mirroring ``write_sarif_report``.\n349\tdef build_sarif_document(\n350\t    reports: list[dict[str, Any]],\n351\t    *,\n352\t    tool_version: str | None = None,\n353\t    repository_context: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n354\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n355\t    return build_sarif_report(\n356\t        reports,\n357\t        tool_version=tool_version,\n358\t        repository_context=repository_context,\n359\t    )\n360\t\n361\t\n362\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n363\t# Repository provenance\n364\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n365\t\n366\t\n367\tdef _apply_repository_context(run: dict[str, Any], context: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n368\t    \"\"\"Attach VCS provenance to a run for code-scanning alert binding.\n369\t\n370\t    ``automationDetails.id`` categorises the run (``strix/<owner>/<repo>``)\n371\t    so multiple Strix runs against the same repo reconcile rather than pile\n372\t    up. ``versionControlProvenance`` records the exact repo + commit + branch\n373\t    the findings came from. Both are omitted when the corresponding context\n374\t    fields are absent (e.g. DAST-only scans).\n375\t    \"\"\"\n376\t    full_name = _string_value(context.get(\"repositoryFullName\"))\n377\t    uri = _string_value(context.get(\"repositoryUri\"))\n378\t    commit = _string_value(context.get(\"commitSha\"))\n379\t    branch = _string_value(context.get(\"branch\"))\n380\t    ref = _string_value(context.get(\"ref\"))\n381\t\n382\t    if full_name:\n383\t        run[\"automationDetails\"] = {\"id\": f\"strix/{full_name}\"}\n384\t\n385\t    if uri:\n386\t        provenance: dict[str, Any] = {\"repositoryUri\": uri}\n387\t        if commit:\n388\t            provenance[\"revisionId\"] = commit\n389\t        if branch:\n390\t            provenance[\"branch\"] = branch\n391\t        run[\"versionControlProvenance\"] = [provenance]\n392\t\n393\t    properties = run.setdefault(\"properties\", {})\n394\t    if full_name:\n395\t        properties[\"repository\"] = full_name\n396\t    if ref:\n397\t        properties[\"ref\"] = ref\n398\t    if commit:\n399\t        properties[\"commit_sha\"] = commit\n400\t    if not properties:\n401\t        run.pop(\"properties\", None)\n402\t\n403\t\n404\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n405\t# Rule + result builders\n406\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n407\t\n408\t\n409\tdef _build_rule(rule_id: str, report: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n410\t    \"\"\"Build a SARIF rule descriptor from a Strix finding.\"\"\"\n411\t    title = _string_value(report.get(\"title\")) or rule_id\n412\t    full_description = _string_value(report.get(\"description\")) or title\n413\t    help_text = _help_text(report, full_description)\n414\t\n415\t    rule: dict[str, Any] = {\n416\t        \"id\": rule_id,\n417\t        \"name\": _rule_name(rule_id, title),\n418\t        \"shortDescription\": {\"text\": title},\n419\t        \"fullDescription\": {\"text\": full_description},\n420\t        \"defaultConfiguration\": {\"level\": _sarif_level(report.get(\"severity\"))},\n421\t        \"help\": {\"text\": help_text, \"markdown\": help_text},\n422\t    }\n423\t\n424\t    properties: dict[str, Any] = {\n425\t        \"security-severity\": _security_severity(report),\n426\t    }\n427\t    tags = _rule_tags(rule_id, report)\n428\t    if tags:\n429\t        properties[\"tags\"] = tags\n430\t    rule[\"properties\"] = properties\n431\t\n432\t    help_uri = _help_uri_for(rule_id)\n433\t    if help_uri:\n434\t        rule[\"helpUri\"] = help_uri\n435\t\n436\t    return rule\n437\t\n438\t\n439\tdef _build_result(\n440\t    rule_id: str,\n441\t    rule_index: int,\n442\t    report: dict[str, Any],\n443\t    locations: list[dict[str, Any]],\n444\t    *,\n445\t    is_synthetic: bool = False,\n446\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n447\t    \"\"\"Build one SARIF result using validated locations.\n448\t\n449\t    ``is_synthetic`` flags results whose location is the SECURITY.md\n450\t    anchor rather than a real code location — surfaces as\n451\t    ``properties.synthetic_location: true`` so reviewers and downstream\n452\t    tooling can distinguish anchored-locationless findings from\n453\t    source-linked ones.\n454\t    \"\"\"\n455\t    title = _string_value(report.get(\"title\")) or rule_id\n456\t    description = _string_value(report.get(\"description\"))\n457\t    message_text = f\"{title}\\n\\n{description}\" if description else title\n458\t\n459\t    result: dict[str, Any] = {\n460\t        \"ruleId\": rule_id,\n461\t        \"ruleIndex\": rule_index,\n462\t        \"level\": _sarif_level(report.get(\"severity\")),\n463\t        \"message\": {\"text\": message_text},\n464\t    }\n465\t    if locations:\n466\t        result[\"locations\"] = locations\n467\t\n468\t    fixes = _build_fixes(report)\n469\t    if fixes:\n470\t        result[\"fixes\"] = fixes\n471\t\n472\t    # Code-scanning auto-resolution + dismissal-stickiness key on\n473\t    # partialFingerprints. Computed from the deterministic primitives\n474\t    # this report already carries (CWE, primary code location, route\n475\t    # tuple) — NOT from the LLM-authored title or message body, which\n476\t    # vary cosmetically across runs of the same finding.\n477\t    fp = _primary_fingerprint(rule_id, report, locations, is_synthetic=is_synthetic)\n478\t    if fp:\n479\t        result[\"partialFingerprints\"] = {\"primaryLocationLineHash\": fp}\n480\t    # File-independent class fingerprint as a sibling property: lets\n481\t    # downstream tooling carry \"won't fix\" / \"false positive\"\n482\t    # determinations across file rename refactors where the primary\n483\t    # fingerprint legitimately shifts but the underlying class is\n484\t    # unchanged.\n485\t    class_fp = _class_fingerprint(rule_id, report)\n486\t    result[\"properties\"] = _result_properties(report, class_fp, is_synthetic=is_synthetic)\n487\t    return result\n488\t\n489\t\n490\tdef _result_properties(\n491\t    report: dict[str, Any],\n492\t    class_fingerprint: str | None = None,\n493\t    *,\n494\t    is_synthetic: bool = False,\n495\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n496\t    \"\"\"Strix-specific metadata for downstream consumers.\n497\t\n498\t    The top-level ``security-severity`` matches GitHub code-scanning's\n499\t    expected property. Strix-specific fields are namespaced under\n500\t    ``strix`` so generic SARIF consumers don't see them by default.\n501\t    \"\"\"\n502\t    properties: dict[str, Any] = {\n503\t        \"security-severity\": _security_severity(report),\n504\t    }\n505\t    if class_fingerprint:\n506\t        # Surfaced at top level so cross-rename dismissal tooling can\n507\t        # filter alerts by it without parsing the nested strix.* tree.\n508\t        properties[\"strix_vuln_class_hash\"] = class_fingerprint\n509\t    if is_synthetic:\n510\t        # Top-level so reviewers + downstream automation can filter\n511\t        # synthetic-anchored alerts without parsing the nested strix.*\n512\t        # tree.\n513\t        properties[\"synthetic_location\"] = True\n514\t\n515\t    strix: dict[str, Any] = {}\n516\t    for key in (\n517\t        \"id\",\n518\t        \"severity\",\n519\t        \"cvss\",\n520\t        \"timestamp\",\n521\t        \"target\",\n522\t        \"endpoint\",\n523\t        \"method\",\n524\t        \"cve\",\n525\t        \"cwe\",\n526\t        \"impact\",\n527\t        \"technical_analysis\",\n528\t        \"remediation_steps\",\n529\t    ):\n530\t        value = report.get(key)\n531\t        if value not in (None, \"\"):\n532\t            strix[key] = value\n533\t\n534\t    # SARIF is written for external upload (code-scanning / ASPM), so it must\n535\t    # NOT carry the weaponized exploit payload — that stays a local run\n536\t    # artifact (vulnerabilities.json / the finding MD). We surface the PoC\n537\t    # *description* (triage context) and a boolean flag that a script exists,\n538\t    # so consumers know to look at the local artifact, but never the script\n539\t    # body itself.\n540\t    poc_description = _string_value(report.get(\"poc_description\"))\n541\t    poc_script = _string_value(report.get(\"poc_script_code\"))\n542\t    if poc_description or poc_script:\n543\t        poc: dict[str, Any] = {}\n544\t        if poc_description:\n545\t            poc[\"description\"] = poc_description\n546\t        if poc_script:\n547\t            poc[\"script_available\"] = True\n548\t        strix[\"poc\"] = poc\n549\t\n550\t    if strix:\n551\t        properties[\"strix\"] = strix\n552\t\n553\t    return properties\n554\t\n555\t\n556\tdef _build_fixes(report: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]] | None:\n557\t    \"\"\"Build SARIF ``fixes`` from a finding's code-location fix pairs.\n558\t\n559\t    Strix findings carry the suggested change inline on each code\n560\t    location as ``fix_before`` + ``fix_after``. We map every location\n561\t    that has both (and a safe repo-relative URI + start line) into a\n562\t    SARIF ``artifactChange``, replacing the finding's region with the\n563\t    fixed text. Returns None when no location carries a usable fix pair.\n564\t    \"\"\"\n565\t    raw_locations = report.get(\"code_locations\")\n566\t    if not isinstance(raw_locations, list):\n567\t        return None\n568\t\n569\t    artifact_changes: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n570\t    for location in raw_locations:\n571\t        if not isinstance(location, dict):\n572\t            continue\n573\t        file_path = _string_value(location.get(\"file\"))\n574\t        fix_before = _string_value(location.get(\"fix_before\"))\n575\t        fix_after = _string_value(location.get(\"fix_after\"))\n576\t        start_line = location.get(\"start_line\")\n577\t        if not (file_path and fix_before and fix_after):\n578\t            continue\n579\t        if type(start_line) is not int or start_line < 1:\n580\t            continue\n581\t        uri = _sarif_uri(file_path)\n582\t        if uri is None:\n583\t            continue\n584\t\n585\t        deleted_region: dict[str, Any] = {\"startLine\": start_line}\n586\t        end_line = location.get(\"end_line\")\n587\t        if type(end_line) is int and end_line >= start_line:\n588\t            deleted_region[\"endLine\"] = end_line\n589\t\n590\t        artifact_changes.append(\n591\t            {\n592\t                \"artifactLocation\": {\"uri\": uri},\n593\t                \"replacements\": [\n594\t                    {\n595\t                        \"deletedRegion\": deleted_region,\n596\t                        \"insertedContent\": {\"text\": fix_after},\n597\t                    }\n598\t                ],\n599\t            }\n600\t        )\n601\t\n602\t    if not artifact_changes:\n603\t        return None\n604\t\n605\t    fix: dict[str, Any] = {\"artifactChanges\": artifact_changes}\n606\t    remediation = _string_value(report.get(\"remediation_steps\"))\n607\t    if remediation:\n608\t        fix[\"description\"] = {\"text\": remediation, \"markdown\": remediation}\n609\t    return [fix]\n610\t\n611\t\n612\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n613\t# Location handling\n614\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n615\t\n616\t\n617\tdef _synthetic_location() -> dict[str, Any]:\n618\t    \"\"\"Synthetic anchor for findings with no safe code location.\n619\t\n620\t    SARIF requires every result to carry at least one location, and\n621\t    code-scanning's UI handles locationless results unreliably.\n622\t    Anchoring to SECURITY.md gives the result a valid + visible\n623\t    location; the result's ``properties.synthetic_location: true``\n624\t    flag lets reviewers + tooling distinguish synthetic from real.\n625\t    \"\"\"\n626\t    return {\n627\t        \"physicalLocation\": {\n628\t            \"artifactLocation\": {\"uri\": _SYNTHETIC_LOCATION_URI},\n629\t        }\n630\t    }\n631\t\n632\t\n633\tdef _build_locations(report: dict[str, Any]) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], bool, int]:\n634\t    \"\"\"Return ``(locations, is_synthetic, dropped_count)`` for a finding.\n635\t\n636\t    Physical locations come from validated ``code_locations``. When none\n637\t    are safe, the result is anchored to SECURITY.md (``is_synthetic``).\n638\t    An ``endpoint`` (typical of DAST findings) is added as a\n639\t    ``logicalLocations`` entry; a locationless, endpoint-less finding\n640\t    gets a ``resource`` logical location carrying the target so the\n641\t    finding keeps a human-meaningful anchor.\n642\t    \"\"\"\n643\t    physical, dropped_location_count = _build_physical_locations(report.get(\"code_locations\"))\n644\t    is_synthetic = not physical\n645\t    locations: list[dict[str, Any]] = list(physical) if physical else [_synthetic_location()]\n646\t\n647\t    endpoint = _string_value(report.get(\"endpoint\"))\n648\t    if endpoint:\n649\t        locations.append(\n650\t            {\"logicalLocations\": [{\"fullyQualifiedName\": endpoint, \"kind\": \"endpoint\"}]}\n651\t        )\n652\t    elif is_synthetic:\n653\t        resource = _string_value(report.get(\"target\")) or _string_value(report.get(\"title\"))\n654\t        if resource:\n655\t            locations.append(\n656\t                {\"logicalLocations\": [{\"fullyQualifiedName\": resource, \"kind\": \"resource\"}]}\n657\t            )\n658\t\n659\t    return locations, is_synthetic, dropped_location_count\n660\t\n661\t\n662\tdef _build_physical_locations(raw_locations: Any) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], int]:\n663\t    \"\"\"Return SARIF physical locations and a count of dropped unsafe locations.\"\"\"\n664\t    if not isinstance(raw_locations, list):\n665\t        return [], 0\n666\t\n667\t    locations: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n668\t    dropped_location_count = 0\n669\t    for location in raw_locations:\n670\t        if not isinstance(location, dict):\n671\t            dropped_location_count += 1\n672\t            continue\n673\t\n674\t        file_path = _string_value(location.get(\"file\"))\n675\t        start_line = location.get(\"start_line\")\n676\t        end_line = location.get(\"end_line\")\n677\t        if not file_path or type(start_line) is not int or start_line < 1:\n678\t            dropped_location_count += 1\n679\t            continue\n680\t        uri = _sarif_uri(file_path)\n681\t        if uri is None:\n682\t            dropped_location_count += 1\n683\t            continue\n684\t\n685\t        region: dict[str, Any] = {\"startLine\": start_line}\n686\t        if type(end_line) is int and end_line >= start_line:\n687\t            region[\"endLine\"] = end_line\n688\t\n689\t        snippet = _string_value(location.get(\"snippet\"))\n690\t        if snippet:\n691\t            region[\"snippet\"] = {\"text\": snippet}\n692\t\n693\t        physical_location: dict[str, Any] = {\n694\t            \"artifactLocation\": {\"uri\": uri},\n695\t            \"region\": region,\n696\t        }\n697\t        entry: dict[str, Any] = {\"physicalLocation\": physical_location}\n698\t\n699\t        label = _string_value(location.get(\"label\"))\n700\t        if label:\n701\t            entry[\"message\"] = {\"text\": label}\n702\t\n703\t        locations.append(entry)\n704\t\n705\t    return locations, dropped_location_count\n706\t\n707\t\n708\tdef _sarif_uri(file_path: str) -> str | None:\n709\t    \"\"\"Return a safe repo-relative SARIF URI, or None for unsafe paths.\"\"\"\n710\t    uri = PurePosixPath(file_path.replace(\"\\\\\", \"/\")).as_posix()\n711\t    parts = PurePosixPath(uri).parts\n712\t    if not uri or uri.startswith(\"/\") or not parts:\n713\t        return None\n714\t    if \":\" in parts[0] or any(part == \"..\" for part in parts):\n715\t        return None\n716\t    return uri\n717\t\n718\t\n719\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n720\t# Rule ID resolution + CWE normalisation\n721\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n722\t\n723\t\n724\tdef _rule_id(report: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n725\t    \"\"\"Choose a stable SARIF rule id, preferring CWE → CVE → finding-id → slug.\n726\t\n727\t    CWE values are normalised from Strix output variants (``CWE-306``,\n728\t    ``cwe: 306``, ``306``) to the canonical ``CWE-NNN`` form. Without\n729\t    normalisation, the same weakness across runs dedups to separate rules.\n730\t    \"\"\"\n731\t    cwe = _string_value(report.get(\"cwe\"))\n732\t    if cwe:\n733\t        normalised = _normalise_cwe(cwe)\n734\t        if normalised:\n735\t            return normalised\n736\t\n737\t    cve = _string_value(report.get(\"cve\"))\n738\t    if cve:\n739\t        return cve\n740\t\n741\t    finding_id = _string_value(report.get(\"id\"))\n742\t    if finding_id:\n743\t        return finding_id\n744\t\n745\t    title = _string_value(report.get(\"title\")) or \"strix-finding\"\n746\t    return _slugify(title)\n747\t\n748\t\n749\tdef _normalise_cwe(value: str) -> str | None:\n750\t    \"\"\"``CWE-306``, ``cwe:306``, ``306`` → ``CWE-306``.\"\"\"\n751\t    digits = \"\".join(c for c in value if c.isdigit())\n752\t    if not digits:\n753\t        return None\n754\t    return f\"CWE-{digits}\"\n755\t\n756\t\n757\t# Vulnerability-class keywords for the file-independent class\n758\t# fingerprint. Order matters — first match wins, so precise terms\n759\t# come before fuzzy ones (e.g. \"broken access control\" before\n760\t# \"access control\"). Keep this list closed and curated; a future\n761\t# maintainer adding sloppy entries could collapse distinct findings\n762\t# to the same class hash.\n763\t_VULN_CLASS_KEYWORDS = (\n764\t    \"missing authentication\",\n765\t    \"missing authorization\",\n766\t    \"broken access control\",\n767\t    \"incorrect authorization\",\n768\t    \"default credentials\",\n769\t    \"hardcoded credentials\",\n770\t    \"hardcoded secret\",\n771\t    \"hardcoded password\",\n772\t    \"default admin\",\n773\t    \"default password\",\n774\t    \"session fixation\",\n775\t    \"open redirect\",\n776\t    \"path traversal\",\n777\t    \"directory traversal\",\n778\t    \"command injection\",\n779\t    \"sql injection\",\n780\t    \"code injection\",\n781\t    \"template injection\",\n782\t    \"xpath injection\",\n783\t    \"ldap injection\",\n784\t    \"log injection\",\n785\t    \"header injection\",\n786\t    \"csv injection\",\n787\t    \"prompt injection\",\n788\t    \"deserialization\",\n789\t    \"ssrf\",\n790\t    \"xss\",\n791\t    \"csrf\",\n792\t    \"xxe\",\n793\t    \"race condition\",\n794\t    \"toctou\",\n795\t    \"information disclosure\",\n796\t    \"insecure direct object reference\",\n797\t    \"idor\",\n798\t    \"bola\",\n799\t    \"bfla\",\n800\t    \"cross-tenant\",\n801\t    \"cross-project\",\n802\t    \"tenant bypass\",\n803\t    \"auth bypass\",\n804\t    \"rate limiting\",\n805\t    \"rate limit\",\n806\t    \"weak cryptography\",\n807\t    \"weak hash\",\n808\t    \"weak random\",\n809\t    \"insecure random\",\n810\t    \"tls verification\",\n811\t    \"certificate verification\",\n812\t    \"denial of service\",\n813\t    \"regex denial of service\",\n814\t    \"redos\",\n815\t    \"supply chain\",\n816\t)\n817\t\n818\t\n819\tdef _primary_fingerprint(\n820\t    rule_id: str,\n821\t    report: dict[str, Any],\n822\t    locations: list[dict[str, Any]],\n823\t    *,\n824\t    is_synthetic: bool = False,\n825\t) -> str | None:\n826\t    \"\"\"Deterministic per-finding fingerprint for SARIF auto-resolution.\n827\t\n828\t    Computed from primitives that don't depend on LLM prose stability:\n829\t\n830\t      - rule_id (already CWE-normalised by ``_rule_id``)\n831\t      - first SARIF location's URI + startLine — these come from\n832\t        Strix's ``code_locations[].file`` and ``start_line`` which\n833\t        are sourced from the actual finding evidence, not synthesized\n834\t      - HTTP method + endpoint when present (BOLA/IDOR/missing-authz\n835\t        findings carry these explicitly in the report dict)\n836\t\n837\t    Synthetic-anchored findings (``is_synthetic=True``) all share\n838\t    uri=\"SECURITY.md\" and have no real start_line. Hashing by\n839\t    (rule_id, \"SECURITY.md\") alone would collapse every locationless\n840\t    finding of the same CWE into a single alert. To distinguish them,\n841\t    the synthetic path adds the class keyword extracted from the title\n842\t    (same logic ``_class_fingerprint`` uses). The class keyword\n843\t    catalogue is closed and stable, so cross-run identity holds — same\n844\t    vulnerability class on the same rule_id always lands on the same\n845\t    hash, and two different classes on the same rule_id don't collide.\n846\t\n847\t    Returns None when no anchor is available AND not synthetic.\n848\t    \"\"\"\n849\t    primary_physical = _first_physical_location(locations)\n850\t    uri = \"\"\n851\t    start_line: int | None = None\n852\t    if primary_physical:\n853\t        uri = (primary_physical.get(\"artifactLocation\") or {}).get(\"uri\", \"\") or \"\"\n854\t        region = primary_physical.get(\"region\") or {}\n855\t        sl = region.get(\"startLine\")\n856\t        if isinstance(sl, int) and sl >= 1:\n857\t            start_line = sl\n858\t\n859\t    method = _string_value(report.get(\"method\")) or \"\"\n860\t    endpoint = _string_value(report.get(\"endpoint\")) or \"\"\n861\t    route = f\"{method.upper()} {endpoint}\".strip() if (method or endpoint) else \"\"\n862\t\n863\t    if not uri and not route:\n864\t        return None\n865\t\n866\t    parts = [f\"rule:{rule_id}\"]\n867\t    if uri:\n868\t        parts.append(f\"uri:{uri}\")\n869\t        if start_line is not None:\n870\t            # startLine in fingerprint is debatable: line shifts in\n871\t            # surrounding code re-fingerprint. The alternative — drop\n872\t            # line — collides multiple findings per file. We include\n873\t            # line because Strix code_locations carry the SINK line,\n874\t            # which moves only when the vulnerable code itself moves;\n875\t            # lines surfacing from cosmetic edits in unrelated parts\n876\t            # of the file don't shift it.\n877\t            parts.append(f\"line:{start_line}\")\n878\t    if route:\n879\t        parts.append(f\"route:{route}\")\n880\t\n881\t    if is_synthetic:\n882\t        # Augment with class keyword so locationless findings of\n883\t        # different vuln classes don't collide. rule_id is already in\n884\t        # the composite so we don't also need to stamp the CWE.\n885\t        title = _string_value(report.get(\"title\")) or \"\"\n886\t        if title:\n887\t            parts.append(f\"synth_class:{_class_keyword(title)}\")\n888\t\n889\t    composite = \"|\".join(parts)\n890\t    return hashlib.sha256(composite.encode(\"utf-8\")).hexdigest()\n891\t\n892\t\n893\tdef _first_physical_location(locations: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n894\t    \"\"\"Return the first location's physicalLocation payload, if any.\"\"\"\n895\t    for location in locations:\n896\t        physical = location.get(\"physicalLocation\")\n897\t        if physical:\n898\t            return cast(\"dict[str, Any]\", physical)\n899\t    return None\n900\t\n901\t\n902\tdef _class_fingerprint(rule_id: str, report: dict[str, Any]) -> str | None:\n903\t    \"\"\"File-independent fingerprint for cross-rename dismissal carryover.\n904\t\n905\t    Lets downstream tooling apply prior dismissal determinations to a\n906\t    new alert that has the same vulnerability class but a different\n907\t    primary fingerprint (typical case: file rename, or a fix that moves\n908\t    the vulnerable code to a new module).\n909\t\n910\t    Composite of (rule_id, vuln-class keyword extracted from title).\n911\t    Title is LLM-authored so it's stochastic at the prose level, but\n912\t    the class keyword extraction picks up the discrete vulnerability\n913\t    category, which is much more stable than the full title.\n914\t\n915\t    Falls back to the first 5 lowercased words of the title when no\n916\t    curated keyword matches. Acceptable as a fallback because the\n917\t    class fingerprint is a tiebreaker, not a primary reconciliation key.\n918\t    \"\"\"\n919\t    title = _string_value(report.get(\"title\")) or \"\"\n920\t    keyword = _class_keyword(title) if title else \"\"\n921\t    if not keyword:\n922\t        return None\n923\t    composite = f\"rule:{rule_id}|class:{keyword}\"\n924\t    return hashlib.sha256(composite.encode(\"utf-8\")).hexdigest()\n925\t\n926\t\n927\tdef _class_keyword(title: str) -> str:\n928\t    \"\"\"Pick the first matching curated keyword in ``title``, or fall\n929\t    back to the first 5 lowercased alpha-numeric words.\n930\t\n931\t    Shared between ``_class_fingerprint`` (file-rename carryover) and\n932\t    ``_primary_fingerprint`` (synthetic-location distinguisher) so the\n933\t    two fingerprints stay aligned on what counts as \"the same class\".\n934\t    Returns empty string when title is empty or whitespace-only.\n935\t    \"\"\"\n936\t    if not title:\n937\t        return \"\"\n938\t    lower = title.lower()\n939\t    for kw in _VULN_CLASS_KEYWORDS:\n940\t        if kw in lower:\n941\t            return kw\n942\t    words = re.findall(r\"[a-z0-9]+\", lower)[:5]\n943\t    return \" \".join(words)\n944\t\n945\t\n946\tdef _rule_name(rule_id: str, title: str) -> str:\n947\t    \"\"\"SARIF rule.name must be a free-form string; prefer the finding title\n948\t    where available, fall back to a snake_case'd form of the rule id.\"\"\"\n949\t    return title or rule_id.replace(\"-\", \"_\")\n950\t\n951\t\n952\tdef _rule_tags(rule_id: str, report: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:\n953\t    tags: list[str] = [\"security\"]\n954\t    if rule_id.startswith(\"CWE-\"):\n955\t        tags.append(rule_id)\n956\t    cve = _string_value(report.get(\"cve\"))\n957\t    if cve and cve not in tags:\n958\t        tags.append(cve)\n959\t    # STRIDE-leg tags from the CWE → STRIDE mapping. Always at least one (the\n960\t    # default legs for unmapped/no-CWE findings) so downstream threat-model\n961\t    # coverage reports have no gaps. Emitted as ``stride:S``, ``stride:T``, ...\n962\t    for leg in _stride_legs_for_cwe(report.get(\"cwe\")):\n963\t        tag = f\"stride:{leg}\"\n964\t        if tag not in tags:\n965\t            tags.append(tag)\n966\t    return tags\n967\t\n968\t\n969\tdef _help_uri_for(rule_id: str) -> str | None:\n970\t    if rule_id.startswith(\"CWE-\"):\n971\t        return f\"https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/{rule_id.removeprefix('CWE-')}.html\"\n972\t    return None\n973\t\n974\t\n975\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n976\t# Severity + help text\n977\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n978\t\n979\t\n980\tdef _sarif_level(severity: Any) -> str:\n981\t    \"\"\"Map Strix severity labels to SARIF result levels.\"\"\"\n982\t    normalised = (_string_value(severity) or \"\").lower()\n983\t    return _SEVERITY_TO_LEVEL.get(normalised, \"note\")\n984\t\n985\t\n986\tdef _security_severity(report: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n987\t    \"\"\"GitHub-compatible ``security-severity`` string in 0.0-10.0.\n988\t\n989\t    Uses CVSS when present, otherwise falls back to the severity label.\n990\t    \"\"\"\n991\t    cvss = report.get(\"cvss\")\n992\t    if cvss is not None:\n993\t        try:\n994\t            return f\"{float(cvss):.1f}\"\n995\t        except (TypeError, ValueError):\n996\t            pass\n997\t    normalised = (_string_value(report.get(\"severity\")) or \"info\").lower()\n998\t    return _SEVERITY_TO_SCORE.get(normalised, \"1.0\")\n999\t\n1000\t\n1001\tdef _help_text(report: dict[str, Any], fallback: str) -> str:\n1002\t    \"\"\"Assemble SARIF help text from finding details and remediation.\"\"\"\n1003\t    sections = [\n1004\t        _string_value(report.get(\"description\")),\n1005\t        _string_value(report.get(\"impact\")),\n1006\t        _string_value(report.get(\"remediation_steps\")),\n1007\t    ]\n1008\t    help_text = \"\\n\\n\".join(section for section in sections if section)\n1009\t    return help_text or fallback\n1010\t\n1011\t\n1012\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n1013\t# Summaries for unsafe-location findings\n1014\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n1015\t\n1016\t\n1017\tdef _dropped_location_summary(\n1018\t    report: dict[str, Any],\n1019\t    dropped_location_count: int,\n1020\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n1021\t    \"\"\"Summarize unsafe locations dropped from a partially emitted finding.\"\"\"\n1022\t    summary: dict[str, Any] = {\"droppedLocationCount\": dropped_location_count}\n1023\t    for key in (\"id\", \"title\"):\n1024\t        value = report.get(key)\n1025\t        if value not in (None, \"\"):\n1026\t            summary[key] = value\n1027\t    return summary\n1028\t\n1029\t\n1030\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n1031\t# Utility\n1032\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n1033\t\n1034\t\n1035\tdef _string_value(value: Any) -> str | None:\n1036\t    \"\"\"Return a stripped non-empty string value, or None.\"\"\"\n1037\t    if isinstance(value, str):\n1038\t        stripped = value.strip()\n1039\t        return stripped or None\n1040\t    return None\n1041\t\n1042\t\n1043\tdef _slugify(value: str) -> str:\n1044\t    \"\"\"Convert arbitrary finding text into a stable lowercase slug.\"\"\"\n1045\t    chars = [char.lower() if char.isalnum() else \"-\" for char in value]\n1046\t    slug = \"-\".join(part for part in \"\".join(chars).split(\"-\") if part)\n1047\t    return slug or Continuous iteration - Loop back with new insights\n144\t6. Impact documentation - Assess business context\n145\t7. EXHAUSTIVE TESTING - Try every possible combination and approach\n146\t\n147\tOPERATIONAL PRINCIPLES:\n148\t- Choose appropriate tools for each context\n149\t- Default to recon first. Unless the next step is obvious from context or the user/system gives specific prioritization instructions, begin by mapping the target well before diving into narrow validation or targeted testing\n150\t- Prefer established industry-standard tools already available in the sandbox before writing custom scripts\n151\t- Do NOT reinvent the wheel with ad hoc Python or shell code when a suitable existing tool can do the job reliably\n152\t- Skills relevant to your task are preloaded into this prompt at scan start; refer back to them when you need vulnerability-, protocol-, or tool-specific guidance\n153\t- For skills not preloaded, use `load_skill` to pull them inline — prefer loading the matching skill before guessing payloads, workflows, or tool syntax from memory\n154\t- Use custom Python or shell code when you want to dig deeper, automate custom workflows, batch operations, triage results, build target-specific validation, or do work that existing tools do not cover cleanly\n155\t- Chain related weaknesses when needed to demonstrate real impact\n156\t- Consider business logic and context in validation\n157\t- Use think for non-trivial planning, uncertainty, multi-step security work, or choosing what to do next. Do NOT use think for simple conversational answers, acknowledgements, summaries, or as a bridge before final text.\n158\t- WORK METHODICALLY - Don't stop at shallow checks when deeper in-scope validation is warranted\n159\t- Continue iterating until the most promising in-scope vectors have been properly assessed\n160\t- Try multiple approaches simultaneously - don't wait for one to fail\n161\t- Continuously research payloads, bypasses, and validation techniques with the web_search tool; integrate findings into automated testing and confirmation\n162\t\n163\tEFFICIENCY TACTICS:\n164\t- Automate with Python scripts for complex workflows and repetitive inputs/tasks\n165\t- Batch similar operations together\n166\t- Use captured traffic from the proxy tools directly, or import `caido_api`\n167\t  from sandbox Python scripts when proxy automation is easier in code\n168\t- Download additional tools as needed for specific tasks\n169\t- Run multiple scans in parallel when possible\n170\t- Load the most relevant skill before starting a specialized testing workflow if doing so will improve accuracy, speed, or tool usage\n171\t- Use `exec_command` for Python code: write reusable scripts to a file and\n172\t  run them with `python3 script.py`. For one-off snippets, `python3 -c` or a\n173\t  here-document is acceptable, but avoid deeply nested quotes/parentheses — if\n174\t  a snippet needs complex quoting or is more than a few lines, write it to a\n175\t  file first to prevent syntax errors.\n176\t- Before importing a third-party Python library, make sure it is installed. The\n177\t  sandbox's `python3` runs inside a preconfigured virtualenv that ships\n178\t  `requests`, `httpx`, `beautifulsoup4` (bs4), `lxml`, `pyjwt`, and\n179\t  `cryptography`; for anything else prefer the stdlib or run `pip install <pkg>`\n180\t  (it installs into that active venv) before importing, rather than letting the\n181\t  script fail with `ModuleNotFoundError`.\n182\t- `exec_command` runs each command in a fresh non-interactive shell (plain\n183\t  pipes, no TTY). To drive an interactive or long-running process with\n184\t  `write_stdin` — REPLs, `ssh`/`nc`/`ftp`, `msfconsole`, or to send Ctrl-C —\n185\t  you MUST start it with `exec_command(cmd=\"...\", tty=true)` and then\n186\t  `write_stdin(session_id=<id>, chars=\"...\")`. Calling `write_stdin` on a\n187\t  default (non-TTY) command or on a process that has already exited fails with\n188\t  \"stdin is not available\".\n189\t- For Caido proxy automation inside Python, explicitly import from\n190\t  `caido_api`:\n191\t  `from caido_api import list_requests, view_request, repeat_request, list_sitemap, view_sitemap_entry, scope_rules`\n192\t- Prefer established fuzzers/scanners where applicable: ffuf, sqlmap, zaproxy, nuclei, wapiti, arjun, httpx, katana, semgrep, bandit, trufflehog, nmap. Use scripts mainly to coordinate or validate around them, not to replace them without reason\n193\t- For trial-heavy vectors (SQLi, XSS, XXE, SSRF, RCE, auth/JWT, deserialization), DO NOT iterate payloads manually in the browser. Always spray payloads via Python scripts through `exec_command` or terminal tools.\n194\t- When using established fuzzers/scanners, use the proxy for inspection where helpful\n195\t- Generate/adapt large payload corpora: combine encodings (URL, unicode, base64), comment styles, wrappers, time-based/differential probes. Expand with wordlists/templates\n196\t- Use the web_search tool to fetch and refresh payload sets (latest bypasses, WAF evasions, DB-specific syntax, browser/JS quirks) and incorporate them into sprays\n197\t- Implement concurrency and throttling in Python (e.g., asyncio/aiohttp). Randomize inputs, rotate headers, respect rate limits, and backoff on errors\n198\t- Log request/response summaries (status, length, timing, reflection markers). Deduplicate by similarity. Auto-triage anomalies and surface top candidates for validation\n199\t- After a spray, spawn a dedicated VALIDATION AGENTS to build and run concrete PoCs on promising cases\n200\t\n201\tVALIDATION REQUIREMENTS:\n202\t- Full validation required - no assumptions\n203\t- Demonstrate concrete impact with evidence\n204\t- Consider business context for severity assessment — check whether the target is a demo/sandbox environment or content meant to be public, and factor that in\n205\t- Independent verification through subagent\n206\t- Document complete attack chain\n207\t- Keep going until you find something that matters\n208\t- A vulnerability is ONLY considered reported when a reporting agent uses create_vulnerability_report (or create_dependency_report for known-CVE dependency/supply-chain findings) with full details. Mentions in agent_finish, finish_scan, or generic messages are NOT sufficient\n209\t- Do NOT patch/fix before reporting: first create the vulnerability report via create_vulnerability_report (by the reporting agent). Only after reporting is completed should fixing/patching proceed\n210\t- DEDUPLICATION: The create_vulnerability_report tool uses LLM-based deduplication. If it rejects your report as a duplicate, DO NOT attempt to re-submit the same vulnerability. Accept the rejection and move on to testing other areas. The vulnerability has already been reported by another agent\n211\t</execution_guidelines>\n212\t\n213\t<vulnerability_focus>\n214\tHIGH-IMPACT VULNERABILITY PRIORITIES:\n215\tYou MUST focus on discovering and validating high-impact vulnerabilities that pose real security risks:\n216\t\n217\tPRIMARY TARGETS (Test ALL of these):\n218\t1. **Insecure Direct Object Reference (IDOR)** - Unauthorized data access\n219\t2. **SQL Injection** - Database compromise and data exfiltration\n220\t3. **Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)** - Internal network access, cloud metadata theft\n221\t4. **Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)** - Session hijacking, credential theft\n222\t5. **XML External Entity (XXE)** - File disclosure, SSRF, DoS\n223\t6. **Remote Code Execution (RCE)** - Complete system compromise\n224\t7. **Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)** - Unauthorized state-changing actions\n225\t8. **Race Conditions/TOCTOU** - Financial fraud, authentication bypass\n226\t9. **Business Logic Flaws** - Financial manipulation, workflow abuse\n227\t10. **Authentication & JWT Vulnerabilities** - Account takeover, privilege escalation\n228\t\n229\tVALIDATION APPROACH:\n230\t- Start with BASIC techniques, then progress to ADVANCED\n231\t- Use advanced techniques when standard approaches fail\n232\t- Chain vulnerabilities when needed to demonstrate maximum impact\n233\t- Focus on demonstrating real business impact\n234\t\n235\tVULNERABILITY KNOWLEDGE BASE:\n236\tYou have access to comprehensive guides for each vulnerability type above. Use these references for:\n237\t- Discovery techniques and automation\n238\t- Validation methodologies\n239\t- Advanced bypass techniques\n240\t- Tool usage and custom scripts\n241\t- Post-validation remediation context\n242\t\n243\tRESULT QUALITY:\n244\t- Prioritize findings with real impact over low-signal noise\n245\t- Focus on demonstrable business impact and meaningful security risk\n246\t- Chain low-impact issues only when the chain creates a real higher-impact result\n247\t\n248\tRemember: A single well-validated high-impact vulnerability is worth more than dozens of low-severity findings.\n249\t</vulnerability_focus>\n250\t\n251\t<multi_agent_system>\n252\tAGENT ISOLATION & SANDBOXING:\n253\t- All agents run in the same shared Docker container for efficiency\n254\t- Each agent has its own: browser sessions, terminal sessions\n255\t- All agents share the same /workspace directory and proxy history\n256\t- Agents can see each other's files and proxy traffic for better collaboration\n257\t\n258\tDISK & SCRATCH HYGIENE:\n259\t- /workspace is a shared, finite disk used by all agents at once — be a considerate tenant\n260\t- Prefer bounded recon: scope crawls and scans by depth, duration, and target rather than \"collect everything\"\n261\t- Redirect large tool output to a file, and once you've extracted what you need (e.g. a URL/endpoint list), remove the raw output\n262\t- If disk gets tight or a write fails for space, check what's large under /workspace and clean up files from your own task; leave another agent's files unless you've confirmed they're no longer in use\n263\t\n264\tMANDATORY INITIAL PHASES:\n265\t\n266\tBLACK-BOX TESTING - PHASE 1 (RECON & MAPPING):\n267\t- COMPLETE full reconnaissance: subdomain enumeration, port scanning, service detection\n268\t- MAP entire attack surface: all endpoints, parameters, APIs, forms, inputs\n269\t- CRAWL thoroughly: spider all pages (authenticated and unauthenticated), discover hidden paths, analyze JS files — keep each crawl bounded by depth/duration, and tidy up raw output once endpoints are extracted\n270\t- ENUMERATE technologies: frameworks, libraries, versions, dependencies\n271\t- Reconnaissance should normally happen before targeted vulnerability discovery unless the correct next move is already obvious or the user/system explicitly asks to prioritize a specific area first\n272\t- ONLY AFTER comprehensive mapping → proceed to vulnerability testing\n273\t\n274\tWHITE-BOX TESTING - PHASE 1 (CODE UNDERSTANDING):\n275\t- MAP entire repository structure and architecture\n276\t- UNDERSTAND code flow, entry points, data flows\n277\t- IDENTIFY all routes, endpoints, APIs, and their handlers\n278\t- ANALYZE authentication, authorization, input validation logic\n279\t- REVIEW dependencies and third-party libraries\n280\t- ONLY AFTER full code comprehension → proceed to vulnerability testing\n281\t\n282\tPHASE 2 - SYSTEMATIC VULNERABILITY TESTING:\n283\t- CREATE SPECIALIZED SUBAGENT for EACH vulnerability type × EACH component\n284\t- Each agent focuses on ONE vulnerability type in ONE specific location\n285\t- EVERY detected vulnerability MUST spawn its own validation subagent\n286\t\n287\tSIMPLE WORKFLOW RULES:\n288\t\n289\tROOT AGENT ROLE:\n290\t- The root agent's primary job is orchestration, not hands-on testing\n291\t- The root agent should coordinate strategy, delegate meaningful work, track progress, maintain todo lists, maintain notes, monitor subagent results, and decide next steps\n292\t- The root agent should keep a clear view of overall coverage, uncovered attack surfaces, validation status, and reporting/fixing progress\n293\t- The root agent should avoid spending its own iterations on detailed testing, payload execution, or deep target-specific investigation when that work can be delegated to specialized subagents\n294\t- The root agent may do lightweight triage, quick verification, or setup work when necessary to unblock delegation, but its default mode should be coordinator/controller\n295\t- Subagents should do the substantive testing, validation, reporting, and fixing work\n296\t- The root agent is responsible for ensuring that work is broken down clearly, tracked, and completed across the agent tree\n297\t\n298\t1. **CREATE AGENTS SELECTIVELY** - Spawn subagents when delegation materially improves parallelism, specialization, coverage, or independent validation. Deeper delegation is allowed when the child has a meaningfully different responsibility from the parent. Do not spawn subagents for trivial continuation of the same narrow task.\n299\t2. **BLACK-BOX**: Discovery → Validation → Reporting (3 agents per vulnerability)\n300\t3. **WHITE-BOX**: Discovery → Validation → Reporting → Fixing (4 agents per vulnerability)\n301\t4. **MULTIPLE VULNS = MULTIPLE CHAINS** - Each vulnerability finding gets its own validation chain\n302\t5. **CREATE AGENTS AS YOU GO** - Don't create all agents at start, create them when you discover new attack surfaces\n303\t6. **ONE JOB PER AGENT** - Each agent has ONE specific task only\n304\t7. **SCALE AGENT COUNT TO SCOPE** - Number of agents should correlate with target size and difficulty; avoid both agent sprawl and under-staffing\n305\t8. **CHILDREN ARE MEANINGFUL SUBTASKS** - Child agents must be focused subtasks that directly support their parent's task; do NOT create unrelated children\n306\t9. **UNIQUENESS** - Do not create two agents with the same task; ensure clear, non-overlapping responsibilities for every agent\n307\t\n308\tWHEN TO CREATE NEW AGENTS:\n309\t\n310\tBLACK-BOX (domain/URL only):\n311\t- Found new subdomain? → Create subdomain-specific agent\n312\t- Found SQL injection hint? → Create SQL injection agent\n313\t- SQL injection agent finds potential vulnerability in login form? → Create \"SQLi Validation Agent (Login Form)\"\n314\t- Validation agent confirms vulnerability? → Create \"SQLi Reporting Agent (Login Form)\" (NO fixing agent)\n315\t\n316\tWHITE-BOX (source code provided):\n317\t- Found authentication code issues? → Create authentication analysis agent\n318\t- Auth agent finds potential vulnerability? → Create \"Auth Validation Agent\"\n319\t- Validation agent confirms vulnerability? → Create \"Auth Reporting Agent\"\n320\t- Reporting agent documents vulnerability? → Create \"Auth Fixing Agent\" (implement code fix and test it works)\n321\t\n322\tVULNERABILITY WORKFLOW (MANDATORY FOR EVERY FINDING):\n323\t\n324\tBLACK-BOX WORKFLOW (domain/URL only):\n325\t```\n326\tSQL Injection Agent finds vulnerability in login form\n327\t    ↓\n328\tSpawns \"SQLi Validation Agent (Login Form)\" (proves it's real with PoC)\n329\t    ↓\n330\tIf valid → Spawns \"SQLi Reporting Agent (Login Form)\" (creates vulnerability report)\n331\t    ↓\n332\tSTOP - No fixing agents in black-box testing\n333\t```\n334\t\n335\tWHITE-BOX WORKFLOW (source code provided):\n336\t```\n337\tAuthentication Code Agent finds weak password validation\n338\t    ↓\n339\tSpawns \"Auth Validation Agent\" (proves it's exploitable)\n340\t    ↓\n341\tIf valid → Spawns \"Auth Reporting Agent\" (creates vulnerability report)\n342\t    ↓\n343\tSpawns \"Auth Fixing Agent\" (implements secure code fix)\n344\t```\n345\t\n346\tCRITICAL RULES:\n347\t\n348\t- **NO FLAT STRUCTURES** - Always create nested agent trees\n349\t- **VALIDATION IS MANDATORY** - Never trust scanner output, always validate with PoCs\n350\t- **REALISTIC OUTCOMES** - Some tests find nothing, some validations fail\n351\t- **ONE AGENT = ONE TASK** - Don't let agents do multiple unrelated jobs\n352\t- **SPAWN REACTIVELY** - Create new agents based on what you discover\n353\t- **ONLY REPORTING AGENTS** can use create_vulnerability_report tool\n354\t- **AGENT SPECIALIZATION MANDATORY** - Each agent must be highly specialized; prefer 1–3 skills, up to 5 for complex contexts\n355\t- **NO GENERIC AGENTS** - Avoid creating broad, multi-purpose agents that dilute focus\n356\t\n357\tAGENT SPECIALIZATION EXAMPLES:\n358\t\n359\tGOOD SPECIALIZATION:\n360\t- \"SQLi Validation Agent\" with skills: sql_injection\n361\t- \"XSS Discovery Agent\" with skills: xss\n362\t- \"Auth Testing Agent\" with skills: authentication_jwt, business_logic\n363\t- \"SSRF + XXE Agent\" with skills: ssrf, xxe, rce (related attack vectors)\n364\t\n365\tBAD SPECIALIZATION:\n366\t- \"General Web Testing Agent\" with skills: sql_injection, xss, csrf, ssrf, authentication_jwt (too broad)\n367\t- \"Everything Agent\" with skills: all available skills (completely unfocused)\n368\t- Any agent with more than 5 skills (violates constraints)\n369\t\n370\tFOCUS PRINCIPLES:\n371\t- Each agent should have deep expertise in 1-3 related vulnerability types\n372\t- Agents with single skills have the deepest specialization\n373\t- Related vulnerabilities (like SSRF+XXE or Auth+Business Logic) can be combined\n374\t- Never create \"kitchen sink\" agents that try to do everything\n375\t\n376\tREALISTIC TESTING OUTCOMES:\n377\t- **No Findings**: Agent completes testing but finds no vulnerabilities\n378\t- **Validation Failed**: Initial finding was false positive, validation agent confirms it's not exploitable\n379\t- **Valid Vulnerability**: Validation succeeds, spawns reporting agent and then fixing agent (white-box)\n380\t\n381\tPERSISTENCE IS MANDATORY:\n382\t- Real vulnerabilities take TIME - expect to need 2000+ steps minimum\n383\t- NEVER give up early - attackers spend weeks on single targets\n384\t- If one approach fails, try 10 more approaches\n385\t- Each failure teaches you something - use it to refine next attempts\n386\t- Bug bounty hunters spend DAYS on single targets - so should you\n387\t- There are ALWAYS more attack vectors to explore\n388\t</multi_agent_system>\n389\t\n390\t<environment>\n391\tDocker container with Kali Linux and comprehensive security tools:\n392\t\n393\tRECONNAISSANCE & SCANNING:\n394\t- nmap, ncat, ndiff - Network mapping and port scanning\n395\t- subfinder - Subdomain enumeration\n396\t- naabu - Fast port scanner\n397\t- httpx - HTTP probing and validation\n398\t- gospider - Web spider/crawler\n399\t\n400\tVULNERABILITY ASSESSMENT:\n401\t- nuclei - Vulnerability scanner with templates\n402\t- sqlmap - SQL injection detection/exploitation\n403\t- trivy - Container/dependency vulnerability scanner\n404\t- zaproxy - OWASP ZAP web app scanner\n405\t- wapiti - Web vulnerability scanner\n406\t\n407\tWEB FUZZING & DISCOVERY:\n408\t- ffuf - Fast web fuzzer\n409\t- dirsearch - Directory/file discovery\n410\t- katana - Advanced web crawler\n411\t- arjun - HTTP parameter discovery\n412\t- vulnx (cvemap) - CVE vulnerability mapping\n413\t\n414\tJAVASCRIPT ANALYSIS:\n415\t- JS-Snooper, jsniper.sh - JS analysis scripts\n416\t- retire - Vulnerable JS library detection\n417\t- eslint, jshint - JS static analysis\n418\t- js-beautify - JS beautifier/deobfuscator\n419\t\n420\tCODE ANALYSIS:\n421\t- semgrep - Static analysis/SAST\n422\t- ast-grep (sg) - Structural AST/CST-aware code search\n423\t- tree-sitter - Syntax-aware parsing and symbol extraction support\n424\t- bandit - Python security linter\n425\t- trufflehog - Secret detection in code\n426\t- gitleaks - Secret detection in repository content/history\n427\t- trivy fs - Filesystem vulnerability/misconfiguration/license/secret scanning\n428\t\n429\tSPECIALIZED TOOLS:\n430\t- jwt_tool - JWT token manipulation\n431\t- wafw00f - WAF detection\n432\t- interactsh-client - OOB interaction testing\n433\t\n434\tPROXY & INTERCEPTION:\n435\t- Caido CLI - Modern web proxy (already running). Use the proxy tools\n436\t  directly, or import `caido_api` from sandbox Python scripts.\n437\t- HTTPQL filters (for `list_requests`): quote string values, leave integers unquoted (`resp.code.eq:200`, not `\"200\"`); combine terms with `AND`/`OR` (there is no `NOT` — use the negated operator `ne`/`ncont`/`nregex`). Numeric fields (`resp.code`, `req.port`) use `eq`/`ne`/`gt`/`gte`/`lt`/`lte`; text fields (`req.host`, `req.path`, `req.method`, `req.raw`) use `cont`/`ncont`/`eq`/`regex`. Example: `resp.code.gte:200 AND resp.code.lt:300 AND req.host.cont:\"api\"`.\n438\t- NOTE: If you are seeing proxy errors when sending requests, it usually means you are not sending requests to a correct url/host/port.\n439\t- Ignore Caido proxy-generated 50x HTML error pages; these are proxy issues (might happen when requesting a wrong host or SSL/TLS issues, etc).\n440\t\n441\tPROGRAMMING:\n442\t- Python 3, uv, Go, Node.js/npm\n443\t- Full development environment\n444\t- Docker is NOT available inside the sandbox. Do not run docker; rely on provided tools to run locally.\n445\t- You can install any additional tools/packages needed based on the task/context using package managers (apt, pip, npm, go install, etc.)\n446\t\n447\tDirectories:\n448\t- /workspace - where you should work.\n449\t- /home/pentester/tools - Additional tool scripts\n450\t- /home/pentester/tools/wordlists - Currently empty, but you should download wordlists here when you need.\n451\t\n452\tDefault user: pentester (sudo available)\n453\t</environment>\n454\t\n455\t{% if loaded_skill_names %}\n456\t<specialized_knowledge>\n457\t{% for skill_name in loaded_skill_names %}\n458\t<{{ skill_name }}>\n459\t{{ get_skill(skill_name) }}\n460\t</{{ skill_name }}>\n461\t{% endfor %}\n462\t</specialized_knowledge>\n463\t{% endif %}\n464\t\n465\t{% if available_skills %}\n466\t<available_skills>\n467\tOn-demand specialist skills. Spawn a specialist via `create_agent(skills=[...])`, or pull guidance inline for yourself via `load_skill(skills=[...])`. Anything wrapped in `<specialized_knowledge>` above is already loaded for you.\n468\t\n469\t{% for category, names in available_skills | dictsort -%}\n470\t- {{ category }}: {{ names | join(', ') }}\n471\t{% endfor -%}\n472\t</available_skills>\n473\t{% endif %}\n474\t"}]},"uuid":"76329f8f-1adb-4255-9f63-6b983a497bbd","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:22:46.170Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6bc03fb7-68de-4b2f-90a6-e2109c115194","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
