{"parentUuid":"31525b19-5fa0-478d-bb7f-bb0265831580","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PQFBK3BKdKpW3N6rLfJQXT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: ssrf\n3\tdescription: SSRF testing for cloud metadata access, internal service discovery, and protocol smuggling\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# SSRF\n7\t\n8\tServer-Side Request Forgery enables the server to reach networks and services the attacker cannot. Focus on cloud metadata endpoints, service meshes, Kubernetes, and protocol abuse to turn a single fetch into credentials, lateral movement, and sometimes RCE.\n9\t\n10\t## Attack Surface\n11\t\n12\t**Scope**\n13\t- Outbound HTTP/HTTPS fetchers (proxies, previewers, importers, webhook testers)\n14\t- Non-HTTP protocols via URL handlers (gopher, dict, file, ftp, smb wrappers)\n15\t- Service-to-service hops through gateways and sidecars (envoy/nginx)\n16\t- Cloud and platform metadata endpoints, instance services, and control planes\n17\t\n18\t**Direct URL Params**\n19\t- `url=`, `link=`, `fetch=`, `src=`, `webhook=`, `avatar=`, `image=`\n20\t\n21\t**Indirect Sources**\n22\t- Open Graph/link previews, PDF/image renderers\n23\t- Server-side analytics (Referer trackers), import/export jobs\n24\t- Webhooks/callback verifiers\n25\t\n26\t**Protocol-Translating Services**\n27\t- PDF via wkhtmltopdf/Chrome headless, image pipelines\n28\t- Document parsers, SSO validators, archive expanders\n29\t\n30\t**Less Obvious**\n31\t- GraphQL resolvers that fetch by URL\n32\t- Background crawlers, repository/package managers (git, npm, pip)\n33\t- Calendar (ICS) fetchers\n34\t\n35\t## High-Value Targets\n36\t\n37\t### AWS\n38\t\n39\t- IMDSv1: `http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/` → `/iam/security-credentials/{role}`, `/user-data`\n40\t- IMDSv2: requires token via PUT `/latest/api/token` with header `X-aws-ec2-metadata-token-ttl-seconds`, then include `X-aws-ec2-metadata-token` on subsequent GETs\n41\t- If sink cannot set headers or methods, seek intermediaries that can\n42\t- ECS/EKS task credentials: `http://169.254.170.2$AWS_CONTAINER_CREDENTIALS_RELATIVE_URI`\n43\t\n44\t### GCP\n45\t\n46\t- Endpoint: `http://metadata.google.internal/computeMetadata/v1/`\n47\t- Required header: `Metadata-Flavor: Google`\n48\t- Target: `/instance/service-accounts/default/token`\n49\t\n50\t### Azure\n51\t\n52\t- Endpoint: `http://169.254.169.254/metadata/instance?api-version=2021-02-01`\n53\t- Required header: `Metadata: true`\n54\t- MSI OAuth: `/metadata/identity/oauth2/token`\n55\t\n56\t### Kubernetes\n57\t\n58\t- Kubelet: 10250 (authenticated) and 10255 (deprecated read-only)\n59\t- Probe `/pods`, `/metrics`, exec/attach endpoints\n60\t- API server: `https://kubernetes.default.svc/`\n61\t- Authorization often needs service account token; SSRF that propagates headers/cookies may reuse them\n62\t- Service discovery: attempt cluster DNS names (`svc.cluster.local`) and default services (kube-dns, metrics-server)\n63\t\n64\t### Internal Services\n65\t\n66\t- Docker API: `http://localhost:2375/v1.24/containers/json` (no TLS variants often internal-only)\n67\t- Redis/Memcached: `dict://localhost:11211/stat`, gopher payloads to Redis on 6379\n68\t- Elasticsearch/OpenSearch: `http://localhost:9200/_cat/indices`\n69\t- Message brokers/admin UIs: RabbitMQ, Kafka REST, Celery/Flower, Jenkins crumb APIs\n70\t- FastCGI/PHP-FPM: `gopher://localhost:9000/` (craft records for file write/exec when app routes to FPM)\n71\t\n72\t## Key Vulnerabilities\n73\t\n74\t### Protocol Exploitation\n75\t\n76\t**Gopher**\n77\t- Speak raw text protocols (Redis/SMTP/IMAP/HTTP/FCGI)\n78\t- Use to craft multi-line payloads, schedule cron via Redis, or build FastCGI requests\n79\t\n80\t**File and Wrappers**\n81\t- `file:///etc/passwd`, `file:///proc/self/environ` when libraries allow file handlers\n82\t- `jar:`, `netdoc:`, `smb://` and language-specific wrappers (`php://`, `expect://`) where enabled\n83\t\n84\t### Address Variants\n85\t\n86\t- Loopback: `127.0.0.1`, `127.1`, `2130706433`, `0x7f000001`, `::1`, `[::ffff:127.0.0.1]`\n87\t- RFC1918/link-local: 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, 169.254/16\n88\t- Test IPv6-mapped and mixed-notation forms\n89\t\n90\t### URL Confusion\n91\t\n92\t- Userinfo and fragments: `http://internal@attacker/` or `http://attacker#@internal/`\n93\t- Scheme-less/relative forms the server might complete internally: `//169.254.169.254/`\n94\t- Trailing dots and mixed case: `internal.` vs `INTERNAL`, Unicode dot lookalikes\n95\t\n96\t### Redirect Abuse\n97\t\n98\t- Allowlist only applied pre-redirect: 302 from attacker → internal host\n99\t- Test multi-hop and protocol switches (http→file/gopher via custom clients)\n100\t\n101\t### Header and Method Control\n102\t\n103\t- Some sinks reflect or allow CRLF-injection into the request line/headers\n104\t- If arbitrary headers/methods are possible, IMDSv2, GCP, and Azure become reachable\n105\t\n106\t## Bypass Techniques\n107\t\n108\t**Address Encoding**\n109\t- Decimal, hex, octal representations of IP addresses\n110\t- IPv6 variants, IPv4-mapped IPv6, mixed notation\n111\t\n112\t**DNS Rebinding**\n113\t- First resolution returns allowed IP, second returns internal target\n114\t- Use short TTL DNS records under attacker control\n115\t\n116\t**URL Parser Differentials**\n117\t- Different parsing between allowlist checker and actual fetcher\n118\t- Exploit inconsistencies in scheme, host, port, path handling\n119\t\n120\t**Redirect Chains**\n121\t- Initial URL passes allowlist, redirect targets internal host\n122\t- Protocol downgrade/upgrade through redirects\n123\t\n124\t## Blind SSRF\n125\t\n126\t- Use OAST (DNS/HTTP) to confirm egress. `interactsh-client -v` (running\n127\t  in the sandbox) gives you a unique `*.oast.fun` domain; embed it in\n128\t  the URL parameter and watch the interactsh stdout for the inbound\n129\t  DNS/HTTP hit. Each invocation yields a fresh domain — restart between\n130\t  payloads if you need to correlate hits to a specific request.\n131\t- Derive internal reachability from timing, response size, TLS errors, and ETag differences\n132\t- Build a port map by binary searching timeouts (short connect/read timeouts yield cleaner diffs)\n133\t\n134\t## Chaining Attacks\n135\t\n136\t- SSRF → Metadata creds → cloud API access (list buckets, read secrets)\n137\t- SSRF → Redis/FCGI/Docker → file write/command execution → shell\n138\t- SSRF → Kubelet/API → pod list/logs → token/secret discovery → lateral movement\n139\t\n140\t## Testing Methodology\n141\t\n142\t1. **Identify surfaces** - Every user-influenced URL/host/path across web/mobile/API and background jobs\n143\t2. **Establish oracle** - Quiet OAST DNS/HTTP callbacks first\n144\t3. **Internal addressing** - Pivot to loopback, RFC1918, link-local, IPv6, hostnames\n145\t4. **Protocol variations** - Test gopher, file, dict where supported\n146\t5. **Parser differentials** - Test across frameworks, CDNs, and language libraries\n147\t6. **Redirect behavior** - Single-hop, multi-hop, protocol switches\n148\t7. **Header/method control** - Can you influence request headers or HTTP method?\n149\t8. **High-value targets** - Metadata, kubelet, Redis, FastCGI, Docker, Vault, internal admin panels\n150\t\n151\t## Validation\n152\t\n153\t1. Prove an outbound server-initiated request occurred (OAST interaction or internal-only response differences)\n154\t2. Show access to non-public resources (metadata, internal admin, service ports) from the vulnerable service\n155\t3. Where possible, demonstrate minimal-impact credential access (short-lived token) or a harmless internal data read\n156\t4. Confirm reproducibility and document request parameters that control scheme/host/headers/method and redirect behavior\n157\t\n158\t## False Positives\n159\t\n160\t- Client-side fetches only (no server request)\n161\t- Strict allowlists with DNS pinning and no redirect following\n162\t- SSRF simulators/mocks returning canned responses without real egress\n163\t- Blocked egress confirmed by uniform errors across all targets and protocols\n164\t- OAST callbacks where the source IP matches the tester's machine, not the server — the browser or a client-side fetch made the request, not the backend\n165\t\n166\t## Impact\n167\t\n168\t- Cloud credential disclosure with subsequent control-plane/API access\n169\t- Access to internal control panels and data stores not exposed publicly\n170\t- Lateral movement into Kubernetes, service meshes, and CI/CD\n171\t- RCE via protocol abuse (FCGI, Redis), Docker daemon access, or scriptable admin interfaces\n172\t\n173\t## Pro Tips\n174\t\n175\t1. Prefer OAST callbacks first; then iterate on internal addressing and protocols\n176\t2. Test IPv6 and mixed-notation addresses; filters often ignore them\n177\t3. Observe library/client differences (curl, Java HttpClient, Node, Go); behavior changes across services and jobs\n178\t4. Redirects are leverage: control both the initial allowlisted host and the next hop\n179\t5. Metadata endpoints require headers/methods; verify if your sink can set them or if intermediaries add them\n180\t6. Use tiny payloads and tight timeouts to map ports with minimal noise\n181\t7. When responses are masked, diff length/ETag/status and TLS error classes to infer reachability\n182\t8. Chain quickly to durable impact (short-lived tokens, harmless internal reads) and stop there\n183\t\n184\t## Summary\n185\t\n186\tAny feature that fetches remote content on behalf of a user is a potential tunnel to internal networks and control planes. Bind scheme/host/port/headers explicitly or expect an attacker to route through them.\n187\t"}]},"uuid":"4f6f57c7-0bc7-4298-8208-f15a5c7d7374","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:22:56.394Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"31525b19-5fa0-478d-bb7f-bb0265831580","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"4f6f57c7-0bc7-4298-8208-f15a5c7d7374","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdCS9YFoH7HTtFJczDnYC","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01T7GCg56HrHSxxHNdLcWt5t","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/strix/strix/skills/scan_modes/deep.md"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":13212,"cache_read_input_tokens":135705,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":13212,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdCS9U9VenY7Gqxkbk1Pr","attributionAgent":"Explore","type":"assistant","uuid":"7a5aaa65-4f72-459b-871c-252a07784e40","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:22:56.780Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"7a5aaa65-4f72-459b-871c-252a07784e40","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01T7GCg56HrHSxxHNdLcWt5t","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: deep\n3\tdescription: Exhaustive security assessment with maximum coverage, depth, and vulnerability chaining\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# Deep Testing Mode\n7\t\n8\tExhaustive security assessment. Maximum coverage, maximum depth. Finding what others miss is the goal.\n9\t\n10\t## Approach\n11\t\n12\tThorough understanding before exploitation. Test every parameter, every endpoint, every edge case. Chain findings for maximum impact.\n13\t\n14\t## Phase 1: Exhaustive Reconnaissance\n15\t\n16\t**Whitebox (source available)**\n17\t- Map every file, module, and code path in the repository\n18\t- Start with broad source-aware triage (`semgrep`, `ast-grep`, `gitleaks`, `trufflehog`, `trivy fs`) and use outputs to drive deep review\n19\t- Execute at least one structural AST pass (`sg` and/or Tree-sitter) per repository and store artifacts for reuse\n20\t- Keep AST artifacts bounded and query-driven (target relevant paths/sinks first; avoid whole-repo generic function dumps)\n21\t- Use syntax-aware parsing (Tree-sitter tooling) to improve symbol, route, and sink extraction quality\n22\t- Trace all entry points from HTTP handlers to database queries\n23\t- Document all authentication mechanisms and implementations\n24\t- Map authorization checks and access control model\n25\t- Identify all external service integrations and API calls\n26\t- Analyze configuration for secrets and misconfigurations\n27\t- Review database schemas and data relationships\n28\t- Map background jobs, cron tasks, async processing\n29\t- Identify all serialization/deserialization points\n30\t- Review file handling: upload, download, processing\n31\t- Understand the deployment model and infrastructure assumptions\n32\t- Check all dependency versions and repository risks against CVE/misconfiguration data\n33\t- For quick CVE lookups on a named product/version, use `vulnx search <query>`\n34\t  (ProjectDiscovery's CVE database) before falling back to web_search\n35\t\n36\t**Blackbox (no source)**\n37\t- Exhaustive subdomain enumeration with multiple sources and tools\n38\t- Full port scanning across all services\n39\t- Complete content discovery with multiple wordlists\n40\t- Technology fingerprinting on all assets\n41\t- API discovery via docs, JavaScript analysis, fuzzing\n42\t- Identify all parameters including hidden and rarely-used ones\n43\t- Map all user roles with different account types\n44\t- Document rate limiting, WAF rules, security controls\n45\t- Document complete application architecture as understood from outside\n46\t\n47\t## Phase 2: Business Logic Deep Dive\n48\t\n49\tCreate a complete storyboard of the application:\n50\t\n51\t- **User flows** - document every step of every workflow\n52\t- **State machines** - map all transitions (Created → Paid → Shipped → Delivered)\n53\t- **Trust boundaries** - identify where privilege changes hands\n54\t- **Invariants** - what rules should the application always enforce\n55\t- **Implicit assumptions** - what does the code assume that might be violated\n56\t- **Multi-step attack surfaces** - where can normal functionality be abused\n57\t- **Third-party integrations** - map all external service dependencies\n58\t\n59\tUse the application extensively as every user type to understand the full data lifecycle.\n60\t\n61\t## Phase 3: Comprehensive Attack Surface Testing\n62\t\n63\tTest every input vector with every applicable technique.\n64\t\n65\t**Input Handling**\n66\t- Multiple injection types: SQL, NoSQL, LDAP, XPath, command, template\n67\t- Encoding bypasses: double encoding, unicode, null bytes\n68\t- Boundary conditions and type confusion\n69\t- Large payloads and buffer-related issues\n70\t\n71\t**Authentication & Session**\n72\t- Exhaustive brute force protection testing\n73\t- Session fixation, hijacking, prediction\n74\t- JWT/token manipulation\n75\t- OAuth flow abuse scenarios\n76\t- Password reset vulnerabilities: token leakage, reuse, timing\n77\t- MFA bypass techniques\n78\t- Account enumeration through all channels\n79\t\n80\t**Access Control**\n81\t- Test every endpoint for horizontal and vertical access control\n82\t- Parameter tampering on all object references\n83\t- Forced browsing to all discovered resources\n84\t- HTTP method tampering (GET vs POST vs PUT vs DELETE)\n85\t- Access control after session state changes (logout, role change)\n86\t\n87\t**File Operations**\n88\t- Exhaustive file upload bypass: extension, content-type, magic bytes\n89\t- Path traversal on all file parameters\n90\t- SSRF through file inclusion\n91\t- XXE through all XML parsing points\n92\t\n93\t**Business Logic**\n94\t- Race conditions on all state-changing operations\n95\t- Workflow bypass on every multi-step process\n96\t- Price/quantity manipulation in transactions\n97\t- Parallel execution attacks\n98\t- TOCTOU (time-of-check to time-of-use) vulnerabilities\n99\t\n100\t**Advanced Techniques**\n101\t- HTTP request smuggling (multiple proxies/servers)\n102\t- Cache poisoning and cache deception\n103\t- Subdomain takeover\n104\t- Prototype pollution (JavaScript applications)\n105\t- CORS misconfiguration exploitation\n106\t- WebSocket security testing\n107\t- GraphQL-specific attacks (introspection, batching, nested queries)\n108\t\n109\t## Phase 4: Vulnerability Chaining\n110\t\n111\tIndividual bugs are starting points. Chain them for maximum impact:\n112\t\n113\t- Combine information disclosure with access control bypass\n114\t- Chain SSRF to reach internal services\n115\t- Use low-severity findings to enable high-impact attacks\n116\t- Build multi-step attack paths that automated tools miss\n117\t- Cross component boundaries: user → admin, external → internal, read → write, single-tenant → cross-tenant\n118\t\n119\t**Chaining Principles**\n120\t- Treat every finding as a pivot point: ask \"what does this unlock next?\"\n121\t- Continue until reaching maximum privilege / maximum data exposure / maximum control\n122\t- Prefer end-to-end exploit paths over isolated bugs: initial foothold → pivot → privilege gain → sensitive action/data\n123\t- Validate chains by executing the full sequence (proxy + browser for workflows, python for automation)\n124\t- When a pivot is found, spawn focused agents to continue the chain in the next component\n125\t\n126\t## Phase 5: Persistent Testing\n127\t\n128\tWhen initial attempts fail:\n129\t\n130\t- Research technology-specific bypasses\n131\t- Try alternative exploitation techniques\n132\t- Test edge cases and unusual functionality\n133\t- Test with different client contexts\n134\t- Revisit areas with new information from other findings\n135\t- Consider timing-based and blind exploitation\n136\t- Look for logic flaws that require deep application understanding\n137\t\n138\t## Phase 6: Comprehensive Reporting\n139\t\n140\t- Document every confirmed vulnerability with full details\n141\t- Include all severity levels—low findings may enable chains\n142\t- Complete reproduction steps and working PoC\n143\t- Remediation recommendations with specific guidance\n144\t- Note areas requiring additional review beyond current scope\n145\t\n146\t## Agent Strategy\n147\t\n148\tAfter reconnaissance, decompose the application hierarchically:\n149\t\n150\t1. **Component level** - Auth System, Payment Gateway, User Profile, Admin Panel\n151\t2. **Feature level** - Login Form, Registration API, Password Reset\n152\t3. **Vulnerability level** - SQLi Agent, XSS Agent, Auth Bypass Agent\n153\t\n154\tSpawn specialized agents at each level. Scale horizontally to maximum parallelization:\n155\t- Do NOT overload a single agent with multiple vulnerability types\n156\t- Each agent focuses on one specific area or vulnerability type\n157\t- Creates a massive parallel swarm covering every angle\n158\t\n159\t## Mindset\n160\t\n161\tRelentless. Creative. Patient. Thorough. Persistent.\n162\t\n163\tThis is about finding what others miss. Test every parameter, every endpoint, every edge case. If one approach fails, try ten more. Understand how components interact to find systemic issues.\n164\t"}]},"uuid":"e97072cc-b6ce-43ab-bfd3-89d495b40f80","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:22:56.794Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7a5aaa65-4f72-459b-871c-252a07784e40","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

writers for Strix scan reports.\"\"\"\n2\t\n3\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n4\t\n5\timport csv\n6\timport io\n7\timport json\n8\timport logging\n9\timport tempfile\n10\tfrom datetime import UTC, datetime\n11\tfrom pathlib import Path\n12\tfrom typing import Any\n13\t\n14\tfrom strix.core.paths import run_record_path\n15\t\n16\t\n17\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n18\t\n19\t_SEVERITY_ORDER = {\"critical\": 0, \"high\": 1, \"medium\": 2, \"low\": 3, \"info\": 4}\n20\t\n21\t\n22\tdef read_run_record(run_dir: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:\n23\t    path = run_record_path(run_dir)\n24\t    if not path.exists():\n25\t        return {}\n26\t    try:\n27\t        data = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n28\t    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:\n29\t        raise RuntimeError(f\"run.json at {path} is unreadable: {exc}\") from exc\n30\t    if not isinstance(data, dict):\n31\t        raise TypeError(f\"run.json at {path} is not an object\")\n32\t    return data\n33\t\n34\t\n35\tdef write_run_record(run_dir: Path, run_record: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n36\t    _atomic_write_text(\n37\t str) -> None:\n43\t    path = run_dir / \"penetration_test_report.md\"\n44\t    with path.open(\"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n45\t        f.write(\"# Security Penetration Test Report\\n\\n\")\n46\t        f.write(f\"**Generated:** {datetime.now(UTC).strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S UTC')}\\n\\n\")\n47\t        f.write(f\"{final_scan_result}\\n\")\n48\t    logger.info(\"Saved final penetration test report to: %s\", path)\n49\t\n50\t\n51\tdef write_vulnerabilities(\n52\t    run_dir: Path,\n53\t    vulnerability_reports: list[dict[str, Any]],\n54\t    saved_vuln_ids: set[str],\n55\t) -> int:\n56\t    vuln_dir = run_dir / \"vulnerabilities\"\n57\t    vuln_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n58\t\n59\t    new_reports = [r for r in vulnerability_reports if r[\"id\"] not in saved_vuln_ids]\n60\t\n61\t    for report in new_reports:\n62\t        _atomic_write_text(\n63\t            vuln_dir / f\"{report['id']}.md\",\n64\t            render_vulnerability_md(report),\n65\t        )\n66\t        saved_vuln_ids.add(report[\"id\"])\n67\t\n68\t    sorted_reports = sorted(\n69\t        vulnerability_reports,\n70\t        key=lambda r: (_SEVERITY_ORDER.get(r[\"severity\"], 5), r[\"timestamp\"]),\n71\t    )\n72\t    csv_path = run_dir / \"vulnerabilities.csv\"\n73\t    csv_buf = io.StringIO()\n74\t    fieldnames = [\"id\", \"title\", \"severity\", \"timestamp\", \"file\"]\n75\t    csv_writer = csv.DictWriter(csv_buf, fieldnames=fieldnames, lineterminator=\"\\r\\n\")\n76\t    csv_writer.writeheader()\n77\t    for report in sorted_reports:\n78\t        csv_writer.writerow(\n79\t            {\n80\t                \"id\": report[\"id\"],\n81\t                \"title\": report[\"title\"],\n82\t                \"severity\": report[\"severity\"].upper(),\n83\t                \"timestamp\": report[\"timestamp\"],\n84\t                \"file\": f\"vulnerabilities/{report['id']}.md\",\n85\t            },\n86\t        )\n87\t _atomic_write_text(path: Path, payload: str) -> None:\n105\t    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n106\t    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(\n107\t        mode=\"w\",\n108\t        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n109\t        dir=str(path.parent),\n110\t        prefix=f\".{path.name}.\",\n111\t        suffix=\".tmp\",\n112\t        delete=False,\n113\t    ) as tmp:\n114\t        tmp.write(payload)\n115\t        tmp_path = Path(tmp.name)\n116\t    tmp_path.replace(path)\n117\t\n118\t\n119\tdef render_vulnerability_md(report: dict[str, Any]) -> str:  # noqa: PLR0912, PLR0915\n120\t    lines: list[str] = [\n121\t        f\"# {report.get('title', 'Untitled Vulnerability')}\\n\",\n122\t        f\"**ID:** {report.get('id', 'unknown')}\",\n123\t        f\"**Severity:** {report.get('severity', 'unknown').upper()}\",\n124\t        f\"**Found:** {report.get('timestamp', 'unknown')}\",\n125\t    ]\n126\t\n127\t    dep_meta = report.get(\"dependency_metadata\") or {}\n128\t    metadata: list[tuple[str, Any]] = [\n129\t        (\"Target\", report.get(\"target\")),\n130\t        (\"Package\", dep_meta.get(\"package_name\")),\n131\t        (\"Ecosystem\", dep_meta.get(\"package_ecosystem\")),\n132\t        (\"Installed Version\", dep_meta.get(\"installed_version\")),\n133\t        (\"Fixed Version\", dep_meta.get(\"fixed_version\")),\n134\t        (\"Endpoint\", report.get(\"endpoint\")),\n135\t        (\"Method\", report.get(\"method\")),\n136\t        (\"CVE\", report.get(\"cve\")),\n137\t        (\"CWE\", report.get(\"cwe\")),\n138\t    ]\n139\t    cvss = report.get(\"cvss\")\n140\t    if cvss is not None:\n141\t        metadata.append((\"CVSS\", cvss))\n142\t    if report.get(\"fix_effort\"):\n143\t        metadata.append((\"Fix Effort\", str(report[\"fix_effort\"]).title()))\n144\t    for label, value in metadata:\n145\t        if value:\n146\t            lines.append(f\"**{label}:** {value}\")\n147\t\n148\t    lines.append(\"\")\n149\t    lines.append(\"## Description\\n\")\n150\t    lines.append(report.get(\"description\") or \"No description provided.\")\n151\t    lines.append(\"\")\n152\t\n153\t    if report.get(\"evidence\"):\n154\t        lines.append(\"## Evidence\\n\")\n155\t        lines.append(str(report[\"evidence\"]))\n156\t        lines.append(\"\")\n157\t\n158\t    if report.get(\"impact\"):\n159\t        lines.append(\"## Impact\\n\")\n160\t        lines.append(str(report[\"impact\"]))\n161\t        lines.append(\"\")\n162\t\n163\t    if report.get(\"technical_analysis\"):\n164\t        lines.append(\"## Technical Analysis\\n\")\n165\t        lines.append(str(report[\"technical_analysis\"]))\n166\t        lines.append(\"\")\n167\t\n168\t    if report.get(\"poc_description\") or report.get(\"poc_script_code\"):\n169\t        lines.append(\"## Proof of Concept\\n\")\n170\t        if report.get(\"poc_description\"):\n171\t            lines.append(str(report[\"poc_description\"]))\n172\t            lines.append(\"\")\n173\t        if report.get(\"poc_script_code\"):\n174\t            lines.append(\"```\")\n175\t            lines.append(str(report[\"poc_script_code\"]))\n176\t            lines.append(\"```\")\n177\t            lines.append(\"\")\n178\t\n179\t    if report.get(\"code_locations\"):\n180\t        lines.append(\"## Code Analysis\\n\")\n181\t        for i, loc in enumerate(report[\"code_locations\"]):\n182\t            file_ref = loc.get(\"file\", \"unknown\")\n183\t            line_ref = \"\"\n184\t            if loc.get(\"start_line\") is not None:\n185\t                if loc.get(\"end_line\") and loc[\"end_line\"] != loc[\"start_line\"]:\n186\t                    line_ref = f\" (lines {loc['start_line']}-{loc['end_line']})\"\n187\t                else:\n188\t                    line_ref = f\" (line {loc['start_line']})\"\n189\t            lines.append(f\"**Location {i + 1}:** `{file_ref}`{line_ref}\")\n190\t            if loc.get(\"label\"):\n191\t                lines.append(f\"  {loc['label']}\")\n192\t            if loc.get(\"snippet\"):\n193\t                lines.append(f\"  ```\\n  {loc['snippet']}\\n  ```\")\n194\t            if loc.get(\"fix_before\") or loc.get(\"fix_after\"):\n195\t                lines.append(\"\\n  **Suggested Fix:**\")\n196\t                lines.append(\"```diff\")\n197\t                if loc.get(\"fix_before\"):\n198\t                    lines.extend(f\"- {ln}\" for ln in str(loc[\"fix_before\"]).splitlines())\n199\t                if loc.get(\"fix_after\"):\n200\t                    lines.extend(f\"+ {ln}\" for ln in str(loc[\"fix_after\"]).splitlines())\n201\t                lines.append(\"```\")\n202\t            lines.append(\"\")\n203\t\n204\t    if report.get(\"remediation_steps\"):\n205\t        lines.append(\"## Remediation\\n\")\n206\t        lines.append(str(report[\"remediation_steps\"]))\n207\t        lines.append(\"\")\n208\t\n209\t    if report.get(\"assumptions\"):\n210\t        lines.append(\"## Assumptions\\n\")\n211\t        lines.append(str(report[\"assumptions\"]))\n212\t        lines.append(\"\")\n213\t\n214\t    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n215\t"}]},"uuid":"2d0dc47b-a2c5-437b-b0f6-14df4e12ba09","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:23:06.229Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3d2b886b-c914-4539-b144-b2e1d7260b0f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"85a0b9a6-c588-4472-b30c-3e04a06607b0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SFYcHY2Df7gipVnRPf1eTS","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Sandbox backend registry — selected via STRIX_RUNTIME_BACKEND (default: docker).\"\"\"\n2\t\n3\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n4\t\n5\timport logging\n6\tfrom collections.abc import Awaitable, Callable\n7\tfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any\n8\t\n9\t\n10\tif TYPE_CHECKING:\n11\t    from agents.sandbox.manifest import Manifest\n12\t\n13\t\n14\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n15\t\n16\t\n17\tSandboxBackend = Callable[..., Awaitable[tuple[Any, Any]]]\n18\t\n19\t\n20\tasync def _docker_backend(\n21\t    *,\n22\t    image: str,\n23\t    manifest: Manifest,\n24\t    exposed_ports: tuple[int, ...],\n25\t    bind_mounts: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,\n26\t) -> tuple[Any, Any]:\n27\t    \"\"\"Bring up a session backed by the local Docker daemon.\n28\t\n29\t    Uses :class:`StrixDockerSandboxClient` to inject NET_ADMIN /\n30\t    NET_RAW caps + ``host.docker.internal`` host-gateway. Imports\n31\t    ``docker`` lazily so deployments that target a non-Docker\n32\t    backend don't need the docker-py library installed.\n33\t\n34\t    ``session.start()`` is what materializes the manifest entries\n35\t    (LocalDir copies and manifest-declared volume/FUSE mounts) into the\n36\t    running container — the SDK's ``client.create()`` only builds the inner\n37\t    session object without applying the manifest. ``async with session:``\n38\t    would call it too, but Strix manages session lifetime explicitly via\n39\t    ``client.delete()`` so we trigger ``start()`` ourselves.\n40\t\n41\t    ``bind_mounts`` are host directories (e.g. large repos passed via\n42\t    ``--mount``) bind-mounted read-only; unlike manifest entries they are\n43\t    applied by Docker at container-create time, not by ``start()``.\n44\t    \"\"\"\n45\t    import docker\n46\t    from agents.sandbox.sandboxes.docker import DockerSandboxClientOptions\n47\t\n48\t    from strix.runtime.docker_client import StrixDockerSandboxClient\n49\t\n50\t    client = StrixDockerSandboxClient(docker.from_env())\n51\t    client.strix_bind_mounts = bind_mounts or []\n52\t    options = DockerSandboxClientOptions(image=image, exposed_ports=exposed_ports)\n53\t    session = await client.create(options=options, manifest=manifest)\n54\t    await session.start()\n55\t    return client, session\n56\t\n57\t\n58\t_BACKENDS: dict[str, SandboxBackend] = {\n59\t    \"docker\": _docker_backend,\n60\t}\n61\t\n62\t\n63\tdef get_backend(name: str) -> SandboxBackend:\n64\t    \"\"\"Return the backend factory for ``name`` or raise.\n65\t\n66\t    Args:\n67\t        name: Backend identifier (e.g. ``\"docker\"``). Match is exact;\n68\t            no fallback. Unknown values raise so config typos surface\n69\t            immediately instead of silently picking a default.\n70\t    \"\"\"\n71\t    backend = _BACKENDS.get(name)\n72\t    if backend is None:\n73\t        supported = \", \".join(sorted(_BACKENDS))\n74\t        raise ValueError(\n75\t            f\"Unknown STRIX_RUNTIME_BACKEND: {name!r} (supported: {supported})\",\n76\t        )\n77\t    logger.debug(\"Selected sandbox backend: %s\", name)\n78\t    return backend\n79\t\n80\t\n81\tdef register_backend(name: str, backend: SandboxBackend) -> None:\n82\t    \"\"\"Register a custom backend under ``name``.\n83\t\n84\t    Intended for downstream users who ship their own runtime — register\n85\t    before any ``session_manager.create_or_reuse`` call. Re-registering\n86\t    an existing name overwrites the prior entry.\n87\t    \"\"\"\n88\t    _BACKENDS[name] = backend\n89\t    logger.info(\"Registered sandbox backend: %s\", name)\n90\t\n91\t\n92\tdef supported_backends() -> list[str]:\n93\t    return sorted(_BACKENDS)\n94\t"}]},"uuid":"3e910099-a8a3-4d14-a4d6-f7efa928c870","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:23:07.034Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"85a0b9a6-c588-4472-b30c-3e04a06607b0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"1085ce4c-9cac-482f-a3f1-641c75afc156","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MvMJEQroW2cNr7x3SqJmVB","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: root-agent\n3\tdescription: Orchestration layer that coordinates specialized subagents for security assessments\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# Root Agent\n7\t\n8\tOrchestration layer for security assessments. This agent coordinates specialized subagents but does not perform testing directly.\n9\t\n10\tYou can create agents throughout the testing process—not just at the beginning. Spawn agents dynamically based on findings and evolving scope.\n11\t\n12\t## Role\n13\t\n14\t- Decompose targets into discrete, parallelizable tasks\n15\t- Spawn and monitor specialized subagents\n16\t- Aggregate findings into a cohesive final report\n17\t- Manage dependencies and handoffs between agents\n18\t\n19\t## Scope Decomposition\n20\t\n21\tBefore spawning agents, analyze the target:\n22\t\n23\t1. **Identify attack surfaces** - web apps, APIs, infrastructure, etc.\n24\t2. **Define boundaries** - in-scope domains, IP ranges, excluded assets\n25\t3. **Determine approach** - blackbox, greybox, or whitebox assessment\n26\t4. **Prioritize by risk** - critical assets and high-value targets first\n27\t\n28\t## Agent Architecture\n29\t\n30\tStructure agents by function:\n31\t\n32\t**Reconnaissance**\n33\t- Asset discovery and enumeration\n34\t- Technology fingerprinting\n35\t- Attack surface mapping\n36\t\n37\t**Vulnerability Assessment**\n38\t- Injection testing (SQLi, XSS, command injection)\n39\t- Authentication and session analysis\n40\t- Access control testing (IDOR, privilege escalation)\n41\t- Business logic flaws\n42\t- Infrastructure vulnerabilities\n43\t\n44\t**Exploitation and Validation**\n45\t- Proof-of-concept development\n46\t- Impact demonstration\n47\t- Vulnerability chaining\n48\t\n49\t**Reporting**\n50\t- Finding documentation\n51\t- Remediation recommendations\n52\t\n53\t## Coordination Principles\n54\t\n55\t**Task Independence**\n56\t\n57\tCreate agents with minimal dependencies. Parallel execution is faster than sequential.\n58\t\n59\t**Clear Objectives**\n60\t\n61\tEach agent should have a specific, measurable goal. Vague objectives lead to scope creep and redundant work.\n62\t\n63\t**Avoid Duplication**\n64\t\n65\tBefore creating agents:\n66\t1. Analyze the target scope and break into independent tasks\n67\t2. Check existing agents to avoid overlap\n68\t3. Create agents with clear, specific objectives\n69\t\n70\t**Hierarchical Delegation**\n71\t\n72\tComplex findings warrant specialized subagents:\n73\t- Discovery agent finds potential vulnerability\n74\t- Validation agent confirms exploitability\n75\t- Reporting agent documents with reproduction steps\n76\t- Fix agent provides remediation (if needed)\n77\t\n78\t**Resource Efficiency**\n79\t\n80\t- Avoid duplicate coverage across agents\n81\t- Terminate agents when objectives are met or no longer relevant\n82\t- Use message passing only when essential (requests/answers, critical handoffs)\n83\t- Prefer batched updates over routine status messages\n84\t\n85\t## Completion\n86\t\n87\tWhen all agents report completion:\n88\t\n89\t1. Collect and deduplicate findings across agents\n90\t2. Assess overall security posture\n91\t3. Compile executive summary with prioritized recommendations\n92\t4. Invoke finish tool with final report\n93\t"}]},"uuid":"b13ad226-ed41-4921-80ae-bbdb52043528","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:23:21.819Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1085ce4c-9cac-482f-a3f1-641c75afc156","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

We subclass\n6\tand reimplement the method body verbatim from the SDK source, with three\n7\tdeltas:\n8\t\n9\t1. Drop the SDK's ``entrypoint=[\"tail\"]`` override; supply ``[\"tail\", \"-f\",\n10\t   \"/dev/null\"]`` as ``command`` instead. This lets our image's\n11\t   ``docker-entrypoint.sh`` actually run — without it, ``caido-cli`` never\n12\t   starts inside the container and ``bootstrap_caido`` retries against a\n13\t   dead port.\n14\t2. Append NET_ADMIN/NET_RAW to ``cap_add`` (required by ``nmap -sS`` and\n15\t   other raw-socket tools).\n16\t3. Add ``host.docker.internal`` → host-gateway to ``extra_hosts`` so the\n17\t   agent can reach host-served apps.\n18\t\n19\tPinned to ``openai-agents==0.14.6``. Bumping the SDK requires\n20\tre-merging the parent body. Track upstream for an injection hook.\n21\t\"\"\"\n22\t\n23\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n24\t\n25\timport contextlib\n26\timport logging\n27\timport os\n28\timport uuid\n29\tfrom typing import Any, cast\n30\t\n31\tfrom agents.sandbox.errors import ExposedPortUnavailableError\n32\tfrom agents.sandbox.manifest import Manifest\n33\tfrom agents.sandbox.sandboxes.docker import (\n34\t    DockerSandboxClient,\n35\t    DockerSandboxSession,\n36\t    _build_docker_volume_mounts,\n37\t    _docker_port_key,\n38\t    _manifest_requires_fuse,\n39\t    _manifest_requires_sys_admin,\n40\t)\n41\tfrom agents.sandbox.session.sandbox_session import SandboxSession\n42\tfrom agents.sandbox.types import ExposedPortEndpoint\n43\tfrom docker import errors as docker_errors  # type: ignore[import-untyped, unused-ignore]\n44\tfrom docker.models.containers import Container  # type: ignore[import-untyped, unused-ignore]\n45\tfrom docker.types import LogConfig  # type: ignore[import-untyped, unused-ignore]\n46\tfrom docker.types import Mount as DockerSDKMount  # type: ignore[import-untyped, unused-ignore]\n47\tfrom docker.utils import parse_repository_tag  # type: ignore[import-untyped, unused-ignore]\n48\tfrom requests.exceptions import RequestException\n49\t\n50\t\n51\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n52\t\n53\t\n54\t_SANDBOX_NETWORK_ENV = \"STRIX_DOCKER_SANDBOX_NETWORK\"\n55\t\n56\t\n57\tdef _sandbox_network() -> str | None:\n58\t    value = os.environ.get(_SANDBOX_NETWORK_ENV, \"\").strip()\n59\t    return value or None\n60\t\n61\t\n62\tdef _apply_sandbox_network(create_kwargs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n63\t    network = _sandbox_network()\n64\t    if network:\n65\t        create_kwargs[\"network\"] = network\n66\t        create_kwargs.pop(\"ports\", None)\n67\t\n68\t\n69\tdef _apply_resource_limits(create_kwargs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n70\t    \"\"\"Apply optional cgroup resource caps from the environment. Unset/blank\n71\t    values leave docker's default (unbounded), so this is opt-in per host.\"\"\"\n72\t    mem_limit = os.environ.get(\"STRIX_SANDBOX_MEM_LIMIT\", \"\").strip()\n73\t    if mem_limit:\n74\t        create_kwargs[\"mem_limit\"] = mem_limit\n75\t\n76\t    shm_size = os.environ.get(\"STRIX_SANDBOX_SHM_SIZE\", \"\").strip()\n77\t    if shm_size:\n78\t        create_kwargs[\"shm_size\"] = shm_size\n79\t\n80\t    cpus = os.environ.get(\"STRIX_SANDBOX_CPUS\", \"\").strip()\n81\t    if cpus:\n82\t        with contextlib.suppress(ValueError, OverflowError):\n83\t            nano_cpus = int(float(cpus) * 1_000_000_000)\n84\t            if 0 < nano_cpus <= 2**63 - 1:\n85\t                create_kwargs[\"nano_cpus\"] = nano_cpus\n86\t\n87\t    pids_limit = os.environ.get(\"STRIX_SANDBOX_PIDS_LIMIT\", \"\").strip()\n88\t    if pids_limit:\n89\t        with contextlib.suppress(ValueError):\n90\t            create_kwargs[\"pids_limit\"] = int(pids_limit)\n91\t\n92\t\n93\tdef _apply_log_limits(create_kwargs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n94\t    \"\"\"Bound the container's json-file log so a runaway process in the sandbox\n95\t    (e.g. a tool that busy-loops writing to stdout) cannot fill the host disk\n96\t    and take the Docker daemon down with it.\n97\t\n98\t    Unlike the cgroup caps above, this defaults **on** — docker's own default\n99\t    is an unbounded json-file, which is unsafe for an autonomous agent that\n100\t    executes arbitrary commands. ``max-file`` rotation means the on-disk cap is\n101\t    ``max-size * max-file``. Set ``STRIX_SANDBOX_LOG_MAX_SIZE`` to ``0``/``off``\n102\t    to opt back out to docker's default.\"\"\"\n103\t    max_size = os.environ.get(\"STRIX_SANDBOX_LOG_MAX_SIZE\", \"50m\").strip()\n104\t    if max_size.lower() in (\"0\", \"off\", \"none\", \"unlimited\"):\n105\t        return\n106\t    max_file = os.environ.get(\"STRIX_SANDBOX_LOG_MAX_FILE\", \"3\").strip() or \"3\"\n107\t    create_kwargs[\"log_config\"] = LogConfig(\n108\t        type=LogConfig.types.JSON,\n109\t        config={\"max-size\": max_size, \"max-file\": max_file},\n110\t    )\n111\t\n112\t\n113\tclass StrixDockerSandboxSession(DockerSandboxSession):\n114\t    sandbox_network: str = \"\"\n115\t\n116\t    async def _resolve_exposed_port(self, port: int) -> ExposedPortEndpoint:\n117\t        try:\n118\t            self._container.reload()\n119\t        except docker_errors.APIError as e:\n120\t            raise ExposedPortUnavailableError(\n121\t                port=port,\n122\t                exposed_ports=self.state.exposed_ports,\n123\t                reason=\"backend_unavailable\",\n124\t                context={\n125\t                    \"backend\": \"docker\",\n126\t                    \"detail\": \"container_reload_failed\",\n127\t                    \"network\": self.sandbox_network,\n128\t                },\n129\t                cause=e,\n130\t            ) from e\n131\t\n132\t        attrs = getattr(self._container, \"attrs\", {}) or {}\n133\t        networks = attrs.get(\"NetworkSettings\", {}).get(\"Networks\", {})\n134\t        endpoint = networks.get(self.sandbox_network) or {}\n135\t        ip = endpoint.get(\"IPAddress\") or endpoint.get(\"GlobalIPv6Address\")\n136\t        if not isinstance(ip, str) or not ip:\n137\t            raise ExposedPortUnavailableError(\n138\t                port=port,\n139\t                exposed_ports=self.state.exposed_ports,\n140\t                reason=\"backend_unavailable\",\n141\t                context={\n142\t                    \"backend\": \"docker\",\n143\t                    \"detail\": \"container_not_on_network\",\n144\t                    \"network\": self.sandbox_network,\n145\t                },\n146\t            )\n147\t        host = f\"[{ip}]\" if \":\" in ip else ip\n148\t        return ExposedPortEndpoint(host=host, port=port, tls=False)\n149\t\n150\t\n151\tclass StrixDockerSandboxClient(DockerSandboxClient):\n152\t    # Host directories to bind-mount into the container, set by the docker\n153\t    # backend before ``create()``. Each item is ``{source, target, read_only}``.\n154\t    strix_bind_mounts: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None\n155\t\n156\t    async def _create_container(\n157\t        self,\n158\t        image: str,\n159\t        *,\n160\t        manifest: Manifest | None = None,\n161\t        exposed_ports: tuple[int, ...] = (),\n162\t        session_id: uuid.UUID | None = None,\n163\t    ) -> Container:\n164\t        # ----- BEGIN VERBATIM COPY of DockerSandboxClient._create_container -----\n165\t        # SDK ref: src/agents/sandbox/sandboxes/docker.py:1434-1477 (v0.14.6).\n166\t        if not self.image_exists(image):\n167\t            repo, tag = parse_repository_tag(image)\n168\t            self.docker_client.images.pull(repo, tag=tag or None, all_tags=False)\n169\t\n170\t        assert self.image_exists(image)\n171\t        environment: dict[str, str] | None = None\n172\t        if manifest:\n173\t            environment = await manifest.environment.resolve()\n174\t        # Strix delta from the SDK body: drop ``entrypoint`` override and\n175\t        # supply ``tail -f /dev/null`` as ``command`` so the image's\n176\t        # ENTRYPOINT (``docker-entrypoint.sh``) runs setup, then ``exec\n177\t        # \"$@\"`` becomes ``exec tail -f /dev/null`` for the keep-alive.\n178\t        # Without this, caido-cli + the in-container CA trust never get\n179\t        # initialized.\n180\t        create_kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {\n181\t            \"image\": image,\n182\t            \"detach\": True,\n183\t            \"command\": [\"tail\", \"-f\", \"/dev/null\"],\n184\t            \"environment\": environment,\n185\t        }\n186\t        if manifest is not None:\n187\t            docker_mounts = _build_docker_volume_mounts(\n188\t                manifest,\n189\t                session_id=session_id,\n190\t            )\n191\t            if docker_mounts:\n192\t                create_kwargs[\"mounts\"] = docker_mounts\n193\t            if _manifest_requires_fuse(manifest):\n194\t                create_kwargs.update(\n195\t                    devices=[\"/dev/fuse\"],\n196\t                    cap_add=[\"SYS_ADMIN\"],\n197\t                    security_opt=[\"apparmor:unconfined\"],\n198\t                )\n199\t            elif _manifest_requires_sys_admin(manifest):\n200\t                create_kwargs.update(\n201\t                    cap_add=[\"SYS_ADMIN\"],\n202\t                    security_opt=[\"apparmor:unconfined\"],\n203\t                )\n204\t        if exposed_ports:\n205\t            create_kwargs[\"ports\"] = {\n206\t                _docker_port_key(port): (\"127.0.0.1\", None) for port in exposed_ports\n207\t            }\n208\t        # ----- END VERBATIM COPY -----\n209\t\n210\t        # Strix injections — append, don't overwrite, so FUSE/SYS_ADMIN survives.\n211\t        cap_add = create_kwargs.setdefault(\"cap_add\", [])\n212\t        if not isinstance(cap_add, list):\n213\t            cap_add = list(cap_add)\n214\t            create_kwargs[\"cap_add\"] = cap_add\n215\t        for cap in (\"NET_ADMIN\", \"NET_RAW\"):\n216\t            if cap not in cap_add:\n217\t                cap_add.append(cap)\n218\t\n219\t        extra_hosts = create_kwargs.setdefault(\"extra_hosts\", {})\n220\t        extra_hosts[\"host.docker.internal\"] = \"host-gateway\"\n221\t\n222\t        _apply_sandbox_network(create_kwargs)\n223\t        _apply_resource_limits(create_kwargs)\n224\t        _apply_log_limits(create_kwargs)\n225\t\n226\t        # Strix injection: host bind mounts (e.g. large repos passed via --mount)\n227\t        # that bypass the SDK's file-by-file LocalDir copy.\n228\t        bind_mounts = getattr(self, \"strix_bind_mounts\", ())\n229\t        if bind_mounts:\n230\t            mounts = create_kwargs.setdefault(\"mounts\", [])\n231\t            for spec in bind_mounts:\n232\t                mounts.append(\n233\t                    DockerSDKMount(\n234\t                        target=spec[\"target\"],\n235\t                        source=spec[\"source\"],\n236\t                        type=\"bind\",\n237\t                        read_only=spec.get(\"read_only\", True),\n238\t                    )\n239\t                )\n240\t\n241\t        logger.debug(\n242\t            \"Creating sandbox container: image=%s caps=%s exposed_ports=%s\",\n243\t            image,\n244\t            cap_add,\n245\t            list(exposed_ports),\n246\t        )\n247\t        container = self.docker_client.containers.create(**create_kwargs)\n248\t        logger.info(\n249\t            \"Sandbox container created: id=%s image=%s\",\n250\t            container.short_id if hasattr(container, \"short_id\") else \"?\",\n251\t            image,\n252\t        )\n253\t        return container\n254\t\n255\t    async def create(self, **kwargs: Any) -> SandboxSession:\n256\t        session = await super().create(**kwargs)\n257\t        network = _sandbox_network()\n258\t        inner = session._inner\n259\t        if network and isinstance(inner, DockerSandboxSession):\n260\t            inner.__class__ = StrixDockerSandboxSession\n261\t            cast(\"StrixDockerSandboxSession\", inner).sandbox_network = network\n262\t        return session\n263\t\n264\t    async def delete(self, session: SandboxSession) -> SandboxSession:\n265\t        container_id = getattr(getattr(session._inner, \"state\", None), \"container_id\", None)\n266\t        if container_id:\n267\t            # Best-effort kill: NotFound/APIError cover a gone or unhappy\n268\t            # container. RequestException covers a torn-down daemon socket —\n269\t            # containers.get() -> inspect_container raises requests'\n270\t            # ConnectionError, which is a sibling of docker.errors.APIError\n271\t            # under requests.RequestException (not a subclass), so it escapes\n272\t            # an APIError-only suppress and surfaces a full traceback even\n273\t            # though this teardown is meant to be best-effort.\n274\t            with contextlib.suppress(\n275\t                docker_errors.NotFound, docker_errors.APIError, RequestException\n276\t            ):\n277\t                self.docker_client.containers.get(container_id).kill()\n278\t        return await super().delete(session)\n279\t"}]},"uuid":"71cf43a5-f71a-4362-83e2-e86584946e2e","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:23:31.336Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9aafe2b4-dc79-4335-bb6e-f846b590c82d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"9270ac4e-7f4c-4de3-aa15-13d2e962b746","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdCSCw3LsdR45NkC67jq9","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Db3TSbg7j6f732RwaJoz79","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/strix && for d in shell apply_patch view_image agent_browser; do echo \"=== $d ===\"; ls strix/tools/$d/; done && echo \"=== agent_browser skill head ===\" && head -40 strix/skills/tooling/agent_browser.md && echo \"=== usage.py head ===\" && head -50 strix/report/usage.py","description":"Inspect remaining tool dirs and usage ledger"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":6403,"cache_read_input_tokens":164244,"output_tokens":605,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":6403},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":605,"cache_read_input_tokens":164244,"cache_creation_input_tokens":6403,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":6403,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdCSCtAhcPwuhv5nBVbRq","attributionAgent":"Explore","type":"assistant","uuid":"9d90fb0f-7396-439a-8fc0-ebaed4148a7f","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:23:44.680Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"9d90fb0f-7396-439a-8fc0-ebaed4148a7f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3bfd3d93-3d8c-4d5a-8244-149007afff7e","agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Db3TSbg7j6f732RwaJoz79","type":"tool_result","content":"=== shell ===\nREADME.md\n=== apply_patch ===\nREADME.md\n=== view_image ===\nREADME.md\n=== agent_browser ===\nREADME.md\n=== agent_browser skill head ===\n---\nname: agent_browser\ndescription: agent-browser CLI for headless Chrome via shell. Snapshot-and-ref workflow, click/fill/extract, screenshots, multi-tab, multi-session, network mocking. Pre-installed in the sandbox; invoke via exec_command.\n---\n\n\n# agent-browser core\n\nFast browser automation CLI for AI agents. Chrome/Chromium via CDP, no\nPlaywright or Puppeteer dependency. Accessibility-tree snapshots with compact\n`@eN` refs let agents interact with pages in ~200-400 tokens instead of\nparsing raw HTML.\n\nPre-installed in the sandbox image. Always invoke via the\n``exec_command`` shell tool. The Caido HTTP/HTTPS proxy is already\nwired via ``http_proxy`` / ``https_proxy`` env vars — **do not pass\n``--proxy``**; agent-browser picks it up automatically and Caido\ncaptures all page traffic. Localhost (CDP) traffic is excluded via\n``NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1``.\n\nDefault viewport is 1280×720. For sites that gate behavior on real\ndesktop dimensions (responsive breakpoints, bot fingerprinting), run\n``agent-browser viewport 1920 1080`` once per session.\n\n## The core loop\n\n```bash\nagent-browser open <url>        # 1. Open a page\nagent-browser snapshot -i       # 2. See what's on it (interactive elements only)\nagent-browser click @e3         # 3. Act on refs from the snapshot\nagent-browser snapshot -i       # 4. Re-snapshot after any page change\n```\n\nRefs (`@e1`, `@e2`, ...) are assigned fresh on every snapshot. They become\n**stale the moment the page changes** — after clicks that navigate, form\nsubmits, dynamic re-renders, dialog opens. Always re-snapshot before your\nnext ref interaction.\n\n## Quickstart\n\n=== usage.py head ===\n\"\"\"SDK-native LLM usage aggregation for scan reports.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom agents.usage import Usage, deserialize_usage, serialize_usage\n\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\nclass LLMUsageLedger:\n    \"\"\"Aggregate SDK ``Usage`` objects and attach best-effort cost estimates.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self._total_usage = Usage()\n        self._agent_usage: dict[str, Usage] = {}\n        self._agent_metadata: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = {}\n        self._total_cost = 0.0\n\n    def record(\n        self,\n        *,\n        agent_id: str,\n        usage: Usage | None,\n        agent_name: str | None = None,\n        model: str | None = None,\n    ) -> bool:\n        if usage is None or not _usage_has_activity(usage):\n            return False\n\n        normalized_agent_id = str(agent_id or \"unknown\")\n        self._total_usage.add(usage)\n        self._agent_usage.setdefault(normalized_agent_id, Usage()).add(usage)\n\n        metadata = self._agent_metadata.setdefault(normalized_agent_id, {})\n        if agent_name:\n            metadata[\"agent_name\"] = agent_name\n        if model:\n            metadata[\"model\"] = model\n\n        if not _is_litellm_routed(model):\n            estimated = _estimate_litellm_cost(usage, model)\n            if estimated:\n                self._total_cost += estimated\n\n        return True","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"5fdf95ed-309c-4d02-8dae-b2574f719510","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:23:47.696Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9d90fb0f-7396-439a-8fc0-ebaed4148a7f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
