{"parentUuid":"013eda2c-42bf-49d2-9501-cf91c2c785ad","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1642ebb3bfdf63d1","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdCSE3iTXYAHbQjqSxG9Y","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've now read the full tool/skill/reporting surface. Here is the in-depth analysis.\n\n---\n\n# Strix — Tool + Skill + Reporting Capability Surface\n\nStrix is built on top of the **openai-agents SDK** (`openai-agents[litellm]==0.14.6`, `pyproject.toml:36`) plus its **sandbox** subsystem. The whole architecture is: a host-side Python orchestrator drives one or more `SandboxAgent`s; agent-internal bookkeeping tools run in-process on the host; the actual offensive work (Kali tools, browser, filesystem edits) runs inside a Docker container via SDK-emitted `Shell`/`Filesystem` tools; and HTTP interception goes through a Caido proxy sidecar in that same container.\n\n---\n\n## 1. The tool framework\n\n### 1a. How the toolset is assembled\n\n`strix/tools/__init__.py` is deliberately empty of code — it's just a docstring stating the design: *\"Host-side SDK function tools live in `<family>/tool[s].py` and are imported directly by `strix.agents.factory`. The sandbox-bound shell + filesystem tools are emitted by the SDK's `Shell` and `Filesystem` capabilities and bound to the live sandbox session per-run.\"*\n\nThe real registry is **`strix/agents/factory.py`**. It imports every host-side tool function and lists them in `_BASE_TOOLS` (`factory.py:329-357`):\n\n```python\n_BASE_TOOLS: tuple[Tool, ...] = (\n    think, load_skill,\n    create_todo, list_todos, update_todo, mark_todo_done, mark_todo_pending, delete_todo,\n    create_note, list_notes, get_note, update_note, delete_note,\n    web_search,\n    create_vulnerability_report, create_dependency_report,\n    list_requests, view_request, repeat_request, list_sitemap, view_sitemap_entry, scope_rules,\n    view_agent_graph, send_message_to_agent, wait_for_message, create_agent, stop_agent,\n)\n```\n\n`build_strix_agent` (`factory.py:402-472`) then constructs the agent. The lifecycle tool is appended based on role: root gets `finish_scan`, children get `agent_finish` (`factory.py:438-441`). Shell + Filesystem capabilities are attached last (`factory.py:460-471`):\n\n```python\nreturn SandboxAgent(\n    name=name, instructions=instructions, tools=tools,\n    tool_use_behavior=_finish_tool_use_behavior, model=None,\n    capabilities=[Filesystem(configure_tools=...), Shell(configure_tools=...)],\n)\n```\n\nThere's also an extension seam: `register_agent_tools(*tools)` (`factory.py:378`) lets downstream code add tools to every future agent, mirroring the backend-registration pattern.\n\n`_finish_tool_use_behavior` (`factory.py:307-326`) is important: a normal tool result does **not** end the agent loop. The run only terminates when a lifecycle tool returns JSON with `success` + `scan_completed`/`agent_completed` (`_lifecycle_tool_completed`, `factory.py:276-290`), or, in interactive mode, when `wait_for_message` parks.\n\n### 1b. Per-tool breakdown\n\n| Tool | File:line | What it does / capability granted | Class |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `think` | `thinking/tool.py:10` | Records private chain-of-thought; **no side effects, returns `{\"success\": true}`**. Pure \"scratchpad\" to force deliberation. | agent-internal |\n| `load_skill` | `load_skill/tool.py:10` | Returns markdown body of ≤5 named skills as a tool result (inline reference injection). | agent-internal |\n| `create/list/get/update/delete_note` | `notes/tools.py:295-433` | Per-run shared knowledge store (categories: general/findings/methodology/questions/plan/wiki; free-form tags). In-memory, mirrored to `{state_dir}/notes.json`. Visible to **all agents in the scan**. | agent-internal |\n| `create/list/update/delete_todo`, `mark_todo_done/pending` | `todo/tools.py:259-585` | **Per-agent private** task list (priority + status ranking, bulk ops). Mirrored to `todos.json`, keyed by `agent_id`. | agent-internal |\n| `web_search` | `web_search/tool.py:121` | Perplexity `sonar-reasoning-pro` call with a security-biased system prompt (`web_search/tool.py:19-41`). Needs `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY`. Fetches CVEs/bypasses/tooling docs. | external research (not the target) |\n| `create_vulnerability_report`, `create_dependency_report` | `reporting/tool.py:321, 826` | File a finding with validation, CVSS scoring, and LLM dedup. | agent-internal (records state) |\n| `list_requests, view_request, repeat_request, list_sitemap, view_sitemap_entry, scope_rules` | `proxy/tools.py` | Caido proxy control — inspect/replay/scope captured HTTP. | **offensive (reaches TARGET via replay)** |\n| `view_agent_graph, send_message_to_agent, wait_for_message, create_agent, stop_agent, agent_finish` | `agents_graph/tools.py` | Multi-agent orchestration over an `AgentCoordinator`. | agent-internal (coordination) |\n| `finish_scan` | `finish/tool.py:82` | Root-only terminal: persists the 4-section executive report and stops. | agent-internal (lifecycle) |\n| `exec_command`, `write_stdin` | SDK `Shell` capability (dir `tools/shell/` is just a README) | Run arbitrary commands **inside the container** (nmap, sqlmap, ffuf, nuclei, agent-browser, python). | **offensive (reaches TARGET)** |\n| `apply_patch` + filesystem read/write | SDK `Filesystem` capability (`tools/apply_patch/`, `tools/view_image/` are READMEs) | Read/edit files in `/workspace` (source review, white-box fixes). | container-side |\n\n**Offensive vs agent-internal split.** The tools that actually touch a target are (1) the SDK **Shell** tools — `exec_command` is where every Kali scanner, `agent-browser`, and custom Python spray runs against the target; and (2) the **proxy** `repeat_request` which dispatches modified HTTP at the target through Caido. `web_search` reaches out to Perplexity, not the target. Everything else — `think`, `notes`, `todo`, `load_skill`, the `agents_graph` coordination tools, `reporting`, and `finish_scan`/`agent_finish` — is agent-internal state/coordination with no target interaction.\n\n---\n\n## 2. How a \"tool\" is defined + registered\n\n### The decorator + schema generation\n\nEvery host-side tool is a plain async function decorated with **`@function_tool`** from the openai-agents SDK. Example (`thinking/tool.py:10`):\n\n```python\nfrom agents import function_tool\n\n@function_tool(timeout=10)\nasync def think(thought: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Record a private chain-of-thought note. ...\n    Args:\n        thought: The reasoning to record. Must be non-empty.\n    \"\"\"\n```\n\nThe SDK derives the LLM-facing JSON schema automatically from the **type hints + the Google-style docstring**: the function name becomes the tool name, the docstring summary becomes the description, and each parameter's type + `Args:` line become the parameter schema. There is no hand-written JSON schema for these. Optional args (`str | None = None`) become optional; `Literal[...]` types become enums (e.g. `message_type: Literal[\"query\",\"instruction\",\"information\"]` in `send_message_to_agent`). Some tools opt out of strict schema — `create_vulnerability_report` uses `@function_tool(timeout=180, strict_mode=False)` (`reporting/tool.py:321`) because it takes a nested `cvss_breakdown: dict` and `code_locations: list[dict]`.\n\nThe first parameter `ctx: RunContextWrapper` is injected by the SDK, not exposed to the model. This is how tools reach run-scoped state: `ctx.context` is a dict populated by the runner (`core/runner.py:283-292`) carrying `coordinator`, `sandbox_session`, `caido_client`, `agent_id`, `parent_id`, `interactive`, `spawn_child_agent`. E.g. proxy tools pull the client via `_ctx_client(ctx)` (`proxy/tools.py:50-52`); todo tools namespace by `ctx.context[\"agent_id\"]` (`todo/tools.py:103-105`).\n\nTools uniformly return a **JSON string** (`json.dumps({\"success\": ..., ...})`) so results are model-parseable and consistent.\n\n### Backend adaptation (Responses vs Chat Completions)\n\n`build_strix_agent(chat_completions_tools=...)` adapts tools for models that can't accept SDK \"custom tools.\" `_custom_tool_as_function_tool` (`factory.py:124-159`) rewraps a `CustomTool` as a `FunctionTool` with a synthetic single-string-input schema (`_raw_input_schema`, `factory.py:77-91`), and `_function_tool_with_error_result` (`factory.py:110-121`) makes tool exceptions return as model-visible strings instead of raising. Shell tools get extra massaging: `_wrap_exec_command` defaults `shell=bash` and translates `InvalidManifestPathError` into a readable message; `_wrap_write_stdin` decodes escape sequences (`factory.py:208-266`).\n\n### Host vs sandbox execution\n\n- **Host-side** (`@function_tool` funcs): run in the orchestrator's Python process. `think`, `notes`, `todo`, `reporting`, `agents_graph`, `web_search`, `load_skill`, `finish` all execute on the host. Proxy tools also run on the host but call the Caido GraphQL API over the container's exposed port.\n- **Sandbox-side** (`Shell`/`Filesystem` capabilities): the SDK emits `exec_command`/`write_stdin`/filesystem tools bound to the live `BaseSandboxSession`, so they execute **inside the Docker container**. This is the isolation boundary — arbitrary attack commands never run on the host.\n- **The container** is created by `StrixDockerSandboxClient._create_container` (`runtime/docker_client.py:156-253`), a verbatim copy of the SDK method with three deltas (documented at `docker_client.py:1-21`): (1) it keeps the image's ENTRYPOINT (supplies `tail -f /dev/null` as `command`) so the `caido-cli` sidecar actually starts; (2) it appends `NET_ADMIN`/`NET_RAW` caps for raw-socket tools like `nmap -sS` (`docker_client.py:211-217`); (3) it adds `host.docker.internal → host-gateway` so the agent can reach host-served apps (`docker_client.py:219-220`). Optional cgroup caps and json-log bounding guard the host (`docker_client.py:69-110`). Backend selection is pluggable via `STRIX_RUNTIME_BACKEND` (`runtime/backends.py:58-78`), default `docker`.\n\n---\n\n## 3. The proxy (Caido — a Burp alternative)\n\n**Bootstrap** (`runtime/caido_bootstrap.py`): Caido CLI runs as an in-container sidecar on `127.0.0.1:48080`. `bootstrap_caido` (`caido_bootstrap.py:83-109`) `session.exec()`s `curl` inside the container to run the GraphQL mutation `loginAsGuest` and grab an access token (retry loop doubles as readiness probe, `caido_bootstrap.py:34-80`), then builds a **host-side** `caido_sdk_client.Client` against the runtime's exposed-port URL and selects a fresh temporary project. That client is placed into every agent's `ctx.context[\"caido_client\"]`.\n\n**The API layer** (`proxy/caido_api.py`) wraps the Caido SDK/GraphQL. Because the GraphQL transport is not concurrency-safe, all calls are serialized under a lock (`caido_api.py:47`, and again at the tool layer `proxy/tools.py:47`). Key operations:\n- `list_requests_with_client` — HTTPQL-filtered, sorted, cursor-paginated history query.\n- `get_request_with_client` — fetch raw request/response bytes.\n- **Replay/repeat**: `repeat_request` reads the original raw request, `parse_raw_request` → `apply_modifications` → `build_raw_request` → `replay_send_raw` (`caido_api.py:170-360`). `replay_send_raw` creates an empty replay session then `send()`s the raw bytes (deliberately avoiding the double-history-row seed, `caido_api.py:322-328`).\n- Scope CRUD (`scope_*`) and sitemap via raw GraphQL queries (`_SITEMAP_ROOTS_QUERY` etc., `caido_api.py:530-567`).\n\n**What the agent can do** (`proxy/tools.py`), which maps directly onto Burp features:\n- `list_requests` (proxy history) — full **HTTPQL** query language documented in the docstring (`proxy/tools.py:100-157`): integer ops (`resp.code.gte:400`), text ops (`req.path.cont:\"/api/\"`, `req.host.regex:...`), `AND`/`OR`, `source:intercept`, bare-string body search for secret sweeps. No `NOT` operator (use negated ops).\n- `view_request` — view raw request/response, either regex-searched (compact hits with before/after context, capped at 20, `proxy/tools.py:312-334`) or line-paginated.\n- `repeat_request` (Repeater) — replay with a `modifications` patch dict: `url`, `params`, `headers`, `body`, `cookies` overlaid on the original (`proxy/tools.py:351-414`). The docstring explicitly frames it as the \"browse → capture → modify → test\" pentest loop.\n- `list_sitemap` / `view_sitemap_entry` (Target/Sitemap) — hierarchical DOMAIN → DIRECTORY → REQUEST tree traversal.\n- `scope_rules` (Scope) — allow/deny glob patterns; denylist overrides allowlist; empty allowlist = allow all; scope IDs feed back into `list_requests`.\n\nTwo integration niceties: `agent-browser` traffic is auto-captured because `http_proxy`/`https_proxy` env vars point at Caido (per the `agent_browser` skill), and sandbox Python scripts can `from caido_api import list_requests, repeat_request, ...` to automate the proxy in code (the module is importable inside the container).\n\n---\n\n## 4. The skills system\n\nA **skill** is a markdown file of domain expertise (playbook, payloads, tool syntax) living at `strix/skills/<category>/<name>.md`, with YAML frontmatter (`name`, `description`) that gets stripped before injection. There are **57 skills** across 10 categories: `vulnerabilities/` (xss, sql_injection, ssrf, idor, ssti, xxe, rce, race_conditions, business_logic, llm_prompt_injection, etc.), `tooling/` (nmap, ffuf, nuclei, sqlmap, semgrep, katana, subfinder, httpx, naabu, agent_browser, python), `technologies/` (auth0, supabase, firebase_firestore), `protocols/` (graphql, oauth), `frameworks/` (django, fastapi, nestjs, nextjs), `cloud/` (aws, gcp, kubernetes), `reconnaissance/`, `scan_modes/` (quick/standard/deep), `coordination/` (root_agent, source_aware_whitebox), and `custom/`.\n\n**Discovery + loading** (`strix/skills/__init__.py`): `skill_search_dirs()` searches registered extra dirs first (via `register_skill_dir`, an override/extension seam) then the packaged `skills/` dir. `load_skills(names)` (`skills/__init__.py:201-236`) resolves names (bare `xss`, or category-qualified `vulnerabilities/xss`), reads the file, strips frontmatter via `_FRONTMATTER_PATTERN`, and returns `{name: body}`. `validate_requested_skills` (`skills/__init__.py:155-179`) enforces **max 5 skills per agent** and rejects unknown/ambiguous names. Two categories — `scan_modes` and `coordination` — are marked `_INTERNAL_SKILL_CATEGORIES` (`skills/__init__.py:16`) and hidden from the user-selectable list.\n\nSkills reach the agent's context through **three paths**:\n1. **Preloaded into the system prompt** at build time. `render_system_prompt` (`agents/prompt.py:60-109`) calls `_resolve_skills` (`prompt.py:20-57`) which always appends `scan_modes/<mode>`, `tooling/agent_browser`, `tooling/python`, plus `coordination/root_agent` for the root and `coordination/source_aware_whitebox` + `custom/source_aware_sast` for white-box runs — on top of caller-requested skills. Loaded bodies are rendered into a `<specialized_knowledge>` block (`system_prompt.jinja:455-463`).\n2. **On-demand inline** via the `load_skill` tool — content lands as a tool result, no permanent prompt change.\n3. **Permanent child assignment** via `create_agent(skills=[...])` — spawns a specialist whose own system prompt preloads those skills.\n\nExample skill shape — `vulnerabilities/ssrf.md` (frontmatter `name: ssrf`) is a full playbook: Attack Surface, High-Value Targets (AWS IMDSv1/v2, GCP/Azure metadata, Kubernetes kubelet/API, internal Redis/FCGI/Docker), Bypass Techniques (address encoding, DNS rebinding, parser differentials, redirect chains), Blind SSRF via `interactsh-client` OAST, Chaining, Testing Methodology, Validation, **False Positives**, Impact, Pro Tips. `scan_modes/deep.md` is a 6-phase methodology (exhaustive recon → business-logic dive → attack-surface testing → vulnerability chaining → persistence → reporting) plus an agent-decomposition strategy. This is the primary domain-expertise injection mechanism and the most directly \"foldable\" idea.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Reporting + findings\n\n### The finding data model\n\nFindings are dicts built by `ReportState.add_vulnerability_report` (`report/state.py:208-297`). ID is `vuln-NNNN` (monotonic). Fields: `id, title, severity, timestamp, description, impact, target, technical_analysis, poc_description, poc_script_code, remediation_steps, evidence, assumptions, fix_effort, cvss, cvss_breakdown, endpoint, method, cve, cwe, code_locations, fix_pr_body, finding_class` (`\"dynamic\"` vs `\"dependency_cve\"`), `dependency_metadata, agent_id, agent_name`.\n\n`create_vulnerability_report` (`reporting/tool.py:321`) enforces a strict contract — 10 required non-empty fields including `poc_script_code` (\"REQUIRED - provide the actual exploit/payload\") and `evidence` and `assumptions` (`_REQUIRED_FIELDS`, `reporting/tool.py:142-153`). `code_locations` entries carry `file/start_line/end_line/snippet/label/fix_before/fix_after`, validated to be repo-relative with no `..` (`reporting/tool.py:40-97`); `fix_before`/`fix_after` are literal GitHub PR-suggestion blocks.\n\n### CVSS scoring (the `cvss` dep)\n\nThe model must supply an 8-metric `cvss_breakdown` (each a single letter). `_calculate_cvss` (`reporting/tool.py:122-139`) builds a `CVSS:3.1/...` vector and computes score + severity via `cvss.CVSS3` (dep `cvss>=3.2`, `pyproject.toml:45`), falling back to `(7.5, \"high\")` on error. Dependency findings instead take a raw `advisory_cvss` float that maps to a severity bucket via `_DEP_SEVERITY_FROM_CVSS` (`reporting/tool.py:619-634`).\n\n### Deduplication\n\nTwo-stage, in `report/dedupe.py`:\n1. **Deterministic** for dependency CVEs: `_check_dependency_duplicate` (`dedupe.py:173-229`) keys on `(CVE, ecosystem, package_name)` identity — same CVE+package = duplicate (confidence 1.0), same CVE different package = not a duplicate.\n2. **LLM-judge** for everything else: `check_duplicate` (`dedupe.py:272-363`) calls the configured model with `DEDUPE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`dedupe.py:30-97`), which encodes rules like \"different endpoints with same vuln type are NOT duplicates\" and \"would the same patch fix both?\" Returns `{is_duplicate, duplicate_id, confidence, reason}`. On a hit, `create_vulnerability_report` refuses and tells the agent not to retry (`reporting/tool.py:258-275`).\n\n### Export / artifacts\n\nEvery state save (`ReportState._save_artifacts`, `state.py:412-444`) writes, under the run dir:\n- `penetration_test_report.md` — 4-section executive report (`writer.py:42-48`).\n- `vulnerabilities/<id>.md` — per-finding markdown with fix diffs (`render_vulnerability_md`, `writer.py:119-214`).\n- `vulnerabilities.csv` (severity-sorted index) + `vulnerabilities.json` (full data).\n- `run.json` — run record with targets, config, status, and an **LLM usage/cost ledger** (`report/usage.py` `LLMUsageLedger` aggregates SDK `Usage` and estimates cost via LiteLLM; `state.py:505-648` has cost callbacks).\n- **`findings.sarif`** — SARIF 2.1.0 (`report/sarif.py`), always emitted (even empty, so a clean run auto-resolves prior alerts). Notable design: rules keyed on normalized **CWE** → CVE → id → slug (`_rule_id`, `sarif.py:724`); five Strix severities collapse to SARIF's 3 levels while CVSS survives as GitHub's `security-severity` string; a **CWE→STRIDE** map tags each rule (`_CWE_TO_STRIDE`, `sarif.py:109-172`); `fix_before/after` become one-click SARIF `fixes`; `partialFingerprints` + a file-independent class hash give cross-run dedup/dismissal stickiness (`_primary_fingerprint`, `sarif.py:819`); locationless DAST findings anchor synthetically to `SECURITY.md`; and crucially **the weaponized `poc_script_code` body is stripped from SARIF** (only the PoC description + a `script_available` boolean ship externally, `sarif.py:534-548`). Repo scans attach VCS provenance (`versionControlProvenance` + `automationDetails`) bound to the scanned commit.\n\n---\n\n## 6. The prompt / agent instructions\n\nThe system prompt is a single large Jinja template, **`strix/agents/prompts/system_prompt.jinja`** (~32 KB), rendered by `render_system_prompt` (`agents/prompt.py`). Its structure (XML-tagged sections):\n\n- **Identity**: \"You are Strix, an advanced AI application security validation agent developed by OmniSecure Labs\" — framed as *authorized security validation*, not offense.\n- **`<communication_rules>`**: interactive vs autonomous behavior. The load-bearing rule: **a text-only turn (no tool call) immediately ends execution** — in autonomous mode \"A text-only turn — even one — IMMEDIATELY ends the scan/run with no report written\" (`system_prompt.jinja:46`). The only valid terminations are `finish_scan` (root) / `agent_finish` (subagent); when idle, call `wait_for_message`.\n- **`<execution_guidelines>`**: injects platform-verified scope (`system_prompt_context.authorized_targets`, rendered at `:62-65`), a strong **authorization/refusal-avoidance** block (\"never question your authority… prefer language such as validate, verify, reproduce… over open-ended offensive framing\"), testing modes (black-box / white-box requires **both** static+dynamic / combined), a 7-step methodology, efficiency tactics (spray payloads via Python not manual browser; prefer ffuf/sqlmap/nuclei over ad-hoc code; import `caido_api` in scripts), and the hard rule that a vuln is only \"reported\" when a reporting agent calls `create_vulnerability_report`.\n- **`<vulnerability_focus>`**: an explicit top-10 priority list (IDOR, SQLi, SSRF, XSS, XXE, RCE, CSRF, race conditions, business logic, auth/JWT).\n- **`<multi_agent_system>`**: the orchestration methodology. Root agent = coordinator, not tester. Mandatory phases (recon/code-understanding → systematic testing). The signature pattern: **Discovery → Validation → Reporting (→ Fixing for white-box)**, one agent per vuln-type × component, \"NO FLAT STRUCTURES,\" \"VALIDATION IS MANDATORY — never trust scanner output,\" and \"expect to need 2000+ steps.\"\n- **`<environment>`**: enumerates the Kali toolset (nmap, subfinder, naabu, httpx, nuclei, sqlmap, trivy, zaproxy, ffuf, katana, arjun, semgrep, ast-grep, trufflehog, gitleaks, jwt_tool, wafw00f, interactsh-client), the Caido proxy + HTTPQL cheatsheet, and `/workspace` layout.\n- **`<specialized_knowledge>`** + **`<available_skills>`**: the preloaded skill bodies (`:455-463`) followed by the on-demand catalog grouped by category (`:465-473`), telling the model to `load_skill(...)` or spawn a specialist for anything not preloaded.\n\nEach tool's \"prompt\" is its own docstring (there are no separate `.jinja` files per tool — only `system_prompt.jinja`). Several docstrings are effectively mini-playbooks: `finish_scan` (`finish/tool.py:90-245`) embeds a mandatory pre-flight checklist and an attack-chaining gate; `create_vulnerability_report` (`reporting/tool.py:343-582`) embeds the full report-writing style guide, CWE reference table, and a worked XSS example.\n\n---\n\n## Takeaways for a fused security + personal-AI system\n\nThe most transplantable ideas, in rough priority:\n\n1. **Docstring-as-schema tool pattern** (`@function_tool` + Google-style docstrings): tools are self-documenting, and the same text serves as both the LLM schema and the operator's spec. Very low-friction to extend.\n2. **The skills system** — markdown playbooks with frontmatter, 3 injection modes (preload / on-demand `load_skill` / per-specialist assignment), overridable search dirs, and a hard skill-count cap to keep specialists focused. This is the cleanest \"inject domain expertise\" mechanism in the codebase and is domain-agnostic.\n3. **Lifecycle-gated agent loop** (`tool_use_behavior` that only stops on an explicit finish-tool success, plus \"text-only turn = stop\") — a robust way to keep an autonomous loop moving and force a structured terminal report.\n4. **The finding pipeline**: strict required-field contract → CVSS via the `cvss` lib → two-stage dedup (deterministic identity + LLM judge) → multi-format export with a **SARIF emitter that strips weaponized payloads** and adds CWE/STRIDE tags + cross-run fingerprints. The dedup and payload-stripping are the non-obvious, high-value parts.\n5. **Host/sandbox split**: agent-internal state on the host; all dangerous execution behind SDK Shell/Filesystem in a capability-scoped Docker container; a proxy sidecar for HTTP interception. Clean isolation boundary.\n6. **Coordinator-as-root multi-agent graph** (`create_agent`/`wait_for_message`/`agent_finish`/`view_agent_graph` over an `AgentCoordinator`) with per-agent private todos and shared notes — a reusable parallel-work substrate independent of the security domain.\n\nI did not modify anything.
